# Dictionary — Terms

*Specialist terms: institutions, offices, events, laws and treaties, parties and organisations, movements, economic terms, social groups, eras and German words. 1583 entries. How to read an entry: see [`README.md`](README.md).*

## #

### 131 Law
<a id="131-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1951
- **Also written as:** Article 131
- **Short:** 1951 law reinstating most civil servants dismissed in denazification.
- **Explanation:** Based on Article 131 of the Basic Law, this 1951 law reinstated most civil servants dismissed during denazification, returning many former Nazis to public service.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 1519 imperial election
<a id="1519-imperial-election"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1519
- **Short:** Election of Charles V as emperor in 1519, financed by the Fugger.
- **Explanation:** Shows the power of south German finance.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 1914-1918-online
<a id="1914-1918-online"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Online international encyclopedia of the First World War, based at the Free University of Berlin.
- **Explanation:** A peer-reviewed reference work; the KB relies on its article on German war losses.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 1938 plot
<a id="1938-plot"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Short:** Officers' plan to overthrow Hitler during the 1938 Sudeten crisis.
- **Explanation:** It was dropped when Britain and France agreed to Hitler's demands at Munich.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 1968 movement
<a id="1968-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** c. 1967–1969
- **Also written as:** 68er · '68 generation · 1968 generation · student movement of 1968 · student movement
- **Short:** West German student revolt of the late 1960s against authority, the Vietnam War and silence about Nazism.
- **Explanation:** Protest movement (its members are called '68er') that challenged universities, parents and the state, and confronted the older generation about the Nazi past. Historians disagree whether it drove liberalisation or is an overrated myth; it also produced dogmatism and a violent fringe.
- **German:** 68er-Bewegung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: historians disagree how far 1968 caused West German liberalisation.

### 1977 marriage-law reform
<a id="marriage-law-reform-1977"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1977
- **Short:** West German reform that ended the legal model of the 'housewife marriage'.
- **Explanation:** Reform of marriage and divorce law in force from 1977. It removed the legal assumption that the wife runs the household, leaving couples to arrange work and housework themselves.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 1994 constitutional reform
<a id="constitutional-reform-1994"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1994
- **Short:** Amendment of the Basic Law after unification.
- **Explanation:** Changes to the Basic Law made after unification; Article 146, allowing a new constitution, remained in force.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 20 July plot
<a id="20-july-plot"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 20 July 1944
- **Also written as:** 20 July 1944 · 20 July
- **Short:** Failed attempt on 20 July 1944 by officers to kill Hitler and seize power.
- **Explanation:** Stauffenberg's bomb failed; about 200 people were executed and families were punished. Many plotters had supported the early regime; motives were mixed. West Germany long honoured it above other resistance.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 2006 World Cup
<a id="2006-world-cup"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2006
- **Short:** Football World Cup hosted by Germany, often cited as normalising display of the national flag.
- **Explanation:** After decades of cautious use of national symbols in West Germany, crowds waving black-red-gold flags became a symbol of relaxed 'football patriotism'.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 2015 refugee crisis
<a id="2015-refugee-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2015–2016
- **Also written as:** refugee summer · refugee crisis
- **Short:** The 2015–16 arrival of about a million refugees in Germany, mainly from Syria, Afghanistan and Iraq.
- **Explanation:** Germany kept its borders open as refugees fled the Syrian war and other conflicts. The arrivals transformed the debate on migration, caused EU asylum conflicts and fuelled the rise of the AfD.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 25-point programme
<a id="25-point-programme"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1920
- **Short:** The Nazi party's 1920 programme, declared 'unalterable' but largely ignored.
- **Explanation:** Its vagueness let the party promise different things to different groups.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### 8-hour day
<a id="8-hour-day"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1918
- **Short:** Limiting the normal working day to eight hours.
- **Explanation:** Won by workers in November 1918 through the Stinnes–Legien agreement, it was later eroded.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

## A

### abbess
<a id="abbess"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** abbesses
- **Short:** Female head of a convent; one of the few medieval roles giving women public authority.
- **Explanation:** The KB names abbesses, and occasionally noblewomen rulers, as exceptions to women's exclusion from medieval politics.
- **German:** Äbtissin
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Abitur
<a id="abitur"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** The school-leaving exam from the Gymnasium that qualifies for university.
- **Explanation:** Established in the era of Humboldt's reforms, it made the classical Gymnasium the bourgeois route into university and state service in the 19th century.
- **German:** Abitur
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### absolutism
<a id="absolutism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 17th–18th c.
- **Also written as:** Absolutism
- **Short:** Rule by a monarch claiming unlimited power; historians now see the label as misleading.
- **Explanation:** In practice most German princes negotiated with estates, nobles, towns and imperial courts. Henshall, Scott and Wilson prefer 'negotiated' or 'consensual' rule.
- **German:** Absolutismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: increasingly regarded as a problematic label.

### accession
<a id="accession"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Also written as:** Accession
- **Short:** Joining the Federal Republic under Article 23, as the GDR did on 3 October 1990.
- **Explanation:** Beitritt: the Volkskammer voted for it on 23 August 1990. It was fast and adopted the Basic Law, but limited East German co-design — a reason some later felt 'annexed'.
- **German:** Beitritt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ADAV
<a id="adav"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1863–1875
- **Also written as:** General German Workers' Association
- **Short:** Lassalle's General German Workers' Association (1863), the first German workers' party.
- **Explanation:** It sought universal suffrage and state-aided producer co-operatives; it merged with the SDAP at Gotha in 1875.
- **German:** Allgemeiner Deutscher Arbeiterverein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Addresses to the German Nation
<a id="addresses-to-the-german-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1807–1808
- **Short:** Fichte's Berlin lectures of 1807–08, a founding text of German nationalism.
- **Explanation:** Delivered in French-occupied Berlin, they called on Germans to renew themselves as a nation through education; the nationalism they expressed was anti-French and cultural.
- **German:** Reden an die deutsche Nation
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Administrative Court of Cologne
<a id="administrative-court-of-cologne"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** VG Köln
- **Short:** Court that in February 2026 provisionally blocked labelling the AfD 'confirmed right-wing extremist'.
- **Explanation:** On 26 February 2026 it found a 'strong suspicion' of extremism but not the certainty needed to label the whole AfD confirmed extremist (case 13 L 1109/25) — a key reference in debates on banning the party.
- **German:** Verwaltungsgericht Köln (VG Köln)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Admonitio generalis
<a id="admonitio-generalis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 789
- **Short:** Charlemagne's 789 decree ('general admonition') for reforming church and society.
- **Explanation:** A key capitulary prescribing an ordered Christian society under one ruler — read today as an aspiration rather than a description of reality.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### AEG
<a id="aeg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Berlin electrical company (General Electricity Company).
- **Explanation:** With Siemens it led German electrical engineering.
- **German:** Allgemeine Elektricitäts-Gesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Against the Murderous, Thieving Hordes of Peasants
<a id="against-the-murderous-hordes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1525
- **Short:** Luther's 1525 pamphlet urging princes to crush the Peasants' War.
- **Explanation:** It showed Luther siding with the princes and his view that the Gospel required worldly obedience.
- **German:** Wider die Mordischen und Reubischen Rotten der Bawren
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Agenda 2010
<a id="agenda-2010"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2003–2005
- **Short:** Chancellor Schröder's 2003 reform programme for the labour market and welfare state.
- **Explanation:** A package of welfare and labour-market cuts launched by the SPD–Green government in 2003, including the Hartz reforms. It is credited with job growth but blamed for insecurity and the decline of the SPD.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### agrarian crisis
<a id="agrarian-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** late 1920s
- **Short:** Crisis of German farming from the late 1920s: falling prices, debt and foreclosures.
- **Explanation:** Farmers' protests preceded the Nazis' rural breakthrough, especially in Protestant areas.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Agrarian League
<a id="agrarian-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1893–1921
- **Short:** Powerful farmers' lobby founded in 1893 for protective tariffs.
- **Explanation:** Dominated by large landowners, it mobilised small farmers and used antisemitic and nationalist rhetoric.
- **German:** Bund der Landwirte
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Aktion Mensch
<a id="aktion-mensch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** German charity supporting people with disabilities and social inclusion.
- **Explanation:** A social lottery and charity whose 2006 poster campaign 'Die Gesellschafter' asked what kind of society Germans want to live in.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Alamanni
<a id="alamanni"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Alamannic
- **Short:** Confederation ('all men') formed in the 3rd century that settled in today's southwest Germany.
- **Explanation:** One of the larger, more durable groupings that appear from the 3rd century, probably formed in response to Roman pressure. They settled the southwest after Rome gave up the upper Limes around 260, and were later conquered by the Franks.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Allgemeine Deutsche Arbeiterverbrüderung
<a id="allgemeine-deutsche-arbeiterverbruderung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1854
- **Short:** 'General German Workers' Brotherhood', the first nationwide workers' association (1848).
- **Explanation:** Founded by Stephan Born, it organised workers and artisans during the revolution.
- **German:** Allgemeine Deutsche Arbeiterverbrüderung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Allgemeines Landrecht
<a id="allgemeines-landrecht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1794
- **Also written as:** ALR · General Law Code
- **Short:** Prussia's General Law Code of 1794, codifying rights and duties of each estate.
- **Explanation:** Begun under Frederick II and completed in 1794, it codified a society of estates rather than equal rights.
- **German:** Allgemeines Landrecht für die Preußischen Staaten
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Alliance 90
<a id="alliance-90"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1990–1993
- **Short:** Electoral alliance of East German civil-rights movements; later merged with the Greens.
- **Explanation:** Bündnis 90 united Neues Forum, Demokratie Jetzt and others, but won only about 3% in March 1990, showing that the activists' goal of a reformed GDR had become a minority view.
- **German:** Bündnis 90
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Alliance 90/The Greens
<a id="alliance-90-the-greens"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1980–
- **Also written as:** Greens
- **Short:** Environmentalist party, in federal government 1998–2005 and 2021–25.
- **Explanation:** Formed from the West German Greens and East German civil-rights activists. Part of the German party system since the 1980s, it won 11.6% in 2025.
- **German:** Bündnis 90/Die Grünen
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Alliance for Germany
<a id="alliance-for-germany"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Short:** Conservative electoral alliance (CDU-East, DSU, DA) that won the March 1990 election.
- **Explanation:** Backed by Helmut Kohl, it promised rapid unification and won about 48% in the first free Volkskammer election on 18 March 1990.
- **German:** Allianz für Deutschland
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Allied Control Council
<a id="allied-control-council"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Four-power body in Berlin that governed Germany as a whole after 1945.
- **Explanation:** Set up in Berlin to rule Germany as a whole, it needed all four Allies to agree. It soon deadlocked, so real power lay with each zone's military commander.
- **German:** Alliierter Kontrollrat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Allied Control Council Law No. 46
<a id="control-council-law-no-46"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1947
- **Short:** 1947 Allied law that formally dissolved the state of Prussia.
- **Explanation:** The occupying powers called Prussia 'a bearer of militarism and reaction' — the official form of the thesis that Prussian militarism was the root of Germany's catastrophes.
- **German:** Kontrollratsgesetz Nr. 46
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Allies
<a id="allies-second-world-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Allied · Western Allies
- **Ambiguous forms:** Allied (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The US, Britain, the Soviet Union and France, who defeated and occupied Germany.
- **Explanation:** The US, Britain, the Soviet Union and France assumed supreme authority over Germany in 1945, divided it into four zones and Berlin into four sectors, and later drifted apart in the Cold War.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** AMBIGUOUS: 'Allies' can refer to other alliances in other chapters.

### Allies
<a id="allies-first-world-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1914–1918
- **Also written as:** Allied · allies
- **Ambiguous forms:** Allied (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The powers fighting Germany in the First World War, such as Britain and France.
- **Explanation:** Allied forces occupied Germany's colonies during the First World War, except East Africa.
- **German:** Alliierte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Alsatians
<a id="alsatians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** People of Alsace, a region on the French border that belonged to Germany 1871–1918.
- **Explanation:** Like Poles and Danes, they were treated as a suspect minority during WWI.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Altdeutsche Tracht
<a id="altdeutsche-tracht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** altdeutsche Tracht
- **Short:** 'Old German' dress, a coded sign of liberal-national sympathy after 1819.
- **Explanation:** Caspar David Friedrich painted figures in such dress; after the Carlsbad Decrees wearing it signalled opposition.
- **German:** altdeutsche Tracht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Alternative for Germany
<a id="alternative-for-germany"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 2013–
- **Also written as:** AfD
- **Short:** Radical-right party founded 2013; second-largest in the Bundestag since 2025, strongest in the East.
- **Explanation:** Founded as a eurosceptic party, it radicalised after 2015. It won 20.8% nationally in 2025 and came first in eastern state elections in 2026. The domestic intelligence service treats it as a suspected extremist case; other parties refuse to cooperate with it (the 'firewall').
- **German:** Alternative für Deutschland (AfD)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: causes of its strength in the East; a 2025 'confirmed extremist' classification was provisionally blocked by a court in 2026.

### American War of Independence
<a id="american-war-of-independence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1775–1783
- **Also written as:** American war
- **Short:** War in which Britain's American colonies won independence.
- **Explanation:** Hesse-Kassel hired out soldiers to Britain for it; the American Revolution (1776) inspired German intellectuals.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Americanisation
<a id="americanisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Adoption of American ways of life, culture and models.
- **Explanation:** Term for US influence on West German society and culture after 1945 — consumption, music, media, politics. In the debate it is compared with the broader idea of 'Westernisation'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Amerika-Häuser
<a id="amerika-hauser"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** US cultural centres in West German cities.
- **Explanation:** 'America Houses' were US-run cultural centres in West German cities, with libraries, lectures and films promoting democracy as part of re-education.
- **German:** Amerika-Häuser
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Amtmann
<a id="amtmann"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Amtmänner
- **Short:** A prince's local district official, collecting revenue and enforcing orders.
- **Explanation:** The Amtmänner linked the princely court to the villages, handling administration, justice and taxes in their district.
- **German:** Amtmann (pl. Amtmänner)
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Anabaptists
<a id="anabaptists"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Radical Protestants who rejected infant baptism and were persecuted by all sides.
- **Explanation:** Catholics and Protestants alike executed or expelled them. Their takeover of Münster in 1534–35 made them a byword for religious radicalism.
- **German:** Täufer
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Annexation of Austria
<a id="annexation-of-austria"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Also written as:** annexation of Austria
- **Short:** Germany's takeover of Austria in March 1938.
- **Explanation:** Known in German as the 'Anschluss', it was a step towards war.
- **German:** Anschluss
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### anointing
<a id="anointing"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** anointed
- **Short:** Ritual pouring of holy oil on a king, borrowed from the Old Testament.
- **Explanation:** It marked the Christian king as God's agent, giving Carolingian rulers sacred legitimacy.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Anschluss
<a id="anschluss"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Short:** Nazi Germany's annexation of Austria in March 1938.
- **Explanation:** Confirmed by a pressured plebiscite, it marked the regime's radicalisation after 1938.
- **German:** Anschluss
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### anti-communism
<a id="anti-communism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Anti-communism
- **Short:** Opposition to communism; a founding state doctrine of West Germany.
- **Explanation:** In the Cold War, rejection of the Soviet Union and the GDR became a core part of West German political identity, justifying Western integration and the 1956 ban of the Communist party.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### anti-imperialism
<a id="anti-imperialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Opposition to Western 'imperialism'; a core GDR ideological theme.
- **Explanation:** Soviet-bloc ideology presenting the West, especially the USA and West Germany, as imperialist aggressors.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### anti-Judaism
<a id="anti-judaism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Christian religious hostility to Jews.
- **Explanation:** Accusations of host desecration, ritual murder and well-poisoning justified medieval massacres.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Anti-Socialist Law
<a id="anti-socialist-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1878–1890
- **Also written as:** Anti-Socialist Laws
- **Short:** Law banning social democratic associations, meetings and publications (1878–90).
- **Explanation:** Passed after two assassination attempts on Wilhelm I (neither by socialists). It did not stop SPD candidates standing; the SPD vote grew and the law lapsed in 1890.
- **German:** Sozialistengesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### anticlericalism
<a id="anticlericalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Anticlericalism
- **Short:** Resentment of the clergy's privileges, wealth and power.
- **Explanation:** A key pressure behind the Reformation: townspeople in particular wanted freedom from clerical privilege.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### antifascism
<a id="antifascism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** anti-fascism · Anti-Fascist
- **Short:** Opposition to fascism; the GDR's founding myth.
- **Explanation:** The GDR presented itself as the 'better Germany' that had broken with Nazism, and the FRG as fascism's successor. It celebrated communist resistance but marginalised the Holocaust — a partial truth used to legitimise dictatorship.
- **German:** Antifaschismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Antifascist-democratic order
<a id="antifascist-democratic-order"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** antifascist-democratic
- **Short:** Soviet-zone slogan for the new political order.
- **Explanation:** The Soviet zone's official label for its new political order after 1945, used to justify expropriations and the growing dominance of the communist-led SED.
- **German:** antifaschistisch-demokratische Ordnung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Antisemitism
<a id="antisemitism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** antisemitism · anti-Jewish · anti-Judaism · antisemitic · antisemites · racial antisemitism · Racial antisemitism
- **Ambiguous forms:** anti-Judaism (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Hostility to Jews; organised as a political movement in Germany from the late 1870s.
- **Explanation:** Anti-Jewish strands existed in early nationalism (Jahn). Wilhelm Marr coined the word in 1879; in the Empire antisemitic parties won seats and antisemitism became a 'cultural code' for anti-modern resentment. It later became central to Nazism.
- **German:** Antisemitismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: how central antisemitism was to Nazi voters' motives is debated.

### "Apolitical German" thesis
<a id="apolitical-german-thesis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Older view that German intellectuals withdrew into culture, leaving politics to princes.
- **Explanation:** Later used in Sonderweg arguments; critics point to lively political journalism, reading societies and reforming bureaucracies.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### APuZ
<a id="apuz"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** 'Aus Politik und Zeitgeschichte': German journal on politics and contemporary history.
- **Explanation:** Journal published by the Federal Agency for Civic Education (bpb); Bernd Rother's 2025 article on Ostpolitik appeared in it.
- **German:** Aus Politik und Zeitgeschichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)

### archbishop
<a id="archbishop"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** archbishops · Archbishops · archbishopric
- **Short:** Senior bishop over a church province of several bishoprics.
- **Explanation:** The archbishops of Mainz, Cologne and Trier were among the seven prince-electors and ruled territories as princes.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### ARD
<a id="ard"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Network of Germany's regional public broadcasters, the older of the two national ones.
- **Explanation:** The joint network of regional public broadcasters, modelled on post-war Allied ideas of broadcasting independent of the state. With ZDF it forms public broadcasting, funded by fees and contested by populists.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Armaments miracle
<a id="armaments-miracle"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** armaments miracle
- **Short:** Tripling of German arms output 1942–44, credited to Speer.
- **Explanation:** It was largely due to earlier investment, rationalisation and forced labour.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Armchair socialists
<a id="armchair-socialists"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** armchair socialists
- **Short:** Mocking nickname for reform-minded professors like Schmoller.
- **Explanation:** They sought state welfare reform rather than revolution.
- **German:** Kathedersozialisten
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### armistice
<a id="armistice"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 11 November 1918
- **Also written as:** Armistice
- **Short:** The ceasefire of 11 November 1918 that ended the fighting of WWI.
- **Explanation:** The army command itself demanded it, but it was signed by a civilian, Matthias Erzberger — allowing the generals to shift blame for defeat onto civilians.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Armleder pogrom
<a id="armleder-pogrom"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1336–38
- **Also written as:** Armleder
- **Short:** Wave of massacres of Jewish communities in 1336–38, led by bands named after leather arm-guards.
- **Explanation:** One of the series of medieval massacres of Jews in the German lands — between the Rintfleisch pogrom (1298) and the far larger Black Death persecutions (1348–50) — showing how fragile the Jews' 'protection' by emperors and bishops was.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Army's oath
<a id="army-s-oath"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** army's oath
- **Short:** The personal oath of loyalty soldiers swore to Hitler from 1934.
- **Explanation:** Many officers later cited it as a reason not to resist.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 116
<a id="article-116"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1949
- **Also written as:** Art. 116
- **Short:** Basic Law article defining 'Germans' to include ethnic German refugees and expellees.
- **Explanation:** It gave ethnic Germans from Eastern Europe a right of return, which later allowed millions of late resettlers to come after 1990.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 119
<a id="article-119"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1919
- **Short:** Versailles Treaty clause by which Germany gave up its colonies.
- **Explanation:** Under it Germany renounced all its overseas possessions in favour of the Allied powers.
- **German:** Artikel 119
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 146
<a id="article-146"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Art. 146
- **Short:** Basic Law article foreseeing a new constitution adopted freely by the whole German people.
- **Explanation:** The Basic Law's final article, which made it 'provisional' until Germans could adopt a constitution freely. In 1990 many East German civil-rights activists favoured this slower, participatory route; unification took the Article 23 route instead, and Article 146 remains in force.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 23
<a id="article-23"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1949–1990
- **Also written as:** Art. 23 · Art. 23 GG · Europe article · EU article
- **Short:** Basic Law article allowing other parts of Germany to join; used by the GDR in 1990.
- **Explanation:** Clause of the original Basic Law letting further German territories accede (join). In 1990 the freely elected East German parliament chose this fast route: the GDR joined the Federal Republic and adopted the Basic Law, rather than both drafting a new constitution under Article 146.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 25
<a id="article-25"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1919–1933
- **Also written as:** Art. 25
- **Short:** Weimar constitutional clause letting the president dissolve the Reichstag.
- **Explanation:** From 1930 it was used to dissolve parliament whenever it moved to cancel emergency decrees, leading to repeated elections.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Article 48
<a id="article-48"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1919–1933
- **Also written as:** Art. 48
- **Short:** Weimar constitutional clause letting the president issue emergency decrees.
- **Explanation:** Meant as an emergency tool, it was used by Ebert mostly to defend the republic. From 1930 it became 'a substitute for regular legislation': chancellors governed by presidential decree instead of parliamentary majority, which hollowed out democracy before Hitler.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Aryan
<a id="aryan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Aryans
- **Short:** Nazi racial label for people counted as being of 'German or related blood'.
- **Explanation:** The term had no scientific basis; it defined who belonged to the 'people's community' and who, above all Jews, was excluded.
- **German:** arisch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Aryan certificate
<a id="aryan-certificate"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Document proving 'Aryan' ancestry, required under Nazi rule.
- **Explanation:** It subjected everyone to racial classification.
- **German:** Ariernachweis
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Aryanisation
<a id="aryanisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Forced transfer of Jewish-owned property and businesses to non-Jews.
- **Explanation:** Many firms profited; it was part of the expropriation of German Jews.
- **German:** Arisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Asocials
<a id="asocials"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** asocials
- **Short:** Nazi label for people branded as not fitting in, such as the homeless or 'work-shy'.
- **Explanation:** People labelled 'asocial' were sent to camps; after 1945 they were long denied recognition as victims.
- **German:** Asoziale
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### asylum compromise
<a id="asylum-compromise"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1993
- **Short:** 1993 constitutional change that sharply restricted the right to asylum (Art. 16a).
- **Explanation:** Agreed amid the early-1990s surge in asylum applications and racist violence. It replaced the unrestricted right to asylum with Article 16a, which excludes most people arriving via safe third countries.
- **German:** Asylkompromiss
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Atonement levy
<a id="atonement-levy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Also written as:** "atonement" levy · atonement
- **Ambiguous forms:** atonement (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 1 billion Reichsmark fine imposed on German Jews after the November 1938 pogrom.
- **Explanation:** After the November 1938 pogrom the regime made German Jews pay 1 billion Reichsmarks, so the victims paid for the violence against them. It was part of their expropriation.
- **German:** Judenvermögensabgabe
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Aufwertung
<a id="aufwertung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Upgrading': Ziemann's term for the Reichstag's growing weight after 1890.
- **Explanation:** As the state took on more tasks needing laws and budgets, governments had to negotiate with parties.
- **German:** Aufwertung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Augsburg Confession
<a id="augsburg-confession"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1530
- **Short:** The founding statement of Lutheran belief, presented in 1530.
- **Explanation:** Presented to the Diet at Augsburg in 1530, it became the Lutheran creed; later peace settlements protected churches that adhered to it.
- **German:** Confessio Augustana
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### August experience
<a id="august-experience"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** August 1914
- **Also written as:** Augusterlebnis · spirit of 1914
- **Short:** The supposed wave of national war enthusiasm in August 1914.
- **Explanation:** Enthusiasm was real in some urban middle-class crowds but not general; anxiety was widespread among workers and rural people (Verhey). The 'spirit of 1914' was later turned into a political myth, used by the Nazis.
- **German:** Augusterlebnis
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Auslandsdeutsche
<a id="auslandsdeutsche"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Germans abroad': German speakers living outside Germany, especially after the border changes of 1919.
- **Explanation:** After the Versailles settlement moved borders, nationalists made the fate of Germans in neighbouring states a grievance, feeding revisionist nationalism in the Weimar years.
- **German:** Auslandsdeutsche
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### austerity
<a id="austerity"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Government policy of sharp spending cuts and tax rises to balance the budget.
- **Explanation:** Brüning's austerity cut benefits, wages and public spending by decree. A 2017/18 study found that districts hit harder by austerity saw larger Nazi vote gains — though only as one factor among others.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated: German responsibility for eurozone austerity.

### Austro-Prussian dualism
<a id="austro-prussian-dualism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1740–1866
- **Short:** Rivalry of Austria and Prussia for leadership in Germany, from 1740 until 1866.
- **Explanation:** It began with Prussia's seizure of Silesia and dominated German politics, undermining the Holy Roman Empire.
- **German:** Dualismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Austro-Prussian rivalry
<a id="austro-prussian-rivalry"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1740–1866
- **Short:** Competition between Austria and Prussia for leadership in Germany.
- **Explanation:** It shaped German politics from the 18th century. It paralysed the Confederation when the two disagreed and was settled by Prussia's victory in 1866.
- **German:** Deutscher Dualismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### autarky
<a id="autarky"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Autarky
- **Short:** Economic self-sufficiency.
- **Explanation:** A goal of the Four-Year Plan to prepare for war.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### authoritarian personality
<a id="authoritarian-personality"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1950
- **Short:** 1950 theory by Adorno and colleagues that some personality types are drawn to authoritarian rule.
- **Explanation:** The study 'The Authoritarian Personality' offered a psychological explanation for support for fascism. The KB notes that such cultural explanations of German history, like the 'Untertan mentality', are now treated cautiously.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: cultural and psychological explanations of German authoritarianism are now treated cautiously.

### auxiliaries
<a id="auxiliaries"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Non-citizen soldiers serving in the Roman army alongside the legions.
- **Explanation:** Many young men from beyond the frontier served as Roman auxiliaries and came home with money and experience — one way Roman contact reshaped Germanic-speaking societies.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### auxiliary police
<a id="auxiliary-police"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1933
- **Short:** SA, SS and Stahlhelm men deputised as police in 1933.
- **Explanation:** Göring's move in Prussia let Nazi paramilitaries arrest and terrorise opponents with state authority.
- **German:** Hilfspolizei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Auxiliary Service Law
<a id="auxiliary-service-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** December 1916
- **Short:** WWI law obliging men to work in the war economy.
- **Explanation:** It conscripted labour but also integrated unions through workers' committees, an example of how the war strengthened unions even as the army took control.
- **German:** Hilfsdienstgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

## B

### Badisches Landrecht
<a id="badisches-landrecht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1810
- **Short:** Baden's 1810 law code, a version of the French Code Napoléon.
- **Explanation:** One way the enlarged middle states introduced French-style legal equality to integrate their new subjects.
- **German:** Badisches Landrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### balance of power
<a id="balance-of-power"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** System in which no single state dominates and others unite against any that tries.
- **Explanation:** Classic European order. The KB notes Germany was too big and central for such a balance, which is why post-war integration was the answer.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### bank crisis
<a id="bank-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** July 1931
- **Short:** The collapse of major German banks in July 1931.
- **Explanation:** It deepened the Depression and limited the government's room for manoeuvre.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### barbarians
<a id="barbarians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** barbarian · barbarism
- **Short:** Roman term for peoples outside the empire, seen as uncivilised.
- **Explanation:** Roman ideology set civilisation against barbarism. In reality, 'barbarian' recruits and allies became essential to the late Roman army.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### baroque
<a id="baroque"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 17th–18th c.
- **Also written as:** Baroque
- **Short:** Ornate, theatrical style of art, architecture and piety of the 17th–18th centuries.
- **Explanation:** It marked princely residence cities like Dresden and Austrian Catholic religious life.
- **German:** Barock
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `00_README.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### BASF
<a id="basf"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1865–
- **Short:** Chemical company founded 1865 in Ludwigshafen, a pioneer of synthetic dyes.
- **Explanation:** One of the firms that made Germany a world leader in chemicals.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Basic Law
<a id="basic-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Grundgesetz · GG
- **Short:** West Germany's 1949 constitution, since 1990 the constitution of united Germany.
- **Explanation:** Constitution drafted by the Parliamentary Council and adopted on 23 May 1949. Called 'provisional' until Germans could decide on unity, it deliberately answered Weimar's weaknesses: human dignity first, enforceable rights, a strong chancellor, a weak president, a powerful Constitutional Court and 'militant democracy'. In 1990 East Germany joined under it.
- **German:** Grundgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Basic Rights
<a id="basic-rights-1848"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1848–1851
- **Also written as:** basic rights
- **Ambiguous forms:** basic rights (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Catalogue of civil rights proclaimed by the Frankfurt Assembly in December 1848.
- **Explanation:** Equality before the law, freedom of press, religion and assembly, and an end to feudal bondage. Valid only where states accepted them, they were annulled in 1851.
- **German:** Grundrechte
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### basic rights
<a id="basic-rights-basic-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Basic rights
- **Short:** The fundamental rights in Articles 1–19 of the Basic Law, directly enforceable in court.
- **Explanation:** They open the constitution, starting with human dignity, and bind all state power — a deliberate answer to Nazi dictatorship.
- **German:** Grundrechte
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Basic Treaty
<a id="basic-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1972
- **Short:** 1972 treaty in which the two German states recognised each other in practice.
- **Explanation:** Agreement between the FRG and the GDR establishing 'permanent missions' instead of embassies, since the FRG did not regard the GDR as a foreign country. Both states joined the UN in 1973. The CDU/CSU abstained in the ratification vote.
- **German:** Grundlagenvertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### basic-mandate clause
<a id="basic-mandate-clause"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** Rule letting a party with three constituency seats into the Bundestag despite under 5%.
- **Explanation:** The 2023 electoral reform abolished it, but in July 2024 the Constitutional Court restored it while upholding the cap of 630 seats.
- **German:** Grundmandatsklausel
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Basket III
<a id="basket-iii"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1975
- **Short:** Human-rights and human-contacts section of the 1975 Helsinki Final Act.
- **Explanation:** Part of the Helsinki agreement covering human rights, family contacts and information, which East Germans used to support exit applications.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bauern
<a id="bauern"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Peasants.
- **Explanation:** The rural majority — perhaps 85–90% of the population — living in villages under a noble, church or town lord.
- **German:** Bauern
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bauhaus
<a id="bauhaus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1919–1933
- **Short:** Influential school of art, design and architecture of the Weimar Republic.
- **Explanation:** Founded in Weimar in 1919 by Walter Gropius, later in Dessau and Berlin, it united art, crafts and industrial design in a modern style. It closed in 1933 under Nazi pressure; its teachers carried its ideas abroad.
- **German:** Bauhaus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Baum group
<a id="baum-group"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Jewish-communist youth resistance group in Berlin, crushed in 1942.
- **Explanation:** Led by Herbert Baum, it attacked an anti-Soviet propaganda exhibition in 1942; most members were executed.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bavarians
<a id="bavarians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Bavarian
- **Ambiguous forms:** Bavarian (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Early medieval people of southeastern Germany, later incorporated into the Frankish realm.
- **Explanation:** One of the later groupings of late antiquity. They kept their own law code and regional identity, which survived in the duchy and today's state of Bavaria.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bayer
<a id="bayer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1863–
- **Short:** Chemical and pharmaceutical company founded 1863.
- **Explanation:** A symbol of Germany's science-based chemical industry.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### BBC
<a id="bbc"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Britain's public broadcaster, whose German service reported on the war and Nazi crimes.
- **Explanation:** Listening to foreign radio was banned and could be denounced, yet many Germans tuned in; it was one channel for news of the mass murder.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Beamte
<a id="beamte"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Career state officials.
- **Explanation:** A growing group in the 18th century, closely tied to the educated middle class.
- **German:** Beamte
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Beguines
<a id="beguines"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Religious women living in communities without taking permanent vows.
- **Explanation:** Along with convents, they offered medieval women an alternative to marriage.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berlin Airlift
<a id="berlin-airlift"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1948–1949
- **Also written as:** Airlift
- **Short:** Western supply flights that kept West Berlin alive during the 1948–49 blockade.
- **Explanation:** US and British aircraft flew food, coal and goods into West Berlin for eleven months, defeating the Soviet blockade and turning the former enemy city into a symbol of Western solidarity.
- **German:** Luftbrücke
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berlin Blockade
<a id="berlin-blockade"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1948–1949
- **Also written as:** blockade · Soviet blockade
- **Ambiguous forms:** blockade (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Soviet blockade of West Berlin, June 1948 – May 1949.
- **Explanation:** After the Western currency reform, the Soviet Union cut all land routes to West Berlin (June 1948 – May 1949). The Western Allies answered with the Airlift, an early Cold War crisis.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berlin Conference
<a id="berlin-conference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1884–1885
- **Short:** Meeting of European powers in Berlin that set rules for colonising Africa.
- **Explanation:** Hosted by Bismarck, it provided diplomatic cover for colonial claims.
- **German:** Berliner Konferenz
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berlin Declaration
<a id="berlin-declaration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 5 June 1945
- **Short:** Allied statement of 5 June 1945 taking supreme authority over Germany.
- **Explanation:** Issued on 5 June 1945 after Germany's unconditional surrender, it announced that the four Allies were taking over supreme government authority in Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berlin Republic
<a id="berlin-republic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1999–
- **Short:** Nickname for united Germany governed from Berlin since 1999.
- **Explanation:** Term for the Federal Republic after the move of government to Berlin. Historians and commentators debate whether it marks a new era or continues the old West German 'Bonn Republic'.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated: continuity with the Bonn Republic or a new era.

### Berlinische Monatsschrift
<a id="berlinische-monatsschrift"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** from 1783
- **Short:** Berlin Enlightenment journal in which Kant's essay 'What is Enlightenment?' appeared.
- **Explanation:** Journals like this were central to the new public sphere of debate among the educated middle class.
- **German:** Berlinische Monatsschrift
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Berufsbeamtentum
<a id="berufsbeamtentum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** The German tradition of a professional, tenured civil service with special duties and protections.
- **Explanation:** German word for the career civil service: officials appointed for life, loyal to the state and barred from striking. The Basic Law (Art. 33(5)) preserved this tradition, one of the continuities from earlier German states.
- **German:** Berufsbeamtentum
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Besthaupt
<a id="besthaupt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Inheritance fee: the best head of cattle, owed to the lord when a peasant died.
- **Explanation:** One of the dues of manorial lordship.
- **German:** Besthaupt
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### better Germany
<a id="better-germany"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The GDR's claim to be the morally superior German state that had learned from Nazism.
- **Explanation:** Core of the GDR's antifascist self-image, contrasting itself with a West Germany portrayed as the heir of fascism.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bicameral
<a id="bicameral"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** bicameral
- **Short:** Having two chambers of parliament.
- **Explanation:** The 1849 constitution and Prussia's 1850 constitution both provided two-chamber parliaments.
- **German:** Zweikammersystem
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Biedermeier
<a id="biedermeier"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** c. 1815–1848
- **Short:** Middle-class style of the restoration years, centred on domestic and private life.
- **Explanation:** The name for the comfortable, family-centred culture of 1815–48, when public politics was censored. The KB warns the era was not politically dead: associations, press and constitutional politics in the south were very active.
- **German:** Biedermeier
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bildung
<a id="bildung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Education understood as lifelong self-formation of the whole person.
- **Explanation:** Central to Weimar classicism and to Humboldt's neohumanism; it gave the educated middle class its identity.
- **German:** Bildung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bildungsbürgertum
<a id="bildungsburgertum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** The educated middle class — officials, professors, pastors, lawyers, doctors — defined by education, not birth.
- **Explanation:** It arose in the 18th century, closely tied to state service, and carried the German Enlightenment.
- **German:** Bildungsbürgertum
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### bishopric
<a id="bishopric"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** bishoprics
- **Short:** Church district governed by a bishop.
- **Explanation:** Roman-era bishoprics like Cologne and Trier survived Rome's fall. Many German bishops became prince-bishops ruling territories.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Black Death
<a id="black-death"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1348–50
- **Short:** Plague epidemic of 1348–50 that killed perhaps a third of the population.
- **Explanation:** It reshaped labour and land and fuelled religious anxiety. Jews were falsely blamed; hundreds of communities were destroyed, often with council complicity and debt cancellation as a motive.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Mortality estimates vary widely (MEDIUM confidence).

### Black market
<a id="black-market"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** black market · black markets
- **Short:** Illegal trade outside rationing and official prices.
- **Explanation:** In the war and the post-war hunger years it flourished, with cigarettes serving as currency.
- **German:** Schwarzmarkt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`

### Black-red-gold
<a id="black-red-gold"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** from 1815
- **Also written as:** black-red-gold
- **Short:** The German national colours, taken from 1815 student and volunteer-corps symbolism.
- **Explanation:** Not an old imperial flag but an 'invented tradition' from the Lützow Free Corps and the Jena fraternity. It became the flag of the national movement (Hambach 1832), and later of Weimar and today's Germany.
- **German:** Schwarz-Rot-Gold
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### black-white-red
<a id="black-white-red"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1867/1871–1918
- **Short:** Colours of the Imperial German flag, later the banner of anti-republican nationalists.
- **Explanation:** In the Weimar Republic, nationalists and monarchists rejected the republic's black-red-gold and flew the old imperial colours. The KB presents the flag conflict as a struggle over the republic itself.
- **German:** Schwarz-Weiß-Rot
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### blank cheque
<a id="blank-cheque"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 5 July 1914
- **Short:** Germany's unconditional promise of support to Austria-Hungary in July 1914.
- **Explanation:** By backing Austria-Hungary's move against Serbia without conditions, despite a high estimated risk of general war, German leaders accepted the danger of a European war. It is central evidence in the debate over German responsibility.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED (degree): how far it shows deliberate German risk-taking is debated.

### Blitzkrieg
<a id="blitzkrieg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** 'Lightning war': fast campaigns using tanks and aircraft.
- **Explanation:** An older view held Germany planned short wars with limited mobilisation; Tooze and Overy show heavy mobilisation from the start.
- **German:** Blitzkrieg
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### bloc parties
<a id="bloc-parties"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1945–1990
- **Short:** Non-communist parties in the GDR allowed to exist under SED control.
- **Explanation:** Parties such as the East German CDU, the liberal LDPD, the national NDPD and the farmers' DBD. They had fixed seat quotas in the Volkskammer but could not challenge the SED; in 1989–90 they broke away.
- **German:** Blockparteien
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Block warden
<a id="block-warden"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** block wardens · Blockleiter · Block wardens
- **Short:** Nazi party representative for roughly 40–60 households who collected donations and watched residents.
- **Explanation:** Block wardens brought the party to every doorstep: they distributed information, collected money and reported on neighbours' behaviour.
- **German:** Blockleiter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
