### Blomberg–Fritsch crisis
<a id="blomberg-fritsch-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Also written as:** 1938 purge
- **Short:** 1938 removal of the war minister and army chief that tightened Hitler's control of the army.
- **Explanation:** Hitler used scandals to oust War Minister Blomberg and army commander Fritsch and took direct command of the armed forces. The KB counts it, with the 1934 oath, as the army's subordination to Hitler.
- **German:** Blomberg-Fritsch-Krise
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Blood and soil
<a id="blood-and-soil"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** blood and soil
- **Short:** Nazi ideology glorifying farmers and the link between race and land.
- **Explanation:** It praised the peasantry as the racial core of the nation and underpinned ideas of settling 'living space' in the East.
- **German:** Blut und Boden
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### blooming landscapes
<a id="blooming-landscapes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Short:** Kohl's 1990 promise that eastern Germany would soon flourish economically.
- **Explanation:** 'Blühende Landschaften': a symbol of the optimistic West German mainstream view of unity, later contrasted with mass unemployment in the East.
- **German:** blühende Landschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bologna reform
<a id="bologna-reform"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1999–
- **Short:** European university reform introducing bachelor's and master's degrees.
- **Explanation:** Launched in 1999, it replaced traditional German degrees such as the Diplom and Magister with a common European degree structure.
- **German:** Bologna-Prozess
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bolsheviks
<a id="bolsheviks"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1903–1952
- **Also written as:** Bolshevik
- **Ambiguous forms:** Bolshevik (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The Russian communist party of Lenin that took power in 1917.
- **Explanation:** Radical wing of Russian socialism that seized power in the October Revolution of 1917 and founded the Soviet state. 'Bolshevik crimes' refers to the mass violence of Soviet rule.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bolshevism
<a id="bolshevism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Bolshevik · anti-Bolshevism
- **Ambiguous forms:** Bolshevik (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Russian communism of Lenin's party; used in Germany as a scare word for revolution.
- **Explanation:** The myth that the 1918 revolution was a Bolshevik plot was false. The Nazis presented Germans with a choice between 'Bolshevism' and 'national renewal', and anti-Bolshevism was part of their ideology.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bonn Republic
<a id="bonn-republic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1949–1990
- **Short:** The West German Federal Republic 1949–90, named after its capital Bonn.
- **Explanation:** Nickname for West Germany's stable post-war democracy. Its institutions were extended to the east in 1990; whether today's Germany is a new 'Berlin Republic' or simply the Bonn Republic enlarged is debated.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated whether the post-1990 state is a continuation or a new era ('Berlin Republic').

### Bonn–Berlin move
<a id="bonn-berlin-move"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1991–1999
- **Also written as:** move from Bonn to Berlin · Capital move
- **Short:** Transfer of parliament and government from Bonn to Berlin after unification.
- **Explanation:** The Bundestag voted in 1991 to make Berlin the seat of government; the move was completed in 1999, when the Bundestag moved into the rebuilt Reichstag building with its glass dome. Some ministries stayed in Bonn.
- **German:** Umzug von Bonn nach Berlin
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bonn–Copenhagen Declarations
<a id="bonn-copenhagen-declarations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1955
- **Short:** 1955 West German and Danish declarations guaranteeing the rights of each other's minorities.
- **Explanation:** They protect the Danish minority in Schleswig and the German minority in Denmark; one result is that the Danish minority's party, the SSW, is exempt from the 5% threshold.
- **German:** Bonn-Kopenhagener Erklärungen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Borchardt controversy
<a id="borchardt-controversy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1979–
- **Short:** Debate over whether Brüning had any alternative to austerity in the Depression.
- **Explanation:** Knut Borchardt said no; Holtfrerich and others disputed his data; Ritschl blamed the foreign debt crisis. The question remains open.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED.

### border troops
<a id="border-troops"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1961–1990
- **Short:** GDR armed units guarding the Wall and inner-German border, with orders to stop escapes by force.
- **Explanation:** Soldiers instructed to prevent 'border breaches' by all means including firearms; successful shooters were rewarded. After 1990 courts convicted guards and political leaders for killings at the border.
- **German:** Grenztruppen der DDR
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Borussian school
<a id="borussian-school"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Borussian
- **Short:** 'Prussian school' of historians who claimed Prussia had a mission to unify Germany.
- **Explanation:** Droysen, Sybel and Treitschke wrote history as retrospective legitimation of Prussian-led unification.
- **German:** borussische Geschichtsschreibung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### bourgeois
<a id="bourgeois"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Belonging to the town middle class.
- **Explanation:** 19th-century liberals saw medieval towns as seedbeds of German freedom.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bourgeois revolution
<a id="bourgeois-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** bourgeois revolution
- **Short:** Marxist term for a revolution in which the middle class overthrows feudal rule.
- **Explanation:** Marxist and GDR historians called 1848 a bourgeois revolution betrayed by the bourgeoisie; Western historians see several parallel revolutions.
- **German:** bürgerliche Revolution
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Bourgeoisie
<a id="bourgeoisie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** bourgeois · bourgeoisie
- **Ambiguous forms:** bourgeois (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The middle class of property and education.
- **Explanation:** In 19th-century Germany it combined the educated and the business middle class and formed the base of liberalism.
- **German:** Bürgertum
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Boycott of 1 April 1933
<a id="boycott-of-1-april-1933"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1 April 1933
- **Also written as:** 1933 boycott · April boycott · Boycott (1 April 1933)
- **Short:** Nazi-organised boycott of Jewish shops, doctors and lawyers.
- **Explanation:** The first nationwide action against German Jews, it began the stage of legal and economic exclusion.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Boycott of Jewish shops
<a id="boycott-of-jewish-shops"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1 April 1933
- **Short:** Nazi-organised boycott of Jewish businesses on 1 April 1933.
- **Explanation:** One of the first nationwide anti-Jewish actions of the regime.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### bpb
<a id="bpb"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1952–
- **Short:** Federal Agency for Civic Education, a German government body publishing political and historical material.
- **Explanation:** The Bundeszentrale für politische Bildung, which publishes accessible history and politics texts, including the journal APuZ and the 'Informationen zur politischen Bildung'. Many KB sources come from it.
- **German:** Bundeszentrale für politische Bildung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

### Brandmauer
<a id="brandmauer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** firewall
- **Short:** 'Firewall': other parties' refusal to cooperate with the AfD.
- **Explanation:** The mainstream parties' pledge not to govern or vote with the AfD. As the AfD becomes the largest party in eastern states, it is debated whether this firewall can hold.
- **German:** Brandmauer
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated whether it is sustainable where the AfD wins pluralities.

### Bretton Woods
<a id="bretton-woods"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1944–1973
- **Short:** Post-war system of fixed exchange rates tied to the US dollar.
- **Explanation:** International monetary system agreed in 1944 in which currencies had fixed rates against the dollar. The Deutsche Mark was undervalued under it, making West German exports cheap and helping the boom.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Brexit
<a id="brexit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2016–2020
- **Short:** The United Kingdom's departure from the EU after its 2016 referendum.
- **Explanation:** The UK left the EU in 2020, removing a large member and increasing Germany's weight within the Union.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### British blockade
<a id="british-blockade"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1914–1919
- **Also written as:** blockade · Allied blockade
- **Ambiguous forms:** blockade (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Britain's naval blockade cutting Germany off from food and raw material imports in WWI.
- **Explanation:** Together with poor harvests it caused hunger at home, including the 'turnip winter' of 1916/17. How many civilians died because of it is disputed: Germany's post-war government claimed about 763,000; some demographers argue for 400,000–500,000.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: the number of blockade-related civilian deaths is uncertain.

### Brockhaus
<a id="brockhaus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Leading German encyclopedia; its 1846 edition defined pauperism.
- **Explanation:** The Brockhaus Konversations-Lexikon was the standard German reference work. The KB quotes its 1846 definition of mass poverty.
- **German:** Brockhaus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bund Deutscher Frauenvereine
<a id="bund-deutscher-frauenvereine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1894–1933
- **Short:** Federation of German Women's Associations, umbrella of the bourgeois women's movement.
- **Explanation:** It campaigned for women's education and rights.
- **German:** Bund Deutscher Frauenvereine
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bundesarchiv
<a id="bundesarchiv"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Germany's Federal Archives, which now hold the Stasi files.
- **Explanation:** National archive of Germany. Its Stasi records section supplies the figures on full-time staff and unofficial informants cited in the KB.
- **German:** Bundesarchiv
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Bundesbank
<a id="bundesbank"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1957–
- **Short:** West Germany's independent central bank, focused on keeping prices stable.
- **Explanation:** The German central bank set up in 1957, independent of the government and committed to price stability. It was part of the 'social market economy'; after 1999 its monetary powers passed to the European Central Bank.
- **German:** Deutsche Bundesbank
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bundesexekution
<a id="bundesexekution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Federal execution': armed enforcement by member states' troops against a Confederation member.
- **Explanation:** The German Confederation had no army of its own. To enforce its decisions on a member state, it could order other members to send troops.
- **German:** Bundesexekution
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Bundesrat
<a id="bundesrat-federal-republic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Federal Council
- **Short:** Federal chamber in which the 16 state governments take part in national law-making.
- **Explanation:** Represents the Länder governments. Many laws ('consent laws') need its approval, so opposition-led states can block federal policy — one reason German politics favours compromise.
- **German:** Bundesrat
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bundesrat
<a id="bundesrat-german-empire"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1871–1918
- **Short:** Federal council of the German Empire, representing the member states' governments.
- **Explanation:** One of the formal ruling institutions of imperial Germany alongside Kaiser, Chancellor and Reichstag. (Not to be confused with today's Bundesrat, the states' chamber in the Federal Republic.)
- **German:** Bundesrat
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bundestag
<a id="bundestag"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Short:** The German federal parliament, elected every four years.
- **Explanation:** Germany's main legislature. It elects the Chancellor, passes laws and must approve armed deployments abroad. Elected by a mixed-member proportional system, it is capped at 630 seats (from the 2025 election) and meets in the rebuilt Reichstag building.
- **German:** Bundestag
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)

### Bundestag inquiry commissions
<a id="bundestag-inquiry-commissions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1992–1998
- **Short:** Parliamentary commissions (1992–98) that investigated the SED dictatorship.
- **Explanation:** Two Enquete-Kommissionen of the German Bundestag that studied the history and consequences of the SED regime, broadly in line with a totalitarianism approach.
- **German:** Enquete-Kommissionen

### Bundestag Research Service
<a id="bundestag-research-service"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Bundestag research service · WD
- **Ambiguous forms:** WD (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Research service of the German parliament.
- **Explanation:** The German parliament's research service ('Wissenschaftliche Dienste', WD) writes background papers for MPs; its summary sums up denazification as 'administratively successful, ideologically failed'.
- **German:** Wissenschaftliche Dienste

### Bundeswehr
<a id="bundeswehr"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1955–
- **Short:** The armed forces of the Federal Republic, founded 1955.
- **Explanation:** A 'parliamentary army' whose deployments need Bundestag approval. After 2022 it received a €100 billion special fund as Germany began rearming.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)

### Bundschuh
<a id="bundschuh"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** from 1493
- **Short:** Peasant conspiracies from 1493, named after the laced peasant shoe used as their emblem.
- **Explanation:** Secret peasant revolt plots in southwest Germany that foreshadowed the Peasants' War of 1524–26.
- **German:** Bundschuh
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)

### bureaucratic absolutism
<a id="bureaucratic-absolutism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Rule through a powerful central bureaucracy, without real representation.
- **Explanation:** Rhine Confederation constitutions promised equality and representation but in practice served this and French war needs.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bürgergeld
<a id="burgergeld"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 2023–
- **Short:** 'Citizen's income': the basic welfare benefit that replaced Hartz IV in 2023.
- **Explanation:** Basic income support for job-seekers and others without enough income, introduced in 2023 in place of Hartz IV and reformed again from 2025.
- **German:** Bürgergeld
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Bürgerrecht
<a id="burgerrecht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Town citizenship: the legal status of a full member of a town.
- **Explanation:** A privilege purchased or inherited, not a right. Servants, journeymen, the poor, Jews and clergy were usually not citizens, so most townspeople had no political voice.
- **German:** Bürgerrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Burgfrieden
<a id="burgfrieden"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 1914
- **Short:** German 'castle peace': the WWI truce in which parties agreed to stop political fighting.
- **Explanation:** In 1914 the parties, including the SPD, set aside conflict to support the war; the SPD voted for war credits. Hunger and war-weariness later broke the truce apart.
- **German:** Burgfrieden
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Burgundians
<a id="burgundians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Burgundian
- **Short:** Germanic-speaking group that took over Roman provinces in the 5th century.
- **Explanation:** Their leaders, like Frankish and Gothic ones, took over Roman provinces as Western Roman authority collapsed in the 5th century, often first as Roman allies. The name survives in the region of Burgundy in France.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Burschenschaften
<a id="burschenschaften"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** from 1815
- **Short:** Nationalist-liberal student fraternities; founded in Jena 1815, banned by the Carlsbad Decrees.
- **Explanation:** The first Burschenschaft was founded in Jena in 1815 and adopted the colours black-red-gold. The fraternities organised the Wartburg festival (1817) and were dissolved under the Carlsbad Decrees in 1819.
- **German:** Burschenschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### BVP
<a id="bvp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1918–1933
- **Short:** Bavarian People's Party: the Bavarian Catholic party, sister to the Centre.
- **Explanation:** A carrier of political Catholicism in Weimar, it defended the republic until drifting right in 1928–33.
- **German:** Bayerische Volkspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## C

### Café Milani
<a id="cafe-milani"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** Right-wing (conservative) club of the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** Before parties existed, deputies formed clubs named after their meeting places. Café Milani gathered the conservative right.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Calvinism
<a id="calvinism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Calvinists · Reformed Protestantism
- **Short:** Reformed Protestantism following Calvin and Zwingli, stricter on church discipline than Lutheranism.
- **Explanation:** Excluded from the 1555 Peace of Augsburg, it spread to the Palatinate and Brandenburg's ruling house; its unclear legal status was one cause of the Thirty Years' War. The Peace of Westphalia finally recognised Calvinists in 1648.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### camarilla
<a id="camarilla"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A small, unofficial circle of advisers around a ruler.
- **Explanation:** Hindenburg's camarilla — his son Oskar, Meissner, Schleicher and others — was persuaded in January 1933 to accept Hitler as chancellor.
- **German:** Kamarilla
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### cameralism
<a id="cameralism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 17th–18th c.
- **Also written as:** Cameralism
- **Short:** German version of mercantilism: training officials to increase the prince's revenue.
- **Explanation:** Cameralists promoted population growth, manufactures and agriculture; chairs of 'cameral sciences' were founded at Halle and Frankfurt (Oder) in 1727.
- **German:** Kameralismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### canton system
<a id="canton-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1732/33
- **Also written as:** cantonists · canton
- **Short:** Prussian recruiting system: each regiment drew soldiers from its own district.
- **Explanation:** Peasants served a few months a year and farmed the rest, under noble landlords who were also their officers. Büsch said this militarised rural society; critics call it more negotiated.
- **German:** Kantonsystem
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### Capitalism
<a id="capitalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** capitalism
- **Short:** Economic system based on private ownership and markets.
- **Explanation:** Marxists in the SPD expected it to collapse; Bernstein argued it was not collapsing.
- **German:** Kapitalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### capitularies
<a id="capitularies"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** Capitularies
- **Short:** Written decrees of Carolingian kings, arranged in chapters.
- **Explanation:** They prescribe an ordered Christian society under one ruler, but enforcement depended on local counts and bishops; historians now read them as programmes and aspirations as much as descriptions.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: how far they describe practice rather than aims is debated.

### Capitulatio de partibus Saxoniae
<a id="capitulatio-de-partibus-saxoniae"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** c. 782/785
- **Short:** Charlemagne's decree for conquered Saxony (c. 782/785) threatening death for refusing baptism.
- **Explanation:** Shows that conversion in Saxony was imposed by force: it punished pagan rites and refusal of baptism with death.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Captain of Köpenick
<a id="captain-of-kopenick"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1906
- **Short:** 1906 hoax in which a cobbler dressed as an army captain took over Köpenick town hall.
- **Explanation:** Wilhelm Voigt, in a second-hand uniform, ordered soldiers to occupy the town hall near Berlin and seize its cash. The episode became a famous satire of Imperial Germans' obedience to the uniform.
- **German:** Hauptmann von Köpenick
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### care insurance
<a id="care-insurance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1995–
- **Also written as:** long-term care
- **Short:** Compulsory insurance, introduced in 1995, that pays for long-term nursing care.
- **Explanation:** The newest branch of German social insurance, added to a system whose roots go back to Bismarck's laws of the 1880s and Weimar's unemployment insurance (1927).
- **German:** Pflegeversicherung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Caritas
<a id="caritas"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Catholic welfare organisation, one of Germany's largest employers.
- **Explanation:** Runs hospitals, care homes and social services. Church welfare associations like Caritas remain major employers despite shrinking church membership.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Carlsbad Decrees
<a id="carlsbad-decrees"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1819
- **Also written as:** Carlsbad system
- **Short:** Measures of 1819 imposing press censorship, university surveillance and a ban on student fraternities.
- **Explanation:** Pushed through by Metternich after the murder of Kotzebue: censorship of publications under 320 pages, supervision of universities, dissolution of student fraternities and an investigating commission in Mainz. They made the Confederation an instrument of repression.
- **German:** Karlsbader Beschlüsse
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Carolingian minuscule
<a id="carolingian-minuscule"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** Caroline minuscule
- **Short:** Clear handwriting developed under Charlemagne; the root of our lowercase letters.
- **Explanation:** Monasteries used this script to copy liturgy and classical texts, preserving much ancient literature.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Carolingian Renaissance
<a id="carolingian-renaissance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Short:** Revival of learning, script and church reform under Charlemagne and his heirs.
- **Explanation:** Carried by a tiny clerical elite in monasteries and bishoprics, it reformed education and preserved classical texts.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Carolingians
<a id="carolingians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 751–911
- **Also written as:** Carolingian
- **Short:** Frankish royal dynasty (751–911 in the east), named after Charlemagne.
- **Explanation:** They deposed the Merovingians in 751 with papal approval and ruled through personal bonds, counts, inspectors and the Church. Their East Frankish line ended in 911.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### cartel law
<a id="cartel-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1957
- **Short:** West Germany's 1957 competition law against price-fixing cartels.
- **Explanation:** Act against Restraints of Competition, a pillar of the social market economy, meant to break with the German tradition of cartels (firms agreeing prices among themselves) and ensure competition.
- **German:** Gesetz gegen Wettbewerbsbeschränkungen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Cartels
<a id="cartels"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** cartels
- **Short:** Agreements among firms to fix prices or output.
- **Explanation:** Legal and common in the Empire (~300 by 1900 by some counts).
- **German:** Kartelle
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Casino
<a id="casino"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** Right-centre liberal club of the Frankfurt Assembly, its largest group.
- **Explanation:** Named after its meeting place, it gathered moderate constitutional liberals.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### "Caster Ware" Vase with Hunt Scene
<a id="caster-ware-vase-with-hunt-scene"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** mid-100s CE
- **Also written as:** Caster Ware
- **Short:** Roman pottery vase made in a Cologne workshop in the mid-2nd century CE.
- **Explanation:** Workshops in Roman Cologne on the Rhine frontier produced such pottery for soldiers and townspeople on both sides of the Limes — evidence that the frontier was a zone of trade, not a sealed wall. Now in the Cleveland Museum of Art.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### catch-all party
<a id="catch-all-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** catch-all parties · people's party
- **Short:** A big party appealing to voters of all classes and religions, not one social group.
- **Explanation:** Type of party (Volkspartei) that seeks votes across society rather than representing one class, religion or region. The CDU/CSU and, after 1959, the SPD became such parties, helping stabilise West German democracy.
- **German:** Volkspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### catch-up growth
<a id="catch-up-growth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** catch-up
- **Short:** Fast growth of an economy recovering to where it would have been without war or crisis.
- **Explanation:** Economists' term for rapid growth as a country closes the gap to its earlier trend or to richer economies. Most historians see it as the main cause of West Germany's 'economic miracle'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### catechism
<a id="catechism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Also written as:** catechises
- **Short:** A question-and-answer summary of Christian doctrine taught to children.
- **Explanation:** After the Reformation pastors used it to instruct and discipline their parishes; schooling expanded so children could read it.
- **German:** Katechismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### cathedral chapters
<a id="cathedral-chapters"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Cathedral chapters
- **Short:** Bodies of clergy at a cathedral who elected the bishop.
- **Explanation:** Dominated by noble families, they made church offices a career track for younger sons.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Catholic social teaching
<a id="catholic-social-teaching"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Catholic Church doctrine on workers' rights and social justice.
- **Explanation:** Shaped by Bishop Ketteler and Rerum Novarum (1891), it inspired Christian unions.
- **German:** katholische Soziallehre
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Catholicism
<a id="catholicism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Catholic
- **Ambiguous forms:** Catholic (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The Christianity of the Latin Church led by the pope.
- **Explanation:** Medieval Germany was wholly Catholic; after the Reformation, Catholic areas were concentrated in the south and west. After 1945, Catholic historians led the revaluation of the Holy Roman Empire.
- **Main chapter:** [`themes/religion.md`](../themes/religion.md)

### Catholics
<a id="catholics"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Catholic
- **Ambiguous forms:** Catholic (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Members of the Roman Catholic Church, one of Germany's two large churches.
- **Explanation:** Catholics once formed a distinct social and political milieu. Together with Protestants they fell below half the population by the mid-2020s, though church welfare organisations like Caritas remain large employers.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)

### CDU/CSU
<a id="cdu-csu"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** CDU
- **Ambiguous forms:** CDU (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Christian Democratic Union and its Bavarian sister, the Christian Social Union.
- **Explanation:** Founded in 1945 as a party open to both Catholics and Protestants, it was licensed by the Allies and became West Germany's leading party under Adenauer. The CSU is its partner in Bavaria.
- **German:** Christlich Demokratische Union / Christlich-Soziale Union
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Celts
<a id="celts"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Celtic
- **Short:** Peoples speaking Celtic languages who lived in the Rhine region before and under Roman rule.
- **Explanation:** Celtic-speaking locals formed a large part of the population of the Roman provinces; their gods merged with Roman ones. Modern Germans descend from them as well as from Germanic, Roman and Slavic populations.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`

### censorship
<a id="censorship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Censorship
- **Short:** State control over what may be printed or published.
- **Explanation:** It existed everywhere, but German fragmentation meant a book banned in one state could be printed in another.
- **German:** Zensur
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)

<a id="central-association-of-german-industrialists"></a>

### Central Committee

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Central Committee Secretariat
- **Short:** The SED's leading party body; its secretariat ran the party apparatus.
- **Explanation:** Formally the SED's highest body between party congresses. Its Secretariat, headed by the First/General Secretary, directed the party apparatus that shadowed every state organ.

<a id="central-european-history"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Also written as:** CEH
- **Short:** Academic journal on the history of German-speaking Central Europe.
- **Explanation:** Scholarly journal in which Samuel Huneke published his 2022 survey of trends in GDR history.

### Central Investigation Commission
<a id="central-investigation-commission"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1819
- **Short:** Confederation body in Mainz that investigated liberal and national 'subversives' after 1819.
- **Explanation:** Created by the Carlsbad Decrees to track down 'demagogic activities'. With the later Mainz Information Bureau (1833), which coordinated spies, it embodied the Confederation's police function.

### Centre Party
<a id="centre-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1870–1933
- **Also written as:** Zentrum · Centre · Catholic Centre
- **Ambiguous forms:** Centre (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Catholic political party; a governing pillar of the Weimar Republic until 1932.
- **Explanation:** It formed part of the 1917 Reichstag majority and the Weimar coalition. Catholic voters were relatively resistant to Nazism. Chancellor Brüning came from its ranks; it drifted to the right from 1928.
- **German:** Zentrum
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)

### chancellor democracy
<a id="chancellor-democracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Label for Adenauer's strongly chancellor-centred style of government in the 1950s.
- **Explanation:** Term for West German government under Konrad Adenauer, when the chancellor dominated the cabinet and party and set policy personally, using the strong position the Basic Law gave the office.
- **German:** Kanzlerdemokratie
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

### charismatic authority
<a id="charismatic-authority"></a>

### Cherusci
<a id="cherusci"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Germanic-speaking group in northwest Germany whose leader Arminius defeated Varus in 9 CE.
- **Explanation:** Their confederation under Arminius was short-lived. Arminius fought for leadership among them — not for 'Germany' — and was killed by his own kinsmen.

- **Dates:** 2013–
- **Also written as:** legal right to a place
- **Short:** Legal entitlement since 2013 to a childcare place for children from age one.
- **Explanation:** With parental allowance (2007) it marks Germany's shift toward the dual-earner family model.

### Christendom
<a id="christendom"></a>

### Christian Democracy
<a id="christian-democracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Political movement combining Christian values, social market economy and anti-communism.
- **Explanation:** In West Germany, the cross-confessional, anti-communist, pro-Western politics of the CDU/CSU under Adenauer, uniting Catholics and Protestants in one party for the first time.

### Christian Democratic Union
<a id="christian-democratic-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1945–
- **Also written as:** CDU · CDU-East
- **Ambiguous forms:** CDU (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Germany's main centre-right party; led the government under Merkel and Merz.
- **Explanation:** Founded after 1945 as a cross-confessional Christian party. Together with its Bavarian sister party, the CSU, it won 28.5% in 2025 and leads the government under Friedrich Merz.
- **German:** Christlich Demokratische Union (CDU)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Christian Social Union
<a id="christian-social-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1945–
- **Also written as:** CSU
- **Short:** Bavaria's centre-right party, sister of the CDU in the Bundestag.
- **Explanation:** Runs only in Bavaria, while the CDU runs everywhere else; in the Bundestag the two form a joint group, the 'Union' (CDU/CSU).
- **German:** Christlich-Soziale Union (CSU)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Christian unions
<a id="christian-unions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Catholic (and some Protestant) trade unions opposed to socialist ones.
- **Explanation:** Many Catholic workers joined them and voted for the Centre Party.
- **German:** christliche Gewerkschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Christian-German state
<a id="christian-german-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Christian-German
- **Short:** Conservative ideal of a monarchy based on estates and Christianity.
- **Explanation:** Associated with Frederick William IV's circle. It saw the nation as an organic whole under a Christian monarch, not a community of citizens.
- **German:** christlich-germanischer Staat
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Christianisation
<a id="christianisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Spreading Christianity and its institutions among a population.
- **Explanation:** It reshaped the calendar, burial, marriage and taxes (tithes). In Saxony it was imposed by war, massacre and law.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### church courts
<a id="church-courts"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Church courts
- **Short:** Courts of the Church with jurisdiction over clergy, marriage, wills and morals.
- **Explanation:** Their power was a source of anticlerical resentment.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### church tax
<a id="church-tax"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Church tax
- **Short:** Tax paid by church members, collected by the state on behalf of the churches.
- **Explanation:** A surcharge on income tax paid by registered members of the major churches, which the state collects and passes on. It is an example of the close cooperation between church and state that continued in the Federal Republic.
- **German:** Kirchensteuer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### church visitation
<a id="church-visitation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** visitations · visitation reports
- **Short:** Official inspection tours of parishes checking pastors' teaching and villagers' beliefs and morals.
- **Explanation:** Used by Protestant princes to enforce their church order. The reports reveal pastors complaining of villagers' ignorance, magic and non-attendance.
- **German:** Visitation
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### citizens in arms
<a id="citizens-in-arms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Prussian reformers' ideal of an army of patriotic citizens rather than drilled subjects.
- **Explanation:** After defeat by Napoleon, reformers linked military service to citizenship; universal conscription followed in 1813/14. The KB notes both liberal and conservative strands in this tradition.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### citizens' committees
<a id="citizens-committees"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1989–1990
- **Short:** Local groups of citizens that occupied Stasi offices to secure files in 1989–90.
- **Explanation:** Bürgerkomitees formed to control the dissolution of the Stasi and stop destruction of its records, beginning in Erfurt on 4 December 1989.
- **German:** Bürgerkomitees
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### citizenship law
<a id="citizenship-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1913–
- **Also written as:** Citizenship
- **Short:** Rules on who is a German citizen: once by descent, now also by birth and residence.
- **Explanation:** The 1913 law made citizenship depend on descent from a German parent. The 2000 reform added partial birthright citizenship (ius soli) for children of long-term residents; the 2024 reform cut the residence requirement to five years and allowed dual citizenship; a 3-year fast track was abolished in 2025.
- **German:** Staatsangehörigkeitsrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### civic culture
<a id="civic-culture"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Citizens' democratic attitudes and trust in institutions that support a democracy.
- **Explanation:** Concept from Almond and Verba's comparative study. They found West Germans' civic culture weak in 1959; by the 1980s it resembled other Western democracies.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Civic guards
<a id="civic-guards"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Also written as:** civic guards
- **Short:** Citizens' militias formed in towns during the 1848 revolution.
- **Explanation:** They were part of the burst of urban political participation in 1848.
- **German:** Bürgerwehr
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Civic nationalism
<a id="civic-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** civic
- **Ambiguous forms:** civic (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Nationalism defining the nation as a community of citizens with equal rights.
- **Explanation:** Hans Kohn contrasted a 'western/civic' type with a German 'eastern/ethnic' type; historians now see both strands in Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### civic nationhood
<a id="civic-nationhood"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Civic vs ethnic nationhood
- **Short:** Belonging to the nation defined by citizenship and shared values, not ancestry.
- **Explanation:** The KB describes a shift in Germany toward civic citizenship (for example birthright citizenship from 2000). The radical right contests it in favour of an ethnic idea of who is German.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Civil Code
<a id="civil-code"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1900–
- **Also written as:** BGB
- **Short:** Germany's main code of private law, covering contracts, property, marriage and family.
- **Explanation:** The Bürgerliches Gesetzbuch (BGB), in force since 1900. Its family law kept the husband-centred 'housewife marriage' in West Germany long after the Basic Law had declared men and women equal, until the 1977 reform.
- **German:** Bürgerliches Gesetzbuch (BGB)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### civil defence
<a id="civil-defence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Measures to protect civilians in war, such as shelters and drills.
- **Explanation:** Part of West German Cold War life, reflecting fears of nuclear war.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Civil marriage
<a id="civil-marriage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1875
- **Also written as:** civil marriage
- **Short:** Marriage performed by the state, made compulsory in 1875.
- **Explanation:** A Kulturkampf measure removing marriage from Church control.
- **German:** Zivilehe
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Civil Service Law
<a id="civil-service-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1933
- **Short:** April 1933 law dismissing Jewish and politically unwanted civil servants.
- **Explanation:** It was the first major law excluding Jews and opened careers for non-Jewish Germans.
- **German:** Berufsbeamtengesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### civil-rights movement
<a id="civil-rights-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1980s–1990
- **Also written as:** Civil-rights movement · civil-rights movements · civil-rights activists
- **Short:** East German opposition groups of the 1980s–90 seeking a democratic, reformed GDR.
- **Explanation:** Small church-sheltered groups for peace, environment and human rights that grew into movements like Neues Forum. They favoured democratic socialism and a new constitution, but became a minority as most East Germans chose rapid unification.
- **German:** Bürgerrechtsbewegung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### class enemy
<a id="class-enemy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Communist label for people or states cast as opponents of the working class.
- **Explanation:** GDR propaganda spoke of the 'imperialist class enemy', above all the West. The KB lists it beside Nazi slogans such as 'enemies of the Reich' as a recurring way authoritarian regimes won support by naming enemies.
- **German:** Klassenfeind
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Clean Wehrmacht myth
<a id="clean-wehrmacht-myth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** clean Wehrmacht
- **Short:** Post-war belief that the army fought honourably and left crimes to the SS.
- **Explanation:** Research and the Wehrmacht exhibition showed extensive army participation in crimes.
- **German:** saubere Wehrmacht
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Cleveland Museum of Art
<a id="cleveland-museum-of-art"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Art museum in Cleveland, Ohio (USA), whose open-access images appear in this KB.
- **Explanation:** Its public-domain (CC0) open-access collection supplies several illustrations here, such as the Roman 'Caster Ware' vase from Cologne and a leaf of the Nuremberg Chronicle.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### client leaders
<a id="client-leaders"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Local rulers beyond the frontier whom Rome paid and armed to keep order for it.
- **Explanation:** Beyond the Limes Rome ruled indirectly through such leaders; access to Roman money concentrated power in their hands.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### co-determination
<a id="co-determination"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1951–
- **Also written as:** codetermination
- **Short:** Workers' right to seats on company boards and a say in company decisions.
- **Explanation:** West German system (Mitbestimmung) introduced in coal and steel in 1951 and extended to large firms in 1976. Worker representatives sit on supervisory boards, institutionalising compromise between labour and capital.
- **German:** Mitbestimmung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Coal syndicate
<a id="coal-syndicate"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1893
- **Also written as:** coal syndicate
- **Short:** Cartel of Ruhr coal producers formed in 1893.
- **Explanation:** A prime example of organised capitalism.
- **German:** Rheinisch-Westfälisches Kohlen-Syndikat
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Coalition
<a id="coalition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** coalitions · combination · coalition
- **Ambiguous forms:** combination (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Workers' right to combine into unions and strike.
- **Explanation:** Illegal in most German states until the 1860s; allowed by the 1869 Industrial Code.
- **German:** Koalitionsfreiheit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### coalition committee
<a id="coalition-committee"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Informal meeting of coalition party leaders where key government decisions are made.
- **Explanation:** Because German governments are almost always coalitions, much real power lies here and in coalition agreements, constraining the Chancellor's formal right to set policy.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Code Napoléon
<a id="code-napoleon"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1804
- **Also written as:** Code civil
- **Short:** Napoleon's French civil law code, establishing equality before the law and property rights.
- **Explanation:** Introduced in Westphalia and adapted in Baden (Badisches Landrecht, 1810), it spread Enlightenment reforms by force.
- **German:** Code civil
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Cold War
<a id="cold-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1947–1989/91
- **Short:** Global rivalry between the US-led West and the Soviet-led East after 1945.
- **Explanation:** Confrontation between two blocs, fought through arms races, propaganda and proxy wars rather than direct war. Divided Germany was its front line, with Berlin as the flashpoint.
- **German:** Kalter Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### collective bargaining
<a id="collective-bargaining"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Collective bargaining
- **Short:** Negotiation of wages and conditions between unions and employers without state interference.
- **Explanation:** Germany's 'Tarifautonomie' goes back to the 1918 Stinnes–Legien Agreement. Coverage by collective agreements has declined to about half of employees.
- **German:** Tarifautonomie
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### collective bargaining autonomy
<a id="collective-bargaining-autonomy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Tarifautonomie
- **Short:** Right of unions and employers to set wages by negotiation, without state interference.
- **Explanation:** Principle (Tarifautonomie) that wage levels are agreed between trade unions and employers' associations. Together with co-determination it formed West Germany's corporatist 'class compromise'.
- **German:** Tarifautonomie
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Collective guilt
<a id="collective-guilt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** collective guilt
- **Short:** Idea that all Germans were guilty for Nazi crimes.
- **Explanation:** Most Germans after 1945 rejected the idea that the whole nation was guilty. Karl Jaspers distinguished criminal, political, moral and metaphysical guilt to clarify who was responsible for what.
- **German:** Kollektivschuld
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### collectivisation
<a id="collectivisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1952–1960
- **Also written as:** Forced collectivisation
- **Short:** Forcing farmers to merge their land into collective farms under state control.
- **Explanation:** In the GDR, farmers were pushed into agricultural cooperatives (LPG) between 1952 and 1960, completed in the 'Socialist Spring' of 1960; many fled west as a result.
- **German:** Kollektivierung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Cologne church conflict
<a id="cologne-church-conflict"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1837
- **Short:** 1837 clash between Prussia and the Catholic Church over mixed marriages; Cologne's archbishop was arrested.
- **Explanation:** Prussia's arrest of the Archbishop of Cologne mobilised Catholics politically and is seen as a starting point of political Catholicism.
- **German:** Kölner Wirren
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Colonial goods
<a id="colonial-goods"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** colonial goods
- **Short:** Imported goods such as coffee, tea, sugar sold in 'colonial goods' shops.
- **Explanation:** A sign of how colonialism entered everyday German culture.
- **German:** Kolonialwaren
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Colonial revisionism
<a id="colonial-revisionism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1919–1945
- **Also written as:** colonial revisionism
- **Short:** Campaign in Weimar Germany to regain the lost colonies.
- **Explanation:** Colonial ideas persisted without colonies after 1919.
- **German:** Kolonialrevisionismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### colonialism
<a id="colonialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Germany's overseas empire (1884–1919), now increasingly debated in German memory culture.
- **Explanation:** Germany held colonies in Africa and the Pacific until the First World War. The KB notes debates over colonialism as a newer strand of German memory culture.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_README.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Comecon
<a id="comecon"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1949–1991
- **Short:** Economic organisation of the Soviet bloc; the GDR joined in 1950.
- **Explanation:** Council for Mutual Economic Assistance, coordinating trade and planning among communist states. Its collapse after 1989 destroyed East German firms' markets.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### comitatus
<a id="comitatus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Warrior retinue bound personally to a leader in return for food, gifts and weapons.
- **Explanation:** The most important 'institution' of early Germanic-speaking societies: loyalty in exchange for reward. If rewards failed, followers could turn on the leader. This personal bond is a long-term ancestor of medieval lordship.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### common chest
<a id="common-chest"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Reformation-era town fund pooling former church income for poor relief.
- **Explanation:** Part of the reorganisation of poor relief after monasteries and church property were taken over by towns and princes.
- **German:** gemeiner Kasten
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Common Penny
<a id="common-penny"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1495
- **Short:** Imperial tax introduced in 1495 — the first general imperial tax — which largely failed.
- **Explanation:** Part of the 1495 imperial reform; its failure shows how little the emperor could tax the territories.
- **German:** Gemeiner Pfennig
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### communalism
<a id="communalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Kommunalismus
- **Short:** Peter Blickle's thesis that villages and towns governed themselves collectively.
- **Explanation:** It stresses peasant agency and negotiation instead of 'misery' narratives.
- **German:** Kommunalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Communicative silence
<a id="communicative-silence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** communicative silence
- **Short:** Lübbe's thesis that silence about Nazi pasts helped stabilise democracy.
- **Explanation:** Hermann Lübbe argued in 1983 that not discussing individuals' Nazi pasts helped integrate millions of former supporters into the new democracy. Critics argue this silence damaged democracy.
- **German:** kommunikatives Beschweigen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)

### Communism
<a id="communism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** communism
- **Short:** Radical socialism aiming at abolishing private property through workers' revolution.
- **Explanation:** In the 1840s it was represented by Weitling and Marx and Engels, whose Communist Manifesto appeared in February 1848.
- **German:** Kommunismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
