### Communist League
<a id="communist-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1847–1852
- **Short:** Small revolutionary group for which Marx and Engels wrote the Communist Manifesto (1848).
- **Explanation:** The KB counts it among the origins of the German labour movement in the 1840s.
- **German:** Bund der Kommunisten
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Communist Manifesto
<a id="communist-manifesto"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Short:** Pamphlet by Marx and Engels (February 1848) calling for workers' revolution.
- **Explanation:** Published just before the 1848 revolutions but with little influence on them. It later became a founding text of the socialist movement.
- **German:** Manifest der Kommunistischen Partei
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### complementary Empire-State
<a id="complementary-empire-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** komplementärer Reichs-Staat
- **Short:** Georg Schmidt's idea that Empire and territories together formed one German state.
- **Explanation:** Critics reject projecting modern state and nation concepts onto the Empire.
- **German:** komplementärer Reichs-Staat
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### composite monarchy
<a id="composite-monarchy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A state made of several lands, each keeping its own estates and laws, under one ruler.
- **Explanation:** The Habsburg monarchy joined the Austrian lands, the Bohemian and Hungarian crowns and others.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### compulsory schooling
<a id="compulsory-schooling"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** Legal duty for children to attend school.
- **Explanation:** Decreed in Weimar (1619), Prussia (1717/1763) and Austria (1774), but implementation lagged.
- **German:** Schulpflicht
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Concentration camp
<a id="concentration-camp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** concentration camp
- **Ambiguous forms:** concentration camp (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Camps where the Nazis imprisoned opponents and persecuted groups without trial.
- **Explanation:** Used from 1933 against political opponents, later against Jews, Roma and many others. Distinct from the death camps built purely for murder.
- **German:** Konzentrationslager (KZ)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### concentration camps
<a id="concentration-camps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Also written as:** Konzentrationslager · concentration camp
- **Ambiguous forms:** concentration camp (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Camps where the Nazis imprisoned people without trial.
- **Explanation:** The first, such as Dachau, opened in March 1933 for political opponents. Run by the SS, the camps later became central to mass murder.
- **German:** Konzentrationslager (KZ)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: continuity to Nazi camps debated.

### Concordat of Worms
<a id="concordat-of-worms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1122
- **Short:** 1122 agreement between pope and emperor ending the Investiture Controversy.
- **Explanation:** It separated spiritual investiture (by the Church) from temporal investiture (by the king). Long-term, bishops became princes in their own right.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### confederation
<a id="confederation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** confederative structures
- **Short:** Loose union of states that keep their sovereignty but cooperate in common institutions.
- **Explanation:** Kohl's 1989 Ten-Point Plan foresaw confederative structures between the two German states as a step towards unity; events soon overtook this gradual path.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Confederation of the Rhine
<a id="confederation-of-the-rhine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1806–1813
- **Also written as:** Rhine Confederation
- **Short:** Napoleon's alliance of German states that left the Holy Roman Empire in 1806.
- **Explanation:** Formed by 16 princes, later about 36 members. Its states — Bavaria, Württemberg, Baden, Westphalia — introduced French-style administration and legal equality while supplying troops for Napoleon.
- **German:** Rheinbund
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Conference on Security and Co-operation in Europe
<a id="conference-on-security-and-co-operation-in-europe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1973–1994
- **Also written as:** KSZE
- **Short:** East–West conference that produced the 1975 Helsinki Final Act (German: KSZE).
- **Explanation:** Forum of European states, the USA and Canada. Historian Bernd Rother argues that without Ostpolitik there would have been no such conference.
- **German:** Konferenz über Sicherheit und Zusammenarbeit in Europa (KSZE)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Confessing Church
<a id="confessing-church"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Protestant opposition group defending church independence from Nazi control.
- **Explanation:** It resisted the German Christians' takeover of the church but generally did not defend Jews. Dietrich Bonhoeffer belonged to it.
- **German:** Bekennende Kirche
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### confession (denomination)
<a id="confession"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** confessional · bi-confessional · tri-confessional
- **Ambiguous forms:** confessional (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** In this period, a church denomination with its own creed — Catholic, Lutheran or Reformed.
- **Explanation:** After 1555 the Empire was 'bi-confessional' (Catholic and Lutheran), later 'tri-confessional' with Calvinism. Confessional identity became a key marker between neighbouring territories.
- **German:** Konfession
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Confessional
<a id="confessional"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** confessional
- **Ambiguous forms:** confessional (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Relating to religious denomination, chiefly Protestant versus Catholic.
- **Explanation:** Confessional divides shaped German politics and society throughout the 19th century.
- **German:** konfessionell
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### confessionalisation
<a id="confessionalisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Confessionalisation thesis · Confessionalisation
- **Short:** Thesis that the churches, by shaping confessional identity and discipline, strengthened early modern states.
- **Explanation:** Proposed by Heinz Schilling and Wolfgang Reinhard in the 1980s. Critics say it is too centred on the state, downplays theological differences and overrates how well discipline worked.
- **German:** Konfessionalisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: historians disagree whether confessionalisation strengthened state power.

### confirmation
<a id="confirmation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Christian rite in which teenagers affirm their faith and become full church members.
- **Explanation:** In the GDR the state's secular Jugendweihe largely displaced Protestant confirmation, as church membership collapsed.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### confirmed right-wing extremist
<a id="confirmed-right-wing-extremist"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 2025–
- **Short:** Highest classification by Germany's domestic intelligence service for an extremist organisation.
- **Explanation:** The service upgraded the AfD from a 'suspected case' to 'confirmed right-wing extremist' in 2025. In February 2026 the Administrative Court of Cologne provisionally blocked the label: it found a 'strong suspicion' but not the certainty needed for the party as a whole. Main proceedings are pending.
- **German:** gesichert rechtsextremistisch
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### confraternities
<a id="confraternities"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Confraternities
- **Short:** Lay religious brotherhoods for prayer, charity and mutual help.
- **Explanation:** They organised piety and poor relief; guilds often also functioned as confraternities.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Congress of Vienna
<a id="congress-of-vienna"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1814–1815
- **Short:** Meeting of European powers that redrew the map after Napoleon and created the German Confederation.
- **Explanation:** Instead of restoring the Holy Roman Empire, it created the German Confederation, a league of sovereign states, and confirmed the monarchs' dynastic legitimacy. It opened a long period of peace (1815–48).
- **German:** Wiener Kongress
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### conscientious objection
<a id="conscientious-objection"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Refusing military service for reasons of conscience.
- **Explanation:** The Basic Law guarantees this right; the KB cites it as part of the Federal Republic's deliberate demilitarisation of political culture.
- **German:** Kriegsdienstverweigerung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### conscription
<a id="conscription"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** universal conscription · Conscription
- **Short:** Compulsory military service, suspended in Germany since 2011.
- **Explanation:** West Germany had compulsory service from the 1950s. It was suspended in 2011; the 2025 Military Service Modernisation Act kept service voluntary but allows it to be reactivated by law.
- **German:** Wehrpflicht
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/militarism.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Conservatism
<a id="conservatism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** conservatism
- **Short:** Ideology defending monarchy, legitimate thrones, the nobility and a Christian state.
- **Explanation:** After 1815 it stood for restoring legitimate dynasties and a Christian state against liberal and national demands. It reorganised in 1848, e.g. through the Kreuzzeitung.
- **German:** Konservatismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Conservative Revolution
<a id="conservative-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1920s
- **Short:** Anti-liberal, authoritarian intellectual current in Weimar Germany.
- **Explanation:** Writers such as Moeller van den Bruck, Spengler, Jünger and Carl Schmitt prepared an anti-democratic climate, though many despised the Nazis' vulgarity.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### consistory
<a id="consistory"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** consistories
- **Short:** Church board run by the prince's officials to govern a Protestant territorial church.
- **Explanation:** Consistories supervised pastors, doctrine and morals, showing how church government was absorbed into princely administration after the Reformation.
- **German:** Konsistorium
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Constitutio Antoniniana
<a id="constitutio-antoniniana"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 212 CE
- **Short:** Roman decree of 212 CE giving citizenship to nearly all free inhabitants of the empire.
- **Explanation:** Issued under Emperor Caracalla. After it, nearly all free people in the Rhine provinces were Roman citizens with legal protections; slaves remained without rights.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Constitution of estates
<a id="constitution-of-estates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** landständische Verfassung · constitution of estates
- **Short:** Promised state constitution with representative assemblies, as in Article 13 of the Federal Act.
- **Explanation:** The Federal Act promised every member state such a constitution with representative assemblies ('estates'). Bavaria, Baden, Württemberg and Hesse-Darmstadt introduced them in 1818–20; Prussia and Austria did not until 1848.
- **German:** landständische Verfassung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### constitutional complaint
<a id="constitutional-complaint"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1951–
- **Short:** Right of any citizen to appeal to the Constitutional Court if the state violates their basic rights.
- **Explanation:** Procedure available since 1951 through which individuals can bring a case directly to the Federal Constitutional Court, making the Basic Law's rights enforceable in practice.
- **German:** Verfassungsbeschwerde
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Constitutional monarchy
<a id="constitutional-monarchy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** constitutional monarchy
- **Short:** Monarchy whose powers are limited by a written constitution.
- **Explanation:** 1848 liberals wanted it; democrats wanted a republic. In Germany the monarch still appointed ministers who were not responsible to parliament.
- **German:** konstitutionelle Monarchie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### constitutional patriotism
<a id="constitutional-patriotism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Constitutional patriotism · Verfassungspatriotismus
- **Short:** Loyalty to a democratic constitution's values rather than to an ethnic nation.
- **Explanation:** Idea (Verfassungspatriotismus) of Dolf Sternberger, popularised by Jürgen Habermas, that West Germans could identify with the Basic Law's democratic values instead of a discredited ethnic nationalism.
- **German:** Verfassungspatriotismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### construction of socialism
<a id="construction-of-socialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1952
- **Short:** SED programme from 1952 to transform the GDR on the Soviet model.
- **Explanation:** Policy (Aufbau des Sozialismus) announced in 1952, which intensified collectivisation, heavy industry and pressure on churches and the middle classes, contributing to the crisis of 1953.
- **German:** Aufbau des Sozialismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### constructive vote of no confidence
<a id="constructive-vote-of-no-confidence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Art. 67
- **Short:** Rule that parliament can remove a chancellor only by electing a successor at the same time.
- **Explanation:** Article 67 of the Basic Law, designed against Weimar, where hostile majorities could topple governments without agreeing on a replacement. It has succeeded only once: in 1982, when Helmut Kohl replaced Helmut Schmidt.
- **German:** konstruktives Misstrauensvotum
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Consumer co-operatives
<a id="consumer-co-operatives"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** consumer co-ops · co-operatives
- **Short:** Shops owned by their working-class members.
- **Explanation:** Part of the Social Democratic milieu from cradle to grave.
- **German:** Konsumgenossenschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Continental System
<a id="continental-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1806–1813
- **Short:** Napoleon's blockade of British trade with continental Europe.
- **Explanation:** It hurt ports but protected some continental manufactures.
- **German:** Kontinentalsperre
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Continuity thesis
<a id="continuity-thesis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** continuity thesis
- **Short:** Argument that colonial violence formed part of the genealogy of Nazi crimes.
- **Explanation:** Advanced by Zimmerer and Hull; critics stress differences.
- **German:** Kontinuitätsthese
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED.

### contract workers
<a id="contract-workers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Foreign workers brought to the GDR under state agreements, kept segregated.
- **Explanation:** Vertragsarbeiter from Vietnam, Mozambique, Cuba and Angola (about 90,000–94,000 in 1989). They lived separately, and pregnant women workers faced deportation.
- **German:** Vertragsarbeiter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### contributions
<a id="contributions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Payments forced from occupied areas to feed and pay armies in the Thirty Years' War.
- **Explanation:** Military entrepreneurs like Wallenstein financed whole armies this way, spreading hunger and ruin.
- **German:** Kontributionen
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### cooperative separation
<a id="cooperative-separation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1919–
- **Also written as:** Cooperative separation
- **Short:** German model of church–state relations: no state church, but close cooperation.
- **Explanation:** Settled in the Weimar constitution (1919) and taken into the Basic Law (Art. 140): the state collects church tax and allows religious education, and churches run welfare services, though no church is official.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### corporatism
<a id="corporatism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Corporatism · corporatist
- **Short:** System where big interest groups (unions, employers) negotiate policy with each other and the state.
- **Explanation:** In West Germany, organised interests — unions, employers' associations, churches — bargained over wages and social policy, institutionalising compromise between classes rather than open conflict.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### correctio
<a id="correctio"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Carolingian 'correction': reform of church, script, education and law.
- **Explanation:** The programme behind the 'Carolingian Renaissance', carried by a tiny clerical elite.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### cottagers
<a id="cottagers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Villagers with a cottage and little or no land.
- **Explanation:** Below the full-holding farmers (Vollbauern) in village society; many had to work for others to survive.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Council of Ministers
<a id="council-of-ministers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–1990
- **Short:** The GDR government, formally responsible for administration but subordinate to the SED.
- **Explanation:** Formal government of the GDR, which executed decisions made in the SED Politburo. In 1989–90 it was led by Hans Modrow and then, after free elections, by Lothar de Maizière.
- **German:** Ministerrat

### Council of People's Deputies
<a id="council-of-peoples-deputies"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1918–1919
- **Short:** Revolutionary government of SPD and USPD after November 1918.
- **Explanation:** It ruled until the elections to the National Assembly in January 1919, which led to the Weimar Republic.
- **German:** Rat der Volksbeauftragten

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Council of State
<a id="council-of-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1960–1990
- **Also written as:** State Council
- **Ambiguous forms:** State Council (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The GDR's collective head of state from 1960.
- **Explanation:** Body that replaced the office of president after 1960; it was chaired by the SED leader (Ulbricht, later Honecker) and had no independent power.
- **German:** Staatsrat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Council of the European Union
<a id="council-of-the-european-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Council
- **Ambiguous forms:** Council (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** EU body where national ministers vote on EU laws.
- **Explanation:** Germany carries the most weight in its votes because it has the largest population.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Council of Trent
<a id="council-of-trent"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1545–1563
- **Short:** Catholic Church council that defined doctrine and reforms against Protestantism.
- **Explanation:** Held in Trento (today northern Italy), it launched the Counter-Reformation's renewal of parishes and piety.
- **German:** Konzil von Trient
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Councils
<a id="councils"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1918–1919
- **Also written as:** councils
- **Ambiguous forms:** councils (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Workers' and soldiers' councils formed in the 1918 revolution.
- **Explanation:** They took local power in November 1918.
- **German:** Räte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### count
<a id="count"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** counts · comites
- **Short:** Royal official governing a district; later a hereditary noble title.
- **Explanation:** Carolingian counts (Latin comites) were the king's local agents for justice and military service. Whether they obeyed depended on their own interests; the office later became hereditary noble lordship.
- **German:** Graf
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### count palatine of the Rhine
<a id="count-palatine-of-the-rhine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Short:** Ruler of the Rhenish Palatinate and one of the seven prince-electors.
- **Explanation:** Named as an elector in the Golden Bull (1356).
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Counter-Reformation
<a id="counter-reformation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 16th–17th c.
- **Short:** Catholic renewal responding to the Reformation, driven by the Jesuits and the Council of Trent.
- **Explanation:** It renewed parish life and piety in Catholic areas, helping make religion a marker of identity between neighbouring territories.
- **German:** Gegenreformation
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Counter-revolution
<a id="counter-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Also written as:** counter-revolution
- **Short:** The princes' military recovery against the revolution from autumn 1848.
- **Explanation:** Vienna was retaken in October 1848, the Prussian assembly dissolved in December, and the last uprisings crushed in 1849.
- **German:** Gegenrevolution
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### country of immigration
<a id="country-of-immigration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** immigration society · immigration country
- **Short:** A country that permanently receives immigrants; West Germany officially denied being one.
- **Explanation:** Until the late 1990s official doctrine held that 'Germany is not a country of immigration' (Einwanderungsland), although millions of former guest workers and their families had settled permanently.
- **German:** Einwanderungsland
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### court Jews
<a id="court-jews"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Hofjuden
- **Short:** Jewish financiers and suppliers serving princely courts.
- **Explanation:** They financed princes but were vulnerable; Joseph Süß Oppenheimer was executed in Württemberg in 1738 after his prince's death.
- **German:** Hofjuden
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Court of Justice of the European Union
<a id="court-of-justice-of-the-european-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Court of Justice
- **Short:** The EU's highest court, ruling on the meaning and validity of EU law.
- **Explanation:** Its rulings bind member states and their courts, which sometimes brings it into tension with Germany's Constitutional Court.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### court society
<a id="court-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** höfische Gesellschaft
- **Short:** The world of princely courts, whose manners and French language set cultural norms.
- **Explanation:** After 1648 residence cities and their courts shaped taste and behaviour; French was the language of courts.
- **German:** höfische Gesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### courts-martial
<a id="courts-martial"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Military courts, used in WWI to enforce order at home.
- **Explanation:** Along with censorship and drafting strikers to the front, they were tools the military used to enforce its rule during the war.
- **German:** Standgerichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### COVID recovery fund
<a id="covid-recovery-fund"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 2020
- **Short:** 2020 EU fund financed by joint EU borrowing to recover from the pandemic.
- **Explanation:** Germany had long rejected common EU debt; accepting joint borrowing for this fund in 2020 was a notable shift.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### COVID-19 pandemic
<a id="covid-19-pandemic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2020–2022
- **Also written as:** pandemic · COVID
- **Short:** The coronavirus pandemic that disrupted German life in 2020–22.
- **Explanation:** The global coronavirus outbreak brought lockdowns and economic disruption. In response, Germany accepted joint EU borrowing for a recovery fund in 2020, a notable shift.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Craft code
<a id="craft-code"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1897
- **Short:** 1897 law re-creating chambers for skilled crafts and formalising apprenticeship training.
- **Explanation:** The KB places it on the path from medieval guild apprenticeship to today's dual vocational training, codified in the Vocational Training Act of 1969.
- **German:** Handwerkerschutzgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Crash of 1873
<a id="crash-of-1873"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1873
- **Also written as:** 1873 crash · crash of 1873
- **Short:** Stock-market collapse ending the post-unification boom.
- **Explanation:** It opened the long slowdown of 1873–c.1895 and a turn to protectionism.
- **German:** Gründerkrach
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Crimean War
<a id="crimean-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1853–1856
- **Short:** War of Britain, France and the Ottomans against Russia.
- **Explanation:** It weakened ties between Austria and Russia, opening room for Prussia.
- **German:** Krimkrieg
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Crimes against humanity
<a id="crimes-against-humanity"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** crimes against humanity
- **Short:** Legal term for mass atrocities against civilians, such as murder, extermination or persecution.
- **Explanation:** The Nuremberg trials established it as a charge in international law.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Crisis of classical modernity
<a id="crisis-of-classical-modernity"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Peukert's view of Weimar as a laboratory of modern society that fell into crisis.
- **Explanation:** The Nazis exploited the tensions of this modernity.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Crisis of the Third Century
<a id="crisis-of-the-third-century"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** c. 235–284 CE
- **Also written as:** 3rd-century crisis
- **Short:** Period of invasions, usurpations and inflation that weakened the Roman Empire in the 200s CE.
- **Explanation:** It weakened the frontier and invited raiding: the upper Limes was abandoned around 260, and the Alamanni settled in the southwest.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### critical philosophy
<a id="critical-philosophy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Critical philosophy
- **Short:** Kant's philosophy examining the powers and limits of human reason.
- **Explanation:** Its themes: autonomy of reason, the moral law, the limits of knowledge and the public use of reason.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### cuius regio, eius religio
<a id="cuius-regio-eius-religio"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** c. 1600
- **Short:** Latin: 'whose realm, his religion' — the ruler decides his territory's faith.
- **Explanation:** The formula sums up the 1555 Peace of Augsburg, but the treaty never used it: the jurist Joachim Stephani coined it around 1600.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### cult of the front soldier
<a id="cult-of-the-front-soldier"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Post-1918 glorification of the frontline soldiers' comradeship and sacrifice.
- **Explanation:** In the Weimar years veterans' and paramilitary leagues celebrated the front soldier as a model for the nation, feeding anti-republican militarism.
- **German:** Frontkämpfer-Kult
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Cultural code
<a id="cultural-code"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** cultural code
- **Short:** Volkov's term for antisemitism as a signal of anti-modern, anti-liberal attitudes.
- **Explanation:** Declaring oneself antisemitic signalled a wider rejection of modernity.
- **German:** kultureller Code
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### cultural nationalism
<a id="cultural-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Nationalism based on shared language and culture rather than a state.
- **Explanation:** Herder's ideas of peoples and languages later fed it.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### cultural sovereignty
<a id="cultural-sovereignty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The German states' authority over schools and culture, rather than the federal government's.
- **Explanation:** In the Federal Republic each Land runs its own school system, which the KB links to early tracking and to differences between states.
- **German:** Kulturhoheit der Länder
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### culture of military restraint
<a id="culture-of-military-restraint"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Kultur der Zurückhaltung
- **Short:** Post-war German reluctance to use military force, rooted in memory of Nazi crimes.
- **Explanation:** The 'Kultur der Zurückhaltung' shaped German foreign policy until 2022, contrasted with calls for Germany to take more 'responsibility'.
- **German:** Kultur der Zurückhaltung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### culture of restraint
<a id="culture-of-restraint"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Post-war German reluctance to use military force abroad.
- **Explanation:** It grew from the lessons of Nazism and militarism. Since Russia's full-scale invasion of Ukraine in 2022 and the 'Zeitenwende', Germany has been renegotiating it.
- **German:** Kultur der Zurückhaltung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### cumulative radicalisation
<a id="cumulative-radicalisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Mommsen's term for how competition drove Nazi policy to ever greater extremes.
- **Explanation:** Officials anticipating Hitler's wishes pushed policy further, especially on the 'Jewish question'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### curiae
<a id="curiae"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Roman town councils made up of local property owners.
- **Explanation:** The only form of representation ordinary provincials had: local councils of the propertied, not any voice at imperial level.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Currency reform
<a id="currency-reform"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1948
- **Also written as:** currency reform · currency reforms
- **Short:** Replacement of the Reichsmark by the Deutsche Mark in the Western zones on 20 June 1948.
- **Explanation:** With price decontrol, shops filled overnight; it triggered the Soviet counter-reform and the Berlin Blockade.
- **German:** Währungsreform
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Customs Parliament
<a id="customs-parliament"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1868–1870
- **Short:** Parliament of the customs union including southern states (1868).
- **Explanation:** Southern voters elected anti-Prussian majorities, showing unity was unpopular in the south before 1870.
- **German:** Zollparlament
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

## D

### D-Day
<a id="d-day"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 6 June 1944
- **Short:** Allied landings in Normandy, France, on 6 June 1944.
- **Explanation:** The Allied landings in Normandy, France, on 6 June 1944 opened a new front in the West and added to the pressures that brought Germany's defeat.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### DA
<a id="da"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1989–1990
- **Short:** Democratic Awakening, an East German reform party in the 1990 Alliance for Germany.
- **Explanation:** Demokratischer Aufbruch, founded in autumn 1989 from church opposition circles; it joined the Alliance for Germany and then merged into the CDU.
- **German:** Demokratischer Aufbruch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Danes
<a id="danes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Danish-speaking minority in Schleswig, on today's German–Danish border.
- **Explanation:** In 1848 the Frankfurt Assembly backed German claims to Schleswig against Danish ones; after 1864 and 1871 Danes in North Schleswig were a national minority in Prussia and the Empire, suspected in wartime. Today Danes in Schleswig-Holstein are a recognised minority.
- **German:** Dänen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Danish War
<a id="danish-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1864
- **Short:** 1864 war in which Prussia and Austria took Schleswig and Holstein from Denmark.
- **Explanation:** The first of Bismarck's three unification wars; joint administration of the duchies led to Austro-Prussian friction.
- **German:** Deutsch-Dänischer Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Darmstädter Bank
<a id="darmstadter-bank"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Early German joint-stock investment bank, founded 1853.
- **Explanation:** It helped finance industrial take-off.
- **German:** Bank für Handel und Industrie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Datscha
<a id="datscha"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Small weekend cottage or allotment garden plot, a GDR leisure refuge.
- **Explanation:** Russian-derived word for the garden plots and cabins where many East Germans spent free time — part of the retreat into private 'niches'.
- **German:** Datscha
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Dawes Plan
<a id="dawes-plan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1924
- **Short:** 1924 agreement restructuring German reparations, backed by American loans.
- **Explanation:** US loans financed recovery and reparations; their withdrawal from 1929 exposed Germany to the Depression.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### day of liberation
<a id="day-of-liberation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1985
- **Short:** Weizsäcker's 1985 description of 8 May 1945, the end of the war, as liberation from Nazism.
- **Explanation:** Phrase from President Richard von Weizsäcker's speech on the 40th anniversary of Germany's surrender. Presenting defeat as liberation was a landmark in West Germany's confrontation with its past.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### DBD
<a id="dbd"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1948–1990
- **Short:** Democratic Farmers' Party of Germany, a GDR bloc party for rural voters.
- **Explanation:** Bloc party set up to bind farmers to SED policy, including collectivisation.
- **German:** Demokratische Bauernpartei Deutschlands
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### DDP
<a id="ddp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** German Democratic Party: left-liberal and pro-republic.
- **Explanation:** Part of the original Weimar coalition, it collapsed electorally; many of its former voters went to the Nazis.
- **German:** Deutsche Demokratische Partei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### De Bello Gallico
<a id="de-bello-gallico"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Julius Caesar's own account of his war in Gaul (58–50 BCE).
- **Explanation:** In it Caesar used the label 'Germani' for peoples beyond the Rhine — partly to justify stopping his conquest there.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### De facto
<a id="de-facto"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** de facto
- **Short:** 'In fact' or 'in practice', as opposed to legally.
- **Explanation:** Used for Prussia's actual dominance of the Empire despite formal rules.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)

### Death camps
<a id="death-camps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** death camps
- **Short:** Camps built to murder people with gas, such as Treblinka and Auschwitz-Birkenau.
- **Explanation:** Also called extermination camps. Unlike concentration camps, their main purpose was killing deportees on arrival.
- **German:** Vernichtungslager
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Death marches
<a id="death-marches"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1944–1945
- **Also written as:** death marches
- **Short:** Forced marches of camp prisoners as Allied armies approached, 1944–45.
- **Explanation:** Many prisoners died from exhaustion, cold and shootings.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### death strip
<a id="death-strip"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Cleared, guarded zone between the Wall's inner and outer barriers.
- **Explanation:** Todesstreifen: a stretch of open ground lit, patrolled and watched from towers, where escapees could be seen and shot.
- **German:** Todesstreifen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### debt brake
<a id="debt-brake"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2009–
- **Also written as:** debt-brake
- **Short:** Constitutional rule (2009) strictly limiting federal and state government borrowing.
- **Explanation:** Written into the Basic Law in 2009, it restricts new public debt. The Constitutional Court enforced it in 2023, toppling the budget plans that broke the Scholz coalition; in 2025 defence spending and a €500 billion infrastructure fund were exempted.
- **German:** Schuldenbremse
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Decartelisation
<a id="decartelisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** decartelisation
- **Short:** Breaking up large business combines that had supported the Nazi war economy.
- **Explanation:** Added to the Allied 'four Ds' at Potsdam. A cartel is an agreement among firms to fix prices or share markets; the Allies saw giant concerns such as IG Farben as pillars of the Nazi war economy.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Defeatism
<a id="defeatism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** defeatism · defeatists
- **Short:** Expressing doubt about German victory; punishable by death in the war.
- **Explanation:** The regime used defeatism trials to terrorise Germans in the final years, and 'defeatists' were among the victims of end-phase crimes.
- **German:** Wehrkraftzersetzung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Defence levy
<a id="defence-levy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1913
- **Also written as:** defence levy
- **Short:** 1913 one-off tax on property to fund army expansion.
- **Explanation:** The first direct Reich tax on property.
- **German:** Wehrbeitrag
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Defenestration of Prague
<a id="defenestration-of-prague"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1618
- **Short:** Bohemian Protestant nobles threw royal officials out of a Prague castle window, starting revolt.
- **Explanation:** It marked the Bohemian nobles' revolt against their Habsburg king and began the Thirty Years' War. Prague is today the Czech capital.
- **German:** Prager Fenstersturz
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### defensive modernisation
<a id="defensive-modernisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Defensive modernisation
- **Short:** Wehler's term: reforms rulers adopted to survive outside pressure.
- **Explanation:** Historians agree the reforms responded to French pressure but debate how modern the results were.
- **German:** defensive Modernisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### deflation
<a id="deflation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** deflationary
- **Short:** Falling prices and wages; also a policy of forcing them down by cutting spending.
- **Explanation:** Brüning's deflationary policy cut wages, salaries and benefits and raised taxes. Whether he had an alternative is disputed (the Borchardt controversy).
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether Brüning had an alternative to deflation is debated.

### dehnbare Formelkompromisse
<a id="dehnbare-formelkompromisse"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Elastic formula compromises': deliberately vague wording that each side could read its own way.
- **Explanation:** Gotthard sees them as one secret of the Empire's longevity.
- **German:** dehnbare Formelkompromisse
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### deindustrialisation
<a id="deindustrialisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Decline of industry — factories, mines, shipyards — in a region or country.
- **Explanation:** After the 1973 oil shock West Germany saw coal, steel and shipbuilding shrink, especially in the Ruhr, contributing to unemployment of over 2 million in the 1980s.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### demagogues
<a id="demagogues"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Dates:** from 1819
- **Short:** Official label for liberal and national activists persecuted after 1819.
- **Explanation:** After the Carlsbad Decrees (1819), the German Confederation hunted so-called 'demagogues' — students, professors and writers who demanded national unity and a constitution. Many were watched, dismissed or imprisoned; the campaign is known as the persecution of demagogues.
- **German:** Demagogen (Demagogenverfolgung)
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### demesne
<a id="demesne"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Land a lord kept for his own use, worked by his peasants' labour services.
- **Explanation:** Unfree tenants could owe up to three days' work a week on it. Over the Middle Ages labour services were increasingly replaced by money rents.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### democracy history
<a id="democracy-history"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Viewing Weimar as part of Germany's democratic history, not just Nazism's prelude.
- **Explanation:** This framing, used by the Federal Agency for Civic Education (2021), stresses Weimar's social reform and democratic practice.
- **German:** Demokratiegeschichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Democratic nationalism
<a id="democratic-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Nationalism demanding popular sovereignty and a republic.
- **Explanation:** Represented by Büchner and the Baden radicals Hecker and Struve.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Democratic radicalism
<a id="democratic-radicalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** democratic radicalism
- **Short:** Current demanding popular sovereignty and a republic, going beyond liberal constitutionalism.
- **Explanation:** Represented by Büchner and the Baden radicals Hecker and Struve. In 1848 democrats split from liberals over the republic and social reform.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### democratic socialism
<a id="democratic-socialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Democratic socialism
- **Short:** Socialism combined with free elections and civil liberties.
- **Explanation:** Aim of many 1989 civil-rights activists who wanted to reform, not abolish, the GDR.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Demokratie Jetzt
<a id="demokratie-jetzt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1989–1991
- **Short:** 'Democracy Now': East German citizens' movement founded in September 1989.
- **Explanation:** Civil-rights group seeking democratic reform of the GDR; it joined Alliance 90 for the March 1990 election.
- **German:** Demokratie Jetzt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Denazification
<a id="denazification"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1945–1948
- **Also written as:** denazification
- **Short:** Allied effort to remove Nazis from public life and punish them, 1945–48.
- **Explanation:** Millions were screened. The West ended in mass rehabilitation; the East used it to replace elites. Its success is debated.
- **German:** Entnazifizierung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: success or failure of denazification.

### Denunciation
<a id="denunciation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** denunciation · denunciations
- **Short:** Reporting a neighbour or colleague to the authorities, often out of conviction or a grudge.
- **Explanation:** Citizens' tips about remarks, foreign radio or relationships with Jews fed the Gestapo. Gellately sees denunciation as central; Evans notes only about a quarter of Düsseldorf cases began that way.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: how central denunciation was (Gellately vs Evans).

### department principle
<a id="department-principle"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Constitutional rule that each minister runs their ministry independently.
- **Explanation:** Under Art. 65 of the Basic Law ('Ressortprinzip'), ministers lead their departments on their own responsibility within the Chancellor's guidelines, which spreads power within government.
- **German:** Ressortprinzip
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### deputy commanding generals
<a id="deputy-commanding-generals"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Dates:** 1914–1918
- **Short:** Regional army commanders who governed civilian life at home during WWI.
- **Explanation:** Under the state of siege they controlled press, assembly and food in their districts, showing how far the military ran domestic life.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Der Geschichtsunterricht
<a id="der-geschichtsunterricht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** c. 1820
- **Also written as:** The History Lesson
- **Short:** Painting by Georg Adam Schmidt: a father teaching his son history at home.
- **Explanation:** A quiet Biedermeier interior of the restoration years, when public politics was censored and political and national ideas were often discussed in the private, domestic sphere.
- **German:** Der Geschichtsunterricht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)

- **Basis:** KB

### Der Staat Hitlers
<a id="der-staat-hitlers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1969
- **Short:** Broszat's 1969 book 'The Hitler State'.
- **Explanation:** Showed the Nazi state as polycratic.
- **German:** Der Staat Hitlers
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Des Deutschen Vaterland
<a id="des-deutschen-vaterland"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1813
- **Short:** Arndt's song 'Where is the German's fatherland?' (1813).
- **Explanation:** Its question showed that nobody agreed where Germany ended. It spread through clubs and festivals as a national song.
- **German:** Des Deutschen Vaterland
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Destatis
<a id="destatis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Germany's Federal Statistical Office.
- **Explanation:** The official statistics agency, whose microcensus figures (for example 26.3% of the population with an immigration history in 2025) the KB rates as highly reliable.
- **German:** Statistisches Bundesamt
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### détente
<a id="detente"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** late 1960s–1970s
- **Also written as:** Détente
- **Short:** Easing of Cold War tensions through treaties, trade and contacts.
- **Explanation:** Period of East–West relaxation. For Germany it brought Ostpolitik, the Basic Treaty and the Helsinki Final Act — more travel, trade and information, which in the long run eroded the GDR's isolation.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### deutsch
<a id="deutsch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Deutsch
- **Short:** 'German'; the word first referred to a language, not to a people or nation.
- **Explanation:** It derives from theodiscus, a late 8th-century term for the vernacular east of the Romance-speaking zone. A 'German people' became visible only later — historians debate whether in the 10th, 11th or later centuries.
- **German:** deutsch
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: when a German identity emerged is debated.

### Deutsche Mark
<a id="deutsche-mark"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1948–2002
- **Also written as:** D-Mark · DM
- **Short:** West Germany's currency from 1948, extended to East Germany on 1 July 1990.
- **Explanation:** Introduced by the 1948 currency reform, it became a symbol of West German stability. The GDR sold political prisoners and took loans for it. The 1990 currency union brought the D-Mark to East Germans, converting wages at 1:1.
- **German:** Deutsche Mark
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Deutscher Hof
<a id="deutscher-hof"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** Democratic left club of the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** A proto-party of democrats favouring popular sovereignty, named after an inn.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Deutsches Historisches Museum
<a id="deutsches-historisches-museum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** DHM
- **Short:** The German Historical Museum in Berlin.
- **Explanation:** It holds objects such as Napoleon's hat captured after Waterloo; its online portal LeMO provides images used in this KB.
- **German:** Deutsches Historisches Museum

### DGB
<a id="dgb"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Short:** German Trade Union Confederation, the umbrella of West German unions.
- **Explanation:** The Deutscher Gewerkschaftsbund, the federation of the major trade unions, which negotiated with employers and was a pillar of West German corporatism and co-determination.
- **German:** Deutscher Gewerkschaftsbund

### Diakonie
<a id="diakonie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Protestant welfare organisation running care homes, hospitals and social services.
- **Explanation:** The Protestant counterpart to Caritas; church welfare associations are among the largest employers in Germany's welfare state.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### dictatorship of the proletariat
<a id="dictatorship-of-the-proletariat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Marxist idea of rule by the working class after a revolution.
- **Explanation:** The KPD and SED set it as their goal while rejecting 'bourgeois' parliamentary democracy; in the GDR it meant rule by the party.
- **German:** Diktatur des Proletariats
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Die Auflösung der Weimarer Republik
<a id="die-auflosung-der-weimarer-republik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1955
- **Short:** Bracher's 1955 book 'The Dissolution of the Weimar Republic'.
- **Explanation:** A classic structural account of Weimar's collapse.
- **German:** Die Auflösung der Weimarer Republik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)

### Die geglückte Demokratie
<a id="die-gegluckte-demokratie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2006
- **Short:** 'The Successful Democracy': Edgar Wolfrum's 2006 history of West Germany.
- **Explanation:** Book by historian Edgar Wolfrum presenting the Federal Republic as a democratic success story — the 'successful democracy' narrative challenged by more critical accounts.
- **German:** Die geglückte Demokratie

### Die Linke
<a id="die-linke"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 2007–
- **Also written as:** the Left · Left
- **Ambiguous forms:** Left (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Left-wing socialist party, strongest in the East, which won the Berlin election in 2026.
- **Explanation:** 'The Left', formed in 2007 from the PDS (successor to the GDR's ruling party) and western left-wingers. Anchored in the East, it lost members to the BSW in 2024 but won 8.8% in 2025 and came first in Berlin in 2026.
- **German:** Die Linke
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)

### Die Wacht am Rhein
<a id="die-wacht-am-rhein"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1840
- **Short:** 'The Watch on the Rhine', patriotic song of the 1840 Rhine crisis.
- **Explanation:** It expressed defiance against French claims and became one of the most popular German patriotic songs.
- **German:** Die Wacht am Rhein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Diet of Speyer
<a id="diet-of-speyer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1526 (and 1529)
- **Short:** Imperial assemblies at Speyer; 1526 let each estate handle religion itself, 1529 revoked it.
- **Explanation:** The 1526 decision let each estate act on religion as it could answer 'to God and the Emperor', in practice licensing princely church reforms. When the 1529 Diet revoked this, the evangelical estates protested — hence 'Protestants'.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Diet of Worms
<a id="diet-of-worms-1495"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1495
- **Short:** Imperial assembly of 1495 that enacted the Imperial Reform.
- **Explanation:** It proclaimed the Perpetual Public Peace and founded the Imperial Chamber Court.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Not to be confused with the Diet of Worms of 1521, where Luther was outlawed (early_modern/01_reformation.md).

### Diet of Worms
<a id="diet-of-worms-1521"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1521
- **Short:** Imperial assembly at Worms that declared Luther an outlaw in 1521.
- **Explanation:** Luther refused to recant before Charles V; the resulting Edict of Worms outlawed him, but his prince Frederick the Wise protected him.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Directorium
<a id="directorium"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1749
- **Short:** Central Habsburg administrative authority created in 1749.
- **Explanation:** Part of Maria Theresa's reforms centralising the administration of the Austrian and Bohemian lands.
- **German:** Directorium
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### dishonourable
<a id="dishonourable"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Trades such as executioners and skinners that carried social stigma.
- **Explanation:** Excluded from guilds and respectable society.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)

### dismantling
<a id="dismantling"></a>

- **Dates:** 1945–1953
- **Short:** Removal of factories and equipment by the Soviets from their zone as reparations.
- **Explanation:** The Soviet Union took industrial plants, rail tracks and output from eastern Germany as war reparations until 1953, heavily burdening the GDR's recovery.

### Displaced persons
<a id="displaced-persons"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** DPs
- **Short:** About 8–10 million former forced labourers and camp survivors in Germany in 1945.
- **Explanation:** Former forced labourers, prisoners of war and camp survivors who found themselves in Germany in 1945. Most were sent home; Jewish DPs remained in camps into the late 1940s and 1950s.

### District reform of 1872
<a id="district-reform-of-1872"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1872
- **Also written as:** 1872 district reform
- **Short:** Prussian reform that ended estate owners' manorial police powers.
- **Explanation:** Estate districts administered by their owners (Gutsbezirke) nevertheless survived until 1927.

### DNVP
<a id="dnvp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1918–1933
- **Short:** German National People's Party: nationalist-conservative and hostile to the republic.
- **Explanation:** Radicalised under Hugenberg from 1928, it allied with Hitler against the Young Plan and joined his 1933 cabinet. Many of its voters defected to the Nazis.

- **Explanation:** Scholarly online reference for key terms and debates in contemporary history, where Konrad Jarausch published his 2023 overview of characterisations of the GDR.
