### Donnersberg
<a id="donnersberg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** The radical left club of the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** The most left-wing of the Assembly's clubs, gathering radical democrats and republicans.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### doping
<a id="doping"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Use of banned drugs to improve athletic performance.
- **Explanation:** The GDR ran a secret state-sponsored doping programme to win Olympic medals, harming many athletes' health.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Doppelverdiener
<a id="doppelverdiener"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Double earners': Depression-era label attacking married women who held paid jobs.
- **Explanation:** Campaigns against 'double earners' pushed women out of work during the Depression, showing the limits of Weimar's advances for women.
- **German:** Doppelverdiener
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### double burden
<a id="double-burden"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Women carrying both full-time jobs and most housework and childcare.
- **Explanation:** Despite near-universal female employment in the GDR, household work remained mainly women's responsibility, and no woman ever became a full Politburo member.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### dpa
<a id="dpa"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Short:** German Press Agency, the main German news agency.
- **Explanation:** Deutsche Presse-Agentur. A dpa photographer's camera smashed during the autumn 1989 protests is now a museum object.
- **German:** Deutsche Presse-Agentur
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Drang nach Osten
<a id="drang-nach-osten"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Drive to the East': nationalist and Nazi framing of medieval eastern settlement.
- **Explanation:** The KB rejects it: settlement was often invited by Slavic rulers and produced mixed societies.
- **German:** Drang nach Osten
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Dresden historians' commission
<a id="dresden-historians-commission"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 2010
- **Short:** Commission that in 2010 put the Dresden bombing deaths at up to about 25,000.
- **Explanation:** It found 'no reliable evidence' for claims of 100,000 or more dead.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Driving the Soviets up the Wall
<a id="driving-the-soviets-up-the-wall"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2003
- **Short:** Hope Harrison's 2003 study of how Ulbricht pushed Moscow to build the Wall.
- **Explanation:** Book showing, from archives, that the East German leadership pressed the Soviets for the Wall, while Khrushchev decided.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### DSU
<a id="dsu"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1990–
- **Short:** German Social Union, a conservative East German party backed by the Bavarian CSU.
- **Explanation:** Party founded in January 1990 and part of the winning Alliance for Germany in the March 1990 election.
- **German:** Deutsche Soziale Union
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Dual State
<a id="dual-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** The Dual State
- **Short:** Fraenkel's model: normal law for everyday matters beside a lawless police sphere.
- **Explanation:** It explains why the regime seemed 'normal' to many while terror operated outside the law.
- **German:** Doppelstaat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### dual vocational training
<a id="dual-vocational-training"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Dual vocational training · dual system · dual training system · dual training
- **Short:** German apprenticeship system combining work in a firm with classes at vocational school.
- **Explanation:** Rooted in guild apprenticeships and regulated by the 1969 Vocational Training Act. Respected internationally, it now faces fewer applicants and a shortage of skilled workers.
- **German:** duale Berufsausbildung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### duke
<a id="duke"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** dukes
- **Short:** High-ranking regional ruler, above counts.
- **Explanation:** In the 9th–10th centuries dukes grew strong by organising defence against raids. They elected the East Frankish kings, and dukes were among the great princes of the Empire.
- **German:** Herzog
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### durchherrschte Gesellschaft
<a id="durchherrschte-gesellschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** 'Thoroughly ruled society': the GDR as penetrated by the party, but never fully controlled.
- **Explanation:** Concept of Jürgen Kocka and Alf Lüdtke: the SED reached into every sphere of life, yet society retained spaces the party did not control.
- **German:** durchherrschte Gesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: one of several competing characterisations of the GDR.

### Dutch Revolt
<a id="dutch-revolt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1568–1648
- **Short:** The Netherlands' long war of independence against Habsburg Spain.
- **Explanation:** One of the European conflicts that overlapped with the Thirty Years' War.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### DVP
<a id="dvp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1918–1933
- **Short:** German People's Party: right-liberal, business-friendly; Stresemann's party.
- **Explanation:** It joined bourgeois coalitions and the grand coalition, then collapsed electorally as its voters moved to the Nazis.
- **German:** Deutsche Volkspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

## E

### early bourgeois revolution
<a id="early-bourgeois-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Marxist label for the Reformation and Peasants' War as a first middle-class revolution.
- **Explanation:** Developed from Engels by East German historians; Western historians preferred Blickle's 'revolution of the common man'.
- **German:** frühbürgerliche Revolution
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### early tracking
<a id="early-tracking"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** tracked schooling · Early tracking
- **Short:** Sorting children into different secondary school types at around age 10.
- **Explanation:** West German Länder divided pupils early among school types. The KB links this to Germany's strong dependence of school success on parental background, highlighted by the 2001 PISA shock.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### EASY
<a id="easy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** German system registering asylum seekers on arrival and distributing them among the states.
- **Explanation:** The initial registration and distribution system for asylum seekers. In 2015 its registrations exceeded the corrected figure of about 890,000 actual arrivals, because some people were counted twice or moved on.
- **German:** EASY (Erstverteilung der Asylbegehrenden)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ebert–Groener pact
<a id="ebert-groener-pact"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 10 November 1918
- **Short:** Deal between the new government and the army against the radical left.
- **Explanation:** The army supported Ebert's government; in return the government preserved the old officer corps — one reason why conservative elites survived into the republic.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ecclesiastical reservation
<a id="ecclesiastical-reservation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1555
- **Short:** Rule that a Catholic prince-bishop who turned Protestant had to resign his territory.
- **Explanation:** Part of the Peace of Augsburg, meant to stop church states from becoming Protestant. Protestants contested it, and it remained an unresolved issue feeding the 1618 crisis.
- **German:** geistlicher Vorbehalt
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### ecological inference
<a id="ecological-inference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Statistical method estimating individual voting from district-level results.
- **Explanation:** Falter used it to estimate who voted Nazi; findings are therefore estimates, e.g. that about 40% of Nazi voters came from working-class households.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Edelweiss Pirates
<a id="edelweiss-pirates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Working-class youth groups who defied and clashed with the Hitler Youth.
- **Explanation:** Active especially in western German cities, they rejected regimented Nazi youth life. Some members were publicly hanged in Cologne in 1944.
- **German:** Edelweißpiraten
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Edict of Potsdam
<a id="edict-of-potsdam"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1685
- **Short:** 1685 decree by Brandenburg's Great Elector inviting persecuted French Protestants to settle.
- **Explanation:** Part of Brandenburg-Prussia's policy of religious toleration to attract immigrants.
- **German:** Edikt von Potsdam
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Edict of Restitution
<a id="edict-of-restitution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1629
- **Short:** Emperor Ferdinand II's decree ordering Protestants to return church lands secularised since 1552.
- **Explanation:** It marked Habsburg overreach at the height of imperial power and alarmed even Catholic princes, prolonging the war.
- **German:** Restitutionsedikt
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Edict of Worms
<a id="edict-of-worms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1521
- **Short:** Imperial decree of 1521 outlawing Luther and banning his writings.
- **Explanation:** It could not be enforced because Luther's own prince protected him — an early sign of the emperor's limited power over the princes.
- **German:** Wormser Edikt
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ehrbarkeit
<a id="ehrbarkeit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Württemberg's non-noble elite of officials and leading townsmen who dominated its estates.
- **Explanation:** Their presence gave Württemberg's territorial assembly unusual weight — an exception to the rule that only nobles and towns were represented.
- **German:** Ehrbarkeit
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Eigen-Sinn
<a id="eigen-sinn"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** 'Self-will': Lüdtke's concept of how people pursued their own purposes within rules.
- **Explanation:** Alf Lüdtke's term for ordinary people's appropriation and reshaping of rules imposed on them, showing that dictatorship did not simply mould passive subjects.
- **German:** Eigen-Sinn
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Einsatzgruppen
<a id="einsatzgruppen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** mobile killing units
- **Short:** SS and police mobile killing units that shot Jews and others behind the Eastern front.
- **Explanation:** From June 1941 they murdered Jews in the occupied Soviet Union, first men and from August 1941 women and children too. Their commanders were tried at Nuremberg and in Ulm (1958).
- **German:** Einsatzgruppen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### elector
<a id="elector"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** Elector · electors · Electoral · electoral · electorate · Archbishop-Elector of Mainz
- **Ambiguous forms:** Electoral · electoral · electors (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** One of the few princes entitled to elect the Holy Roman Emperor.
- **Explanation:** Electors (e.g. of Saxony, Brandenburg, Mainz) formed the first college of the Imperial Diet; the Archbishop-Elector of Mainz chaired it. Their territories were called electorates.
- **German:** Kurfürst
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### electoral capitulation
<a id="electoral-capitulation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Also written as:** Wahlkapitulation
- **Short:** Agreement a new emperor had to sign before election, limiting his powers.
- **Explanation:** It specified what required the Diet's consent — one reason the emperor could not rule alone.
- **German:** Wahlkapitulation
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### electoral reform (2023)
<a id="electoral-reform-2023"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2023
- **Also written as:** Electoral reform (2023) · electoral reform
- **Short:** 2023 reform capping the Bundestag at 630 seats.
- **Explanation:** Passed to stop the parliament growing ever larger. In July 2024 the Constitutional Court upheld the size cap but restored the basic-mandate clause.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Eliminationist antisemitism
<a id="eliminationist-antisemitism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** eliminationist antisemitism
- **Short:** Goldhagen's term for a hatred of Jews aiming at their removal or destruction.
- **Explanation:** He claimed it pervaded German culture and explained why ordinary Germans killed; most historians find this too sweeping.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: Goldhagen vs Browning.

### Élysée Treaty
<a id="elysee-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1963
- **Short:** 1963 Franco-German friendship treaty sealing post-war reconciliation.
- **Explanation:** Signed by Adenauer and de Gaulle, it created regular consultations between the two governments and made the Franco-German relationship the core of European integration.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### emergency decree
<a id="emergency-decree"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** emergency decrees · presidential decree
- **Short:** A law issued by the president under Article 48, bypassing parliament.
- **Explanation:** Meant for crises, from 1930 decrees became the normal way of governing. In February 1933 the Reichstag Fire Decree used this power to suspend basic rights.
- **German:** Notverordnung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### emergency laws
<a id="emergency-laws"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1968
- **Short:** 1968 amendments to the Basic Law setting government powers in war and crisis.
- **Explanation:** Because of the history of the Prussian state of siege, Article 48 and the Reichstag Fire Decree, they were designed with parliamentary controls. Their passage was fiercely opposed by the protest movement of 1968.
- **German:** Notstandsgesetze
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ems Dispatch
<a id="ems-dispatch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1870
- **Short:** Telegram edited by Bismarck in 1870 to provoke France into war.
- **Explanation:** His shortened version made the exchange look like an insult; France declared war and the southern states joined Prussia.
- **German:** Emser Depesche
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Enabling Act
<a id="enabling-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 23 March 1933
- **Also written as:** Ermächtigungsgesetz
- **Short:** 1933 law letting Hitler's government make laws without parliament.
- **Explanation:** Passed 444 to 94 with only the SPD against, Communist deputies excluded and the SA occupying the building. It gave dictatorship a legal façade and was renewed in 1937, 1939 and 1943.
- **German:** Ermächtigungsgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Encirclement
<a id="encirclement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** encirclement
- **Short:** German fear of being surrounded by hostile powers before 1914.
- **Explanation:** Encirclement anxieties shaped foreign policy.
- **German:** Einkreisung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Endspiel
<a id="endspiel"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2009
- **Short:** 'Endgame': Ilko-Sascha Kowalczuk's 2009 history of the 1989 revolution in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Book arguing that 1989 was a revolution driven by citizens' agency rather than a mere collapse.
- **German:** Endspiel. Die Revolution von 1989 in der DDR
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Enemies of the Reich
<a id="enemies-of-the-reich"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** enemies of the Reich
- **Short:** Label for groups treated as disloyal in the Empire: Catholics, socialists, Poles.
- **Explanation:** Governments rallied support against them — part of 'negative integration'.
- **German:** Reichsfeinde
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### energy transition
<a id="energy-transition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Germany's shift from fossil and nuclear power to renewable energy.
- **Explanation:** The 'Energiewende' is listed in the KB as a source of pressure on the economy and politics, especially after Russian gas imports ended in 2022.
- **German:** Energiewende
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### enfeoffment
<a id="enfeoffment"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Formal grant of land or rights by an overlord, here the emperor, to a vassal.
- **Explanation:** Imperial enfeoffment was one way princes acquired or confirmed their territories.
- **German:** Belehnung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### enlightened absolutism
<a id="enlightened-absolutism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 18th c.
- **Also written as:** Enlightened absolutism
- **Short:** Reform from above for the common good by monarchs claiming full power, without participation.
- **Explanation:** Frederick II and Joseph II are the classic examples: legal codes, less torture, toleration and schools, but no political rights for subjects.
- **German:** aufgeklärter Absolutismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Enlightenment
<a id="enlightenment"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** c. 1700–1800
- **Also written as:** Aufklärung
- **Short:** 18th-century movement for reason, reform, toleration and public debate.
- **Explanation:** In Germany it was more state-friendly than in France, centred on universities, parsonages, reading societies and journals, and often pursued through reforming princes rather than against them.
- **German:** Aufklärung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Entente
<a id="entente"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1904–1918
- **Short:** The alliance of France, Britain and Russia that fought Germany in the First World War.
- **Explanation:** German war nationalism in 1914–18 was directed against it; nationalists had earlier described its formation as the 'encirclement' of Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Equalisation of Burdens Act
<a id="equalisation-of-burdens-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1952
- **Also written as:** Lastenausgleich
- **Short:** 1952 law sharing war losses by taxing surviving property to compensate expellees and bombed-out people.
- **Explanation:** West German law (Lastenausgleich) that levied a charge on property that had survived the war and paid compensation to those who had lost everything, above all the expellees. It helped integrate millions of newcomers.
- **German:** Lastenausgleich
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Equality Act
<a id="equality-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1957
- **Short:** 1957 West German law on equal rights for women that still kept the 'housewife marriage'.
- **Explanation:** Law (Gleichberechtigungsgesetz) passed by the Bundestag in 1957 to adapt civil law to the Basic Law's equality clause. It removed some husbands' rights but still allowed wives to work only if compatible with family duties.
- **German:** Gleichberechtigungsgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### equestrian rank
<a id="equestrian-rank"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Roman upper-class order ('knights') just below the senators.
- **Explanation:** Arminius held this rank — showing he was a Roman-trained officer and citizen, not an outsider fighting for 'Germany'.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### equivalent living conditions
<a id="equivalent-living-conditions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** gleichwertige Lebensverhältnisse
- **Short:** Constitutional goal of comparable living standards in all parts of Germany.
- **Explanation:** A phrase from Art. 72 of the Basic Law guiding federal policy. In practice, gaps between East and West in wages, wealth and representation in national elites have persisted for more than three decades.
- **German:** gleichwertige Lebensverhältnisse
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Erbuntertänigkeit
<a id="erbuntertanigkeit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** to 1810 (Prussia)
- **Also written as:** Erbuntertanen · hereditary subjection
- **Short:** 'Hereditary subjection': peasants bound to a lord by birth, East Elbian serfdom.
- **Explanation:** Hereditary subjects owed labour services and needed the lord's consent to marry or leave. Prussia's October Edict of 1807 abolished it from 1810.
- **German:** Erbuntertänigkeit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Erfurt Programme
<a id="erfurt-programme"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1891
- **Short:** SPD programme of 1891: Marxist theory plus practical reform demands.
- **Explanation:** Demanded universal suffrage including women, 8-hour day, progressive taxation and secular schools. It made the SPD the first major party to demand women's vote.
- **German:** Erfurter Programm
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Ersatz
<a id="ersatz"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** German for 'substitute': inferior replacement goods, especially food, in wartime.
- **Explanation:** Queues, ration cards and Ersatz foods were part of everyday hunger on the WWI home front.
- **German:** Ersatz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Erweiterte Oberschule
<a id="erweiterte-oberschule"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** The GDR's upper secondary school leading to the university entrance exam.
- **Explanation:** Admission depended on class origin and political loyalty, so children of church families and dissenters were often excluded.
- **German:** Erweiterte Oberschule (EOS)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### escheat
<a id="escheat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** escheated
- **Short:** Return of a fief to the lord when the holder's line died out.
- **Explanation:** German kings usually re-granted escheated fiefs.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### estate society
<a id="estate-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Estate society · estates order
- **Short:** Society divided into legally defined orders — nobility, clergy, burghers, peasants — each with own rights.
- **Explanation:** Rank came by birth and brought different rights, duties and taxes. It survived the Reformation, the Thirty Years' War and the Enlightenment largely intact.
- **German:** Ständegesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### etatism
<a id="etatism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** etatist · Etatismus
- **Short:** Explaining history mainly from the viewpoint and power of the state.
- **Explanation:** Critics of the confessionalisation thesis call it 'etatist' because it credits the state with discipline that often came from communities.
- **German:** Etatismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### eternity clause
<a id="eternity-clause"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Eternity clause
- **Short:** Basic Law article making human dignity, democracy, federalism and the rule of law unamendable.
- **Explanation:** Article 79(3) of the Basic Law. Because the Nazis had hollowed out the Weimar constitution legally (the Enabling Act), these core principles cannot be removed even by a constitutional amendment.
- **German:** Ewigkeitsklausel
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ethnic nationalism
<a id="ethnic-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** ethnic
- **Ambiguous forms:** ethnic (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Idea that the nation is defined by ancestry and ethnicity.
- **Explanation:** The belief that only people of a given descent truly belong to the nation. The KB lists it among the ideological explanations for radical-right support in eastern Germany, alongside anti-migrant attitudes.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ethnogenesis
<a id="ethnogenesis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The process by which a people or ethnic group comes into being.
- **Explanation:** The Vienna School argued that early medieval peoples formed around small elite groups carrying shared traditions; the Toronto School argues even this concedes too much. Either way, 'peoples' were not ancient, unchanging tribes.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: Vienna School vs Toronto School.

### EU Charter of Fundamental Rights
<a id="eu-charter-of-fundamental-rights"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Also written as:** EU Charter
- **Short:** The EU's catalogue of basic rights, binding since 2009.
- **Explanation:** Applies alongside the Basic Law and the European Convention on Human Rights when EU law is at stake.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### EU eastern enlargement
<a id="eu-eastern-enlargement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2004
- **Also written as:** Enlargement to the East
- **Short:** The 2004 entry of Poland and other Central and Eastern European states into the EU.
- **Explanation:** Germany supported it, moving it from the EU's eastern edge to its centre and opening its labour market to new member states.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### euro
<a id="euro"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1999–
- **Short:** The EU's common currency, introduced 1999 and as cash in 2002, replacing the D-Mark.
- **Explanation:** Germany gave up the powerful D-Mark partly to reassure neighbours about unification. The euro binds Germany's economy to the rest of the eurozone, including its crises.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Central Bank
<a id="european-central-bank"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1998–
- **Also written as:** ECB
- **Short:** Central bank setting monetary policy (interest rates, money supply) for the eurozone.
- **Explanation:** Based in Frankfurt. The Bundesbank is part of its system and carries weight in it; the German Constitutional Court challenged its bond-buying programme in 2020.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Coal and Steel Community
<a id="european-coal-and-steel-community"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1951–2002
- **Also written as:** ECSC
- **Short:** 1951 community pooling West German, French and other coal and steel industries.
- **Explanation:** The first step of European integration: putting war-making industries under joint control made war between France and Germany practically impossible.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Commission
<a id="european-commission"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Commission
- **Ambiguous forms:** Commission (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The EU's executive, which proposes laws and enforces EU rules.
- **Explanation:** Led since 2019 by German politician Ursula von der Leyen. It can open infringement procedures against member states that break EU law.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Communities
<a id="european-communities"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1967–1993
- **Short:** Umbrella name for the coal and steel community, the EEC and Euratom.
- **Explanation:** The joined European institutions of the Cold War era, predecessors of the European Union, in which West Germany was integrated.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Convention on Human Rights
<a id="european-convention-on-human-rights"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1950–
- **Short:** Council of Europe treaty protecting human rights, enforced by a court in Strasbourg.
- **Explanation:** A treaty of European states (1950) whose rights apply in Germany alongside the Basic Law and the EU Charter.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Council
<a id="european-council"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Summit of EU heads of state and government that sets the EU's direction.
- **Explanation:** Where member-state leaders, including the German Chancellor, make the big political decisions of the EU.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Economic Community
<a id="european-economic-community"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1957–1993
- **Also written as:** EEC · EC
- **Ambiguous forms:** EC (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Common market founded 1957 by six states including West Germany; forerunner of the EU.
- **Explanation:** Created by the Treaties of Rome. Later known as the European Community (EC), it deepened economic integration before the Maastricht Treaty created the EU.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European integration
<a id="european-integration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1950s–
- **Short:** Step-by-step merging of European states' economies and policies into shared institutions.
- **Explanation:** Post-war West Germany's answer to the 'German question': binding itself into the ECSC, EEC and later the EU and euro to regain trust and sovereignty. Since 2022, debate has turned to leadership in European defence.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Monetary System
<a id="european-monetary-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1979–1998
- **Also written as:** EMS
- **Short:** 1979 system limiting exchange-rate swings between European currencies; forerunner of the euro.
- **Explanation:** Created by Helmut Schmidt and Valéry Giscard d'Estaing. It tied European currencies together, with the strong D-Mark at its centre.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Parliament
<a id="european-parliament"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** EP
- **Short:** Directly elected EU parliament; Germany has the largest delegation, 96 seats.
- **Explanation:** Co-decides EU laws with the Council. Germans can vote in its elections from age 16 since 2024.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### European Union
<a id="european-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1993–
- **Also written as:** EU
- **Short:** Political and economic union of 27 European states, in which Germany is the largest member.
- **Explanation:** Grown from the post-war communities for coal, steel and trade, it became the EU with the Maastricht Treaty (1992). Membership binds Germany to shared law and the euro; Germany is its largest net contributor and pivotal economic power.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Europeanism
<a id="europeanism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Commitment to European integration as a core of German identity and policy.
- **Explanation:** The conviction that Germany's interests are best served inside the EU. It is a pillar of post-war German policy, but tensions between solidarity and fiscal orthodoxy persist and it is contested by the AfD and BSW.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Euroscepticism
<a id="euroscepticism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Eurosceptic · eurosceptic
- **Short:** Opposition to or distrust of European integration.
- **Explanation:** Grew in Germany with the eurozone crisis, refugee policy and the energy shock after 2022, represented above all by the AfD and BSW.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### eurozone
<a id="eurozone"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** The group of EU countries that use the euro.
- **Explanation:** Sharing one currency and one monetary policy. Its architecture and debt rules remain a source of tension between creditor states like Germany and others.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### eurozone crisis
<a id="eurozone-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2010–2012
- **Also written as:** euro crisis · euro crises
- **Short:** 2010–12 debt crisis in which several euro countries needed rescue loans.
- **Explanation:** Germany, as the main creditor, set strict conditions and was criticised for demanding austerity. The crisis boosted Euroscepticism and the founding of the AfD.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated: German responsibility for eurozone austerity.

### Euthanasia programme
<a id="euthanasia-programme"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1939–1945
- **Also written as:** euthanasia · T4
- **Short:** Nazi murder of disabled people from 1939, disguised as 'mercy killing'.
- **Explanation:** Under the code name T4, about 70,000 people were gassed by August 1941; killings then continued by other means. In total 250,000–300,000 disabled people were murdered. Staff and methods were later used in the death camps.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Évian Conference
<a id="evian-conference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1938
- **Also written as:** Évian
- **Short:** 1938 international meeting on Jewish refugees where most countries refused to take more.
- **Explanation:** Held at Évian in France, it showed that Jews forced to emigrate from Germany faced closed doors elsewhere.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### EVZ Foundation
<a id="evz-foundation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 2000
- **Also written as:** EVZ
- **Short:** German foundation set up in 2000 to compensate former Nazi forced labourers.
- **Explanation:** Its full name is 'Remembrance, Responsibility and Future'. It came more than half a century after the war.
- **German:** Stiftung Erinnerung, Verantwortung und Zukunft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### excise
<a id="excise"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** A tax on goods (e.g. beer, meat, grain) collected when sold or brought into towns.
- **Explanation:** Prussia's rulers used town excise to raise money without asking the estates.
- **German:** Akzise
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### excise tax
<a id="excise-tax"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Excise taxes
- **Short:** Tax on goods sold, such as beer or wine.
- **Explanation:** A main revenue of imperial cities.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### excommunication
<a id="excommunication"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Exclusion from the Church's sacraments by a pope or bishop.
- **Explanation:** A papal weapon against kings: Henry IV's excommunication led to his penance at Canossa in 1077.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### exit and voice
<a id="exit-and-voice"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** exit · voice
- **Ambiguous forms:** exit · voice (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Hirschman's two responses to decline: leaving (exit) or protesting (voice).
- **Explanation:** Applied to 1989: East Germans' mass flight (exit) and demonstrations (voice) were distinct responses that reinforced each other to bring down the regime.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### exit applications
<a id="exit-applications"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Requests by GDR citizens for permission to emigrate permanently to the West.
- **Explanation:** Ausreiseanträge, often punished with discrimination. Over 100,000 were pending in 1989 (estimate); applicants cited the Helsinki Final Act's human-rights commitments.
- **German:** Ausreiseantrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### exodus
<a id="exodus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** Exodus
- **Short:** The mass flight of East Germans to the West, before 1961 and again in 1989.
- **Explanation:** About 2.7–3 million people left before the Wall was built. In 1989, tens of thousands fled via Hungary and West German embassies in Prague and Warsaw, destabilising the GDR.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### export model
<a id="export-model"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Germany's reliance on exporting cars, machinery and chemicals for growth.
- **Explanation:** Germany's economy depends on selling manufactured goods abroad, above all in the EU single market and China, and long relied on cheap Russian energy. Both dependencies became vulnerabilities in the 2020s.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### extremism of the centre
<a id="extremism-of-the-centre"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Lipset's theory that Nazism was a radicalised middle-class movement.
- **Explanation:** A class-based explanation later challenged by research showing the Nazis drew voters from all classes.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Exzellenzinitiative
<a id="exzellenzinitiative"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 2005–
- **Short:** 'Excellence Initiative': federal–state programme funding selected top universities and research clusters.
- **Explanation:** It gave extra money to a few universities chosen in competition, a break with the tradition of treating German universities as roughly equal. It has continued as the Excellence Strategy.
- **German:** Exzellenzinitiative
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

## F

### fallow
<a id="fallow"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Farmland left unplanted for a season to recover.
- **Explanation:** One of the three fields in the medieval three-field rotation, alongside winter and summer grain.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### fascism
<a id="fascism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Authoritarian, ultranationalist ideology and regime type of the interwar period.
- **Explanation:** Marxist theories saw fascism as capital's instrument; 'fascism' theories of Nazism compete with totalitarianism theory.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/political_power.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Fatherland Party
<a id="fatherland-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1917–1918
- **Short:** Nationalist mass organisation founded in 1917 to push for a victorious peace with annexations.
- **Explanation:** It claimed over a million members and opposed the Reichstag's Peace Resolution.
- **German:** Deutsche Vaterlandspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### FDGB
<a id="fdgb"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1945–1990
- **Also written as:** Free German Trade Union Federation
- **Short:** The GDR's official trade union federation, which also distributed holidays.
- **Explanation:** The Freier Deutscher Gewerkschaftsbund: not an independent union but an SED-led mass organisation that ran workers' holiday homes and social benefits.
- **German:** Freier Deutscher Gewerkschaftsbund
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### FDJ
<a id="fdj"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1946–
- **Also written as:** Free German Youth
- **Short:** Free German Youth, the SED's official youth organisation.
- **Explanation:** The Freie Deutsche Jugend, to which most young East Germans belonged; it provided ideological education, leisure activities and recruitment for the party.
- **German:** Freie Deutsche Jugend
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Act
<a id="federal-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1815
- **Short:** Founding treaty of the German Confederation (1815).
- **Explanation:** It set up the Confederation as a defensive league. Its Article 13 promised each state a 'constitution of estates', which only the southern states implemented.
- **German:** Bundesakte
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Federal Assembly
<a id="federal-assembly"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1815–1866
- **Also written as:** Bundesversammlung
- **Short:** Council of state envoys in Frankfurt; the German Confederation's only organ.
- **Explanation:** The Federal Assembly (Bundesversammlung, also called Bundestag) was a meeting of diplomats representing the member governments, chaired by Austria. It had no elected members; in 1848 it was bypassed by the National Assembly.
- **German:** Bundesversammlung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Chancellor
<a id="federal-chancellor"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** Chancellor
- **Ambiguous forms:** Chancellor (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Germany's head of government, elected by the Bundestag.
- **Explanation:** Formally sets the guidelines of policy, but in practice depends on coalition partners and the Bundestag majority. Protected against sudden removal by the constructive vote of no confidence.
- **German:** Bundeskanzler(in)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Compensation Law
<a id="federal-compensation-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1953/1956
- **Short:** West German law (1953/56) compensating victims of Nazi persecution.
- **Explanation:** Restitution was partial and slow; many victim groups were long excluded.
- **German:** Bundesentschädigungsgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Constitutional Court
<a id="federal-constitutional-court"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1951–
- **Also written as:** Constitutional Court · BVerfG
- **Short:** Germany's highest court for constitutional questions, in Karlsruhe; can annul laws and ban parties.
- **Explanation:** Court created under the Basic Law that became one of the world's most influential. It can strike down laws, hear individual complaints and ban anti-constitutional parties. It shaped abortion law (1975), party finance, co-determination and data protection.
- **German:** Bundesverfassungsgericht
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Convention
<a id="federal-convention"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Short:** Special assembly that elects the German Federal President.
- **Explanation:** Assembly made up of all members of the Bundestag plus an equal number of delegates from the state parliaments. It meets only to elect the Federal President, making the head of state indirectly elected.
- **German:** Bundesversammlung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal Government
<a id="federal-government"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** German Federal Government
- **Short:** Today's German national government (Bundesregierung).
- **Explanation:** It recognised the Herero and Nama genocide in 2021 and estimates about 100,000 deaths.
- **German:** Bundesregierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Federal nationalism
<a id="federal-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** federative national consciousness · federal nationalism
- **Short:** Langewiesche's idea that German national identity long combined with loyalty to one's own state.
- **Explanation:** Gymnastics and singing movements celebrated the nation without demanding the abolition of the individual states.
- **German:** föderatives Nationalbewusstsein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Federal Office for the Protection of the Constitution
<a id="federal-office-for-the-protection-of-the-constitution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Verfassungsschutz
- **Short:** Germany's domestic intelligence service, which monitors extremist organisations.
- **Explanation:** The 'Verfassungsschutz' watches groups considered threats to the democratic order, subject to court review. It classified the AfD as a suspected extremist case; its 2025 upgrade to 'confirmed right-wing extremist' was provisionally blocked by the Administrative Court of Cologne in 2026.
- **German:** Bundesamt für Verfassungsschutz
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Federal President
<a id="federal-president"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Also written as:** President
- **Ambiguous forms:** President (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Germany's largely ceremonial head of state, elected by the Federal Convention.
- **Explanation:** Head of state under the Basic Law. Unlike the powerful Weimar president, he or she is indirectly elected and has no emergency powers; the role is mostly representative, though speeches can matter (e.g. Weizsäcker's 1985 'day of liberation').
- **German:** Bundespräsident
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### federalism
<a id="federalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Federalism
- **Short:** System in which power is divided between a central government and member states.
- **Explanation:** In the Federal Republic the Länder (states) run schools, police and much administration and share in federal law-making through the Bundesrat. The Basic Law's eternity clause protects federalism, a long German tradition.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### federalism reform (2006)
<a id="federalism-reform-2006"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2006
- **Also written as:** 2006 federalism reform
- **Short:** 2006 constitutional reform that reduced the number of laws needing Bundesrat consent.
- **Explanation:** Aimed at untangling federal and state powers and making government faster. Debates on reforming federalism and bureaucracy continue.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Feel-good dictatorship
<a id="feel-good-dictatorship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Gefälligkeitsdiktatur · feel-good dictatorship
- **Short:** Götz Aly's claim that Nazi rule bought Germans' loyalty with plunder-funded benefits.
- **Explanation:** Aly argued loot from Europe funded living standards and spared ordinary Germans taxes. Tooze showed most war costs were paid by Germans; the strong form is rejected.
- **German:** Gefälligkeitsdiktatur
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** REJECTED in its strong form (Tooze).

### feud
<a id="feud"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Feud
- **Short:** Private violence between families or nobles to settle wrongs, legally recognised until 1495.
- **Explanation:** In early societies, feud and compensation payments, backed by kin, replaced courts. In the medieval Empire, feud remained a legitimate legal means for nobles until the Perpetual Public Peace of 1495 banned it.
- **German:** Fehde
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Feudal dues
<a id="feudal-dues"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** feudal dues · feudal burdens
- **Short:** Payments and labour peasants owed their lords under the old agrarian order.
- **Explanation:** Many were abolished or made redeemable in spring 1848, after which much of the countryside withdrew from the revolution.
- **German:** Feudallasten
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### feudalisation of the bourgeoisie
<a id="feudalisation-of-the-bourgeoisie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Feudalisation of the bourgeoisie
- **Short:** Sonderweg claim that Imperial Germany's middle class copied aristocratic values instead of challenging nobles.
- **Explanation:** Supporters pointed to bourgeois men prizing reserve-officer rank and duelling. Blackbourn and Eley countered that the bourgeoisie actually shaped modern Germany culturally and economically — a 'silent bourgeois revolution'.
- **German:** Feudalisierung des Bürgertums
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: challenged by Blackbourn and Eley.

### feudalism
<a id="feudalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Feudalism · feudal pyramid · feudal
- **Ambiguous forms:** feudal (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Classic model of society built on lords granting fiefs to vassals for service.
- **Explanation:** Susan Reynolds (1994) argued the model distorts the evidence; German historians prefer Lehnswesen (fief system) and Grundherrschaft (manorial lordship).
- **German:** Feudalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: usefulness of the concept is debated.

### fibula
<a id="fibula"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** Fibel · Fibula
- **Short:** Brooch used to fasten a cloak; a common grave find.
- **Explanation:** Brooches like the Frankish bow fibula (German: Fibel) are much of what we know about early Germanic-speaking elites: they show status, craft and contact with the Roman world.
- **German:** Fibel
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### fief
<a id="fief"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** fiefs
- **Short:** Land or rights granted by a lord to a vassal in return for loyalty and service.
- **Explanation:** In Germany fiefs became hereditary and princes treated them as property; kings usually re-granted fiefs that fell back to them, so territorial power stayed with the princes.
- **German:** Lehen
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Final Solution
<a id="final-solution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Nazi euphemism for the planned murder of all European Jews.
- **Explanation:** The Wannsee Conference coordinated its implementation in January 1942.
- **German:** Endlösung der Judenfrage
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### First Crusade
<a id="first-crusade"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1096
- **Also written as:** crusaders
- **Short:** Crusade of 1096 whose participants massacred Jews in Speyer, Worms and Mainz.
- **Explanation:** On their way east in 1096, crusader bands massacred the Jewish communities of Speyer, Worms and Mainz — the first great massacres of Jews in medieval Germany, which destroyed or scattered some of the most important Jewish communities in Europe.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### first servant of the state
<a id="first-servant-of-the-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Frederick II's description of the ruler — a secular justification of monarchy.
- **Explanation:** The king justified his rule by service to the state rather than by divine right.
- **German:** erster Diener des Staates
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### First World War
<a id="first-world-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1914–1918
- **Also written as:** WWI
- **Short:** Global war that Germany lost; it ended the Empire.
- **Explanation:** It brought military dictatorship, hunger and defeat, and cost Germany its colonies.
- **German:** Erster Weltkrieg
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### fisc
<a id="fisc"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** The king's own estates and revenues.
- **Explanation:** Carolingian kings lived largely from these royal estates, plus plunder, tribute and tolls.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### fiscal equalisation
<a id="fiscal-equalisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Redistribution of tax revenue from richer to poorer German states.
- **Explanation:** A system that evens out differences in the Länder's finances so all can provide comparable services — part of the goal of 'equivalent living conditions'.
- **German:** Finanzausgleich
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### fiscal-military state
<a id="fiscal-military-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** fiscal-military
- **Short:** A state organised around raising taxes to pay for a permanent army.
- **Explanation:** Standing armies required permanent taxes, driving the growth of bureaucracies in Prussia, Austria and other territories.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Fischer controversy
<a id="fischer-controversy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1960s
- **Short:** Fierce 1960s debate sparked by Fritz Fischer's claim that Germany sought war in 1914.
- **Explanation:** It led to a broad consensus by the 1980s–90s that the German Reich bore a 'special responsibility' for WWI alongside other powers.
- **German:** Fischer-Kontroverse
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### five-percent threshold
<a id="five-percent-threshold"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1953–
- **Also written as:** 5% threshold
- **Short:** Rule that a party needs at least 5% of the party vote to enter the Bundestag.
- **Explanation:** Designed against the fragmentation of Weimar. Parties winning three constituency seats are exempt (the basic-mandate clause). In 2025 the BSW (4.97%) and FDP (4.3%) fell short.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### five-year plans
<a id="five-year-plans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Five-year plans
- **Short:** Central economic plans setting production targets for five years at a time.
- **Explanation:** Planning tool of Soviet-type economies, used in the GDR to direct all state enterprises.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### FKK
<a id="fkk"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Free body culture': naturism, popular on GDR beaches.
- **Explanation:** Freikörperkultur, nude bathing and recreation, which was widespread and tolerated in the GDR.
- **German:** Freikörperkultur
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Flak helpers
<a id="flak-helpers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** flak helpers
- **Short:** Schoolboys from the Hitler Youth who helped man anti-aircraft guns.
- **Explanation:** 'Flak' is the German abbreviation for anti-aircraft artillery.
- **German:** Flakhelfer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)

### Flight and expulsion
<a id="flight-and-expulsion"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1944–1950
- **Also written as:** flight and expulsion · expellees · Expellees
- **Short:** Flight and forced removal of about 12 million Germans from Eastern Europe, 1944–50.
- **Explanation:** Agreed in principle at Potsdam as 'orderly and humane', but rarely so. Deaths are estimated at 400,000 to 2 million. The expellees reshaped post-war society.
- **German:** Flucht und Vertreibung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Flight into war
<a id="flight-into-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** flight into war
- **Short:** Tim Mason's thesis that economic strains pushed Hitler into war in 1939.
- **Explanation:** Tim Mason argued that economic strains in the late 1930s pushed the regime into war in 1939. The thesis remains contested.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### flight tax
<a id="flight-tax"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Tax on emigrants' assets, used by the Nazis to strip Jews leaving Germany.
- **Explanation:** One of the instruments of Jewish expropriation.
- **German:** Reichsfluchtsteuer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)

### Flying courts-martial
<a id="flying-courts-martial"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1945
- **Also written as:** flying courts-martial
- **Short:** Mobile summary courts in 1945 that executed deserters and 'defeatists'.
- **Explanation:** They were part of the regime's end-phase terror against Germans.
- **German:** fliegende Standgerichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)

### FNZ-Online
<a id="fnz-online"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** University of Vienna online portal on early modern history, cited in this KB.
- **Explanation:** An academic web resource of the University of Vienna (FNZ = Frühe Neuzeit, 'early modern period'); the KB uses it, for example, on confessionalisation — the building of distinct Catholic, Lutheran and Reformed churches and identities.

### foederati
<a id="foederati"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Allied 'barbarian' groups fighting for Rome under treaty.
- **Explanation:** Late Rome increasingly relied on such allies. In the 5th century many of their leaders took over the provinces they had defended.

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Forced labour
<a id="forced-labour"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** forced labour · forced labourers · Forced labourers
- **Short:** About 13 million foreigners made to work in Germany during the war.
- **Explanation:** Civilians, prisoners of war and camp inmates worked in factories, farms and households. By August 1944 civilian forced workers were over a quarter of the workforce. Treatment followed a racial hierarchy.

- **Basis:** KB+K

### forced merger
<a id="forced-merger"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1946
- **Also written as:** Forced merger
- **Short:** April 1946 merger of the KPD and SPD into the SED in the Soviet zone.
- **Explanation:** Carried out under Soviet pressure, it created East Germany's ruling party. Social Democrats were then purged, and 'Social Democratism' became an offence.

### forced secularisation
<a id="forced-secularisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Term for the GDR's pressure that drove most East Germans out of the churches.
- **Explanation:** Church membership in the East fell from about 90% to 25–30% under an atheist state; the KB treats this as a German special case in debates on secularisation.
- **German:** Zwangssäkularisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
