### Foreign Office
<a id="foreign-office"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Germany's foreign ministry.
- **Explanation:** It pressed allied states to hand over their Jews. A 2010 study showed that many former Nazis worked there after the war.
- **German:** Auswärtiges Amt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Forty-Eighters
<a id="forty-eighters"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Revolutionaries of 1848 who emigrated, especially to the USA, after defeat.
- **Explanation:** Thousands left after the revolution failed, among them Carl Schurz.
- **German:** Achtundvierziger
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Four Ds
<a id="four-ds"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** four Ds
- **Short:** Allied aims for Germany: denazification, demilitarisation, democratisation, decentralisation.
- **Explanation:** Agreed at Potsdam in 1945 as the Allies' programme for Germany: remove Nazis, abolish the military, build democracy and spread power away from the centre. Decartelisation — breaking up big business combines — was added as a fifth aim.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Four-Power Agreement on Berlin
<a id="four-power-agreement-on-berlin"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1971
- **Short:** 1971 agreement of the four Allies securing West Berlin's links to West Germany.
- **Explanation:** Deal between the USA, Britain, France and the Soviet Union guaranteeing transit routes and ties between West Berlin and the Federal Republic, easing the Berlin question.
- **German:** Viermächteabkommen über Berlin
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Four-power rights
<a id="four-power-rights"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1945–1991
- **Also written as:** four-power status · Allied reserved rights
- **Short:** Rights over Berlin and Germany as a whole kept by the four victorious powers until 1990–91.
- **Explanation:** The USA, Britain, France and the Soviet Union retained legal responsibility for Berlin and 'Germany as a whole' after 1949. That is why West Berlin was not formally part of the Federal Republic. The Two-plus-Four Treaty ended these rights, giving Germany full sovereignty on 15 March 1991.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Four-Year Plan
<a id="four-year-plan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1936
- **Short:** Göring's 1936 programme to prepare the economy for war.
- **Explanation:** It aimed at autarky and war readiness, including synthetic fuel and rubber; the state directed investment while private ownership remained.
- **German:** Vierjahresplan
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Fragebogen
<a id="fragebogen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Also written as:** questionnaire
- **Short:** Denazification questionnaire every adult in the US zone had to fill in.
- **Explanation:** It asked about Nazi memberships and activities and was used to classify people.
- **German:** Fragebogen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frame of reference
<a id="frame-of-reference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** frame of reference
- **Short:** Neitzel and Welzer's term for soldiers' shared assumptions that made violence seem normal.
- **Explanation:** Based on secret recordings of German POWs, it shows how ordinary soldiers talked about killing.
- **German:** Referenzrahmen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Franco-German tandem
<a id="franco-german-tandem"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Franco-German relationship
- **Short:** Close partnership of France and Germany as the driving force of the EU.
- **Explanation:** Built on the Élysée Treaty of 1963. Germany rarely acts alone in Europe: its preferences usually depend on agreement with France.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Franco-Prussian War
<a id="franco-prussian-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1870–1871
- **Short:** War in which Prussia and the German states defeated France and completed unification.
- **Explanation:** The national war created emotional unity; about 45,000 Germans died. France lost Alsace-Lorraine and paid 5 billion francs.
- **German:** Deutsch-Französischer Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Frankfurt Auschwitz trial
<a id="frankfurt-auschwitz-trial"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1963–1965
- **Also written as:** Auschwitz trials · Auschwitz trial
- **Short:** West German trial (1963–65) of Auschwitz camp staff, driven by prosecutor Fritz Bauer.
- **Explanation:** Trial in Frankfurt of former SS members and staff of the Auschwitz camp. Brought to court by Hessian prosecutor Fritz Bauer, it exposed the workings of the camp to the West German public and marked a turn from silence to confrontation with the Nazi past.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frankfurt Auschwitz trials
<a id="frankfurt-auschwitz-trials"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1963–1965
- **Short:** West German trials of Auschwitz staff in Frankfurt, 1963–65.
- **Explanation:** They brought the details of Auschwitz to broad public attention in West Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frankfurt Documents
<a id="frankfurt-documents"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1948
- **Short:** 1948 Allied papers instructing West German state leaders to create a West German state.
- **Explanation:** Documents handed by the Western military governors to the heads of the West German Länder in July 1948, calling for a constitutional assembly — which became the Parliamentary Council.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frankfurt National Assembly
<a id="frankfurt-national-assembly"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Also written as:** National Assembly · Frankfurt Assembly
- **Ambiguous forms:** National Assembly (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** First elected all-German parliament, meeting in Frankfurt's St. Paul's Church 1848–49.
- **Explanation:** Elected by broad male suffrage and dominated by educated professionals, it drafted Basic Rights and a constitution for a united Germany. It had no army or revenue, and failed when the Prussian king refused the crown in 1849.
- **German:** Frankfurter Nationalversammlung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Franks
<a id="franks"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Frankish
- **Short:** Confederation ('the bold/free') that built the most successful post-Roman kingdom in the West.
- **Explanation:** A 3rd-century grouping on the lower Rhine that took over the Rhineland in the 5th century. Under Merovingian and Carolingian kings they conquered almost all later German lands — the Franks, not 'Germans', united them.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frauen-Zeitung
<a id="frauen-zeitung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1849–1852
- **Short:** Women's newspaper founded by Louise Otto-Peters in 1849.
- **Explanation:** It showed women's participation in the revolution's journalism though they had no vote.
- **German:** Frauen-Zeitung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Free city
<a id="free-city"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** free cities · Free cities · Freie Städte
- **Short:** City governing itself as a state, not ruled by a prince.
- **Explanation:** Four free cities (Frankfurt, Hamburg, Bremen, Lübeck) were members of the German Confederation; three remained federal states in the Empire of 1871.
- **German:** Freie Stadt
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Free Democratic Party
<a id="free-democratic-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1948–
- **Also written as:** FDP
- **Short:** Liberal, pro-market party that failed to re-enter the Bundestag in 2025.
- **Explanation:** Frequent junior partner in post-war coalitions. It won only 4.3% in 2025 and dropped out of the Bundestag under the 5% threshold.
- **German:** Freie Demokratische Partei (FDP)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### free movement
<a id="free-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** EU free movement
- **Short:** EU citizens' right to live, work and study in any member state.
- **Explanation:** Brought many EU citizens, notably from Poland and Romania, to Germany and let Germans move freely across Europe.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Free Trade Unions
<a id="free-trade-unions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** The socialist trade unions linked to the SPD; ~2.5 million members by 1913.
- **Explanation:** They were the core of the Social Democratic milieu and largely reformist in practice.
- **German:** Freie Gewerkschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Free Unions
<a id="free-unions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** The socialist trade union federation, allied with the SPD.
- **Explanation:** Together with the SPD it carried social democracy and defended the republic. The Nazis stormed and dissolved the unions on 2 May 1933.
- **German:** Freie Gewerkschaften
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Freedom of trade
<a id="freedom-of-trade"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1810–1811
- **Also written as:** freedom of trade
- **Short:** Right to practise any trade without guild permission.
- **Explanation:** Its introduction exposed traditional artisans to competition, pushing many into poverty. The North German Confederation introduced it generally in 1869.
- **German:** Gewerbefreiheit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Freiheiten
<a id="freiheiten"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Liberties': privileges belonging to specific groups, not individual rights.
- **Explanation:** Clergy, nobles and towns each held their own 'liberties' — the medieval idea of rights as group privileges.
- **German:** Freiheiten
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Freikorps
<a id="freikorps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1918–1920s
- **Short:** Right-wing volunteer units of ex-soldiers used against the left after 1918.
- **Explanation:** The SPD-led government relied on these paramilitary units to crush communist uprisings in 1919; Freikorps men murdered Rosa Luxemburg and Karl Liebknecht. Historians disagree whether using them 'betrayed' the revolution (Haffner) or saved democracy from Bolshevism (Winkler).
- **German:** Freikorps
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: the SPD's reliance on the Freikorps is judged differently by historians.

### French Revolution
<a id="french-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1789–1799
- **Short:** The 1789 revolution in France that overthrew the old order of privilege.
- **Explanation:** Many German intellectuals welcomed it at first, then feared it after the Terror. Its wars from 1792 destroyed the Holy Roman Empire.
- **German:** Französische Revolution
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### French revolutionary wars
<a id="french-revolutionary-wars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1792–1802
- **Also written as:** Revolutionary war
- **Short:** Wars of revolutionary France against European monarchies from 1792.
- **Explanation:** France annexed the left bank of the Rhine, triggering the redistribution of German territories.
- **German:** Koalitionskriege
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Fringsen
<a id="fringsen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** fringsen
- **Short:** Slang for stealing coal to survive, after Cardinal Frings.
- **Explanation:** Cardinal Frings of Cologne said in a 1946 sermon that taking what one needed to survive could be excused; stealing coal was then nicknamed 'fringsen'.
- **German:** fringsen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Frisians
<a id="frisians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** People of the North Sea coast with their own Frisian language; a recognised minority.
- **Explanation:** Frisian-speaking communities live in North Frisia (Schleswig-Holstein) and in parts of Lower Saxony. Together with Danes, Sorbs and Sinti and Roma they are one of Germany's four officially recognised national minorities.
- **German:** Friesen
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### FU Berlin
<a id="fu-berlin"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1948–
- **Short:** Free University of Berlin, founded in West Berlin in 1948.
- **Explanation:** University whose research team estimated deaths at the inner-German border at about 327.
- **German:** Freie Universität Berlin
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Fugger
<a id="fugger"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Augsburg merchant-banking family that financed Habsburg politics around 1500.
- **Explanation:** Their wealth from trade and mining finance bankrolled Habsburg politics, including the 1519 imperial election — a sign of how south German capital shaped the Empire.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Führer
<a id="fuhrer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Dates:** 1934–1945
- **Also written as:** Führer and Reich Chancellor
- **Short:** German for 'leader'; Hitler's title as dictator.
- **Explanation:** On Hindenburg's death in August 1934 Hitler merged the offices of president and chancellor as 'Führer and Reich Chancellor', and the army swore a personal oath to him.
- **German:** Führer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### functionalism
<a id="functionalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** structuralism
- **Short:** View that Nazi policy emerged from institutional chaos and competition.
- **Explanation:** Broszat and Mommsen argued this; today's synthesis combines it with intentionalism.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: intentionalism vs functionalism.

### Fürsorgediktatur
<a id="fursorgediktatur"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Welfare dictatorship
- **Short:** 'Welfare dictatorship': Jarausch's label for the GDR's mix of care and repression.
- **Explanation:** Konrad Jarausch's term for the contradictory GDR, which 'both had an emancipatory claim and consisted of Stalinist practice'.
- **German:** Fürsorgediktatur
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

## G

### Gap theory
<a id="gap-theory"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1862
- **Also written as:** gap theory
- **Short:** Bismarck's argument that where the constitution is silent, the king decides.
- **Explanation:** He used it to justify governing without an approved budget in 1862–66.
- **German:** Lückentheorie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Gas vans
<a id="gas-vans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** gas vans
- **Short:** Trucks whose exhaust fumes were piped inside to kill people.
- **Explanation:** Trucks used as mobile gas chambers, notably at Chełmno from December 1941; they were a step towards the stationary gas chambers of the death camps.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gau
<a id="gau"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Gaue
- **Short:** A regional division of the Nazi party (plural Gaue).
- **Explanation:** Each Gau was run by a Gauleiter; the network helped the party organise across Germany.
- **German:** Gau / Gaue
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gauleiter
<a id="gauleiter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** Gauleiters
- **Short:** Nazi party regional leader, head of a Gau.
- **Explanation:** Gauleiters were powerful figures in the party apparatus, one of the rival power centres in the Nazi state.
- **German:** Gauleiter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### GDP
<a id="gdp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Gross domestic product: the total value of goods and services an economy produces in a year.
- **Explanation:** The standard measure of a country's economic output. The 2025 constitutional change exempted defence spending above 1% of GDP from the debt brake.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### GDP per capita
<a id="gdp-per-capita"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Total economic output divided by population — a measure of average income.
- **Explanation:** Gierok finds urban GDP per capita did not regain pre-war levels until well into the 18th century.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### GDR districts
<a id="gdr-districts"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1952–1990
- **Also written as:** districts
- **Ambiguous forms:** districts (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The administrative districts that replaced the East German states (Länder) in 1952.
- **Explanation:** By abolishing the Länder, the SED destroyed federalism in the East and centralised power. The KB counts this with the Nazi regime's 1934 measures as the only breaks in Germany's federal tradition. The Länder were re-created in 1990.
- **German:** Bezirke
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### GDR local elections of May 1989
<a id="gdr-local-elections-1989"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 7 May 1989
- **Also written as:** Election fraud
- **Ambiguous forms:** Election fraud (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** East German local elections whose rigged results were exposed by opposition groups.
- **Explanation:** The regime announced 98.85% 'yes' votes for its single list; opposition groups watched the counting and documented the fraud. The exposure fuelled anger and protest in the months before the Peaceful Revolution.
- **German:** Kommunalwahlen 1989
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### GEAS
<a id="geas"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2024–2026
- **Short:** The Common European Asylum System, reformed by the EU in 2024–26.
- **Explanation:** The German abbreviation for the EU's shared asylum rules. The KB lists its reform as the latest stage after Germany's unconditional asylum right (1949) and the 1993 asylum compromise.
- **German:** Gemeinsames Europäisches Asylsystem
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gemeinde
<a id="gemeinde"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Gemeinden
- **Short:** Village community that regulated common fields and negotiated with lords.
- **Explanation:** Village communities gave peasants collective agency — central to Peter Blickle's 'communalism' thesis.
- **German:** Gemeinde
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### gemeiner Nutzen
<a id="gemeiner-nutzen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Common good
- **Short:** 'Common good': a civic ideal of medieval towns.
- **Explanation:** Alongside 'peace', the core civic value of medieval towns: councils claimed to govern for the good of the whole community, even though they were run by small elites.
- **German:** gemeiner Nutzen
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### gendarmerie
<a id="gendarmerie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** A military-style police force on the French model.
- **Explanation:** Rhine Confederation states such as Bavaria used it to enforce centralised rule.
- **German:** Gendarmerie

### gender pay gap
<a id="gender-pay-gap"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Difference between men's and women's average hourly pay.
- **Explanation:** The KB gives about 17% in the West (2024) but about 5% in the East, a legacy of the GDR's near-universal female employment.
- **German:** Gender Pay Gap
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### General Directory
<a id="general-directory"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1723
- **Short:** Prussia's central authority for finances and war, created in 1723.
- **Explanation:** Created by Frederick William I, it combined management of the royal domains and the war commissariat, tying administration to the needs of the army.
- **German:** Generaldirektorium
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### General School Regulation
<a id="general-school-regulation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1763
- **Short:** Prussian decree of 1763 requiring schooling for rural children.
- **Explanation:** Implementation lagged far behind the law.
- **German:** Generallandschulreglement
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### General Secretary
<a id="general-secretary"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Short:** Title of the SED leader (First Secretary until 1976), the most powerful person in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Head of the party apparatus and, in practice, ruler of the GDR: Walter Ulbricht (1950–71) and Erich Honecker (1971–89).
- **German:** Generalsekretär / Erster Sekretär
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### General Staff
<a id="general-staff"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** The army's planning and command staff.
- **Explanation:** In the Empire it shared power with the Kaiser and Chancellor, outside parliamentary control.
- **German:** Generalstab
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### general strike
<a id="general-strike-1920"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** March 1920
- **Short:** Nationwide stoppage of work in March 1920 that defeated the Kapp Putsch.
- **Explanation:** Called by unions and the SPD, the strike stopped transport, industry and administration, leaving the putschists unable to govern. It showed that organised labour could defend the republic when the army would not.
- **German:** Generalstreik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Generalplan Ost
<a id="generalplan-ost"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Nazi master plan to resettle Eastern Europe with Germans and remove its population.
- **Explanation:** It envisaged deporting or killing millions of Slavs to make way for German settlers.
- **German:** Generalplan Ost
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Genocide
<a id="genocide"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** genocide · genocidal
- **Short:** Destruction of a national, ethnic, racial or religious group as such.
- **Explanation:** German colonial rule included the first genocide of the 20th century (Herero and Nama).
- **German:** Völkermord
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)

### gens
<a id="gens"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Latin word Romans used for a 'people' or tribe.
- **Explanation:** Roman writers called a Germanic-speaking group a gens ('people'). Such units were small — perhaps a few thousand to tens of thousands — and historians debate whether they were stable peoples at all.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)

### Gerichtsherrschaft
<a id="gerichtsherrschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Jurisdictional lordship
- **Short:** Jurisdictional lordship: a lord's right to hold courts.
- **Explanation:** Often held by a different lord from the landlord, adding to the layered character of medieval rule.
- **German:** Gerichtsherrschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)

### German Christians
<a id="german-christians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Pro-Nazi movement within the Protestant church.
- **Explanation:** They sought to merge Christianity with Nazi ideology and a racial church. Their rise split German Protestantism and provoked the Confessing Church.
- **German:** Deutsche Christen

### German Colonial Society
<a id="german-colonial-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1887–1936
- **Also written as:** Colonial Society
- **Short:** Pro-colonial lobby founded in 1887, with ~40,000 members.
- **Explanation:** It concentrated colonial enthusiasm among commercial, missionary and educated circles.
- **German:** Deutsche Kolonialgesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)

### German Confederation
<a id="german-confederation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1815–1866
- **Also written as:** Confederation · Deutscher Bund
- **Ambiguous forms:** Confederation (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** League of about 39 sovereign German states created in 1815; not a nation-state.
- **Explanation:** Its only organ was a Federal Assembly of envoys in Frankfurt chaired by Austria. Designed for security and for suppressing revolution, it enforced censorship under Metternich. It collapsed when Prussia went to war with Austria in 1866.
- **German:** Deutscher Bund
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### German Conservative Party
<a id="german-conservative-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1876–1918
- **Short:** Party of the Protestant, agrarian Prussian nobility.
- **Explanation:** It represented the conservative-Protestant-agrarian milieu of East Elbia.
- **German:** Deutschkonservative Partei

### German Dictionary
<a id="german-dictionary"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1838–1961
- **Short:** Huge dictionary of the German language begun by the Grimm brothers in 1838.
- **Explanation:** A monument of the 'cultural nation' idea: the nation defined through its language.
- **German:** Deutsches Wörterbuch
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)

### German glance
<a id="german-glance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** der deutsche Blick
- **Short:** Joke name for looking over one's shoulder before speaking, for fear of being overheard.
- **Explanation:** It captured the everyday fear of denunciation under the regime.
- **German:** der deutsche Blick

### German Labour Front
<a id="german-labour-front"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Also written as:** DAF
- **Short:** Nazi organisation replacing trade unions, with about 25 million members.
- **Explanation:** After unions were destroyed in 1933, the DAF organised employers and workers together under party control; its leisure arm was Strength Through Joy.
- **German:** Deutsche Arbeitsfront

### German liberty
<a id="german-liberty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** German freedom · teutsche Libertät
- **Short:** The princes' and estates' constitutional liberties against imperial (Habsburg) or princely 'tyranny'.
- **Explanation:** Protestant princes used it against Charles V; later the estates invoked it against despotism. It was a constitutional, not democratic, idea.
- **German:** teutsche Libertät

### German Limes Commission
<a id="german-limes-commission"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Deutsche Limeskommission
- **Short:** German body that manages and studies the Limes World Heritage site.
- **Explanation:** The KB quotes it: the Limes 'was not so much a military bulwark but rather a monitored border where entry and exit were controlled and goods were traded or cleared through customs.'
- **German:** Deutsche Limeskommission
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)

### German question
<a id="german-question"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The problem of where a large, central Germany fits into a stable Europe.
- **Explanation:** For centuries Germany was either too fragmented or too powerful for European stability. After 1945 the answer was integration: binding Germany into Western and European institutions.

### German War
<a id="german-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1866
- **Short:** 1866 war in which Prussia defeated Austria and its German allies.
- **Explanation:** Decided at Königgrätz. The Confederation was dissolved, Austria excluded, and Prussia annexed Hanover, Hesse-Kassel, Nassau, Frankfurt and Schleswig-Holstein.
- **German:** Deutscher Krieg

### Germanen. Eine archäologische Bestandsaufnahme
<a id="germanen-eine-archaologische-bestandsaufnahme"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2020–21
- **Short:** Berlin and Bonn exhibition (2020–21) that kept 'Germanen' as an archaeological term.
- **Explanation:** Title means 'The Germani: an archaeological stocktaking'. Steinacher cites it as evidence that archaeologists still use 'Germanic' for the 1st–4th centuries while many historians avoid it.

### Germania
<a id="germania"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Short book by Tacitus (c. 98 CE) describing the Germani, idealised as a mirror to Rome.
- **Explanation:** Portrays the Germani as chaste, brave and free to shame Roman decadence; Tacitus never visited. Printed in the late 15th century, it gave German humanists glorious ancestors and was later prized by nationalists and the Nazis. Best read as Roman moral literature.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** The same word also names the Roman frontier region and the archaeological journal Germania.

### Germania libera
<a id="germania-libera"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Free Germania': a romantic modern phrase for non-Roman lands, not found in ancient sources.
- **Explanation:** Latin for 'free Germania'. Steinacher notes it was introduced by Jacob Grimm in 1835/36; no ancient source uses it.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Germanic
<a id="germanic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Germani · Germanen · Germanic peoples
- **Short:** Label for related languages and, contestedly, for peoples between Rhine and Vistula in antiquity.
- **Explanation:** Linguists use it for a family of languages. As a name for peoples it comes from Rome ('Germani'): no ancient group called itself Germanic. Historians increasingly avoid it; archaeologists keep it as a working label. Nationalists and Nazis later misused it.
- **German:** Germanen
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: historians disagree whether 'Germanic' should be used for peoples at all.

### Germanisation
<a id="germanisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Policies forcing German language and settlement on Poles in Prussia.
- **Explanation:** Included school language laws, the Settlement Commission (1886) and expulsions of ~30,000 non-Prussian Poles and Jews in 1885–86.
- **German:** Germanisierung

- **Also written as:** German-Polish treaty
- **Short:** November 1990 treaty confirming the Oder–Neisse line as the German–Polish border.
- **Explanation:** Treaty signed by united Germany and Poland shortly after unification, confirming the final border required by the Two-plus-Four Treaty.
- **German:** Deutsch-polnischer Grenzvertrag

<a id="germanpolish-textbook-commission"></a>

- **Explanation:** It helped replace nationalist accounts of the Ostsiedlung with a shared European view.

### Geruchskonserve
<a id="geruchskonserve"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Preserved smell': Stasi jar holding a person's scent for tracking dogs.
- **Explanation:** Glass jar in which the Stasi kept a cloth carrying a person's body odour, so that dogs could later track 'suspects'. It has become a symbol of total surveillance.

<a id="gestapo"></a>

- **Short:** The Nazi secret state police.
- **Explanation:** It was small (about 7,000 employees in 1937) and depended on denunciations, party officials and the ordinary police. How many cases began with denunciations is debated.

### Godesberg Programme
<a id="godesberg-programme"></a>

### Golden Bull
<a id="golden-bull"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1356
- **Short:** Imperial law of 1356 fixing the seven prince-electors and majority election of the king.
- **Explanation:** It also made electoral lands indivisible. It did not give the emperor power over the territories; an emperor was strong only through his own dynastic lands.

### Golden Twenties
<a id="golden-twenties"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1924–1929
- **Short:** Weimar's relatively stable years 1924–29, famous for a cultural flowering.
- **Explanation:** After the hyperinflation, American loans and the Dawes Plan brought relative stability. Berlin became a centre of modern art, theatre, film and design (the Bauhaus). The economy stayed fragile and depended on foreign credit, which the Depression withdrew.

- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)

### Göttingen Seven
<a id="gottingen-seven"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Dates:** 1837
- **Short:** Seven Göttingen professors dismissed in 1837 for protesting Hanover's cancellation of its constitution.
- **Explanation:** They included the Grimm brothers. Their dismissal became a famous symbol of princes repressing liberal academics in the Vormärz.
- **German:** Göttinger Sieben
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gradual
<a id="gradual"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Medieval chant book containing music for the Mass.
- **Explanation:** Carolingian monasteries copied such books in the new Carolingian minuscule script.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### grand coalition
<a id="grand-coalition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1928–1930
- **Short:** Coalition government of the two largest rival parties.
- **Explanation:** In Germany usually CDU/CSU with SPD. In 1990 Lothar de Maizière led a grand coalition in the GDR, including the Social Democrats.
- **German:** Große Koalition
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### grand coalition state
<a id="grand-coalition-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Manfred G. Schmidt's label for West Germany's consensus politics, forced by the Bundesrat veto.
- **Explanation:** Description of the Federal Republic by political scientist Manfred G. Schmidt: because opposition-led states could block laws in the Bundesrat, the main parties had to cooperate constantly, as if governing in a grand coalition.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### grand duchy
<a id="grand-duchy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Short:** A state ruled by a grand duke, ranking between duchy and kingdom.
- **Explanation:** Baden became a grand duchy in 1806.
- **German:** Großherzogtum
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### grassroots democracy
<a id="grassroots-democracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Politics driven by ordinary citizens' direct participation rather than party hierarchies.
- **Explanation:** Ideal of the East German civil-rights movement and of the Round Tables in 1989–90.
- **German:** Basisdemokratie
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gravamina of the German Nation
<a id="gravamina-of-the-german-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Grievances against papal taxation raised from the mid-15th century.
- **Explanation:** They show growing anticlerical and national resentment before the Reformation.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Great Depression
<a id="great-depression"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1929–1933
- **Also written as:** Depression
- **Ambiguous forms:** Depression (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The worldwide economic crisis beginning in 1929.
- **Explanation:** In Germany US credit was withdrawn, banks collapsed in 1931, industrial production fell about 40% and registered unemployment reached about 6 million. It destroyed the parliamentary majority and brought the Nazi breakthrough.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Great Depression (1873–1895)
<a id="great-depression-18731895"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1873–c.1895
- **Also written as:** Great Depression
- **Ambiguous forms:** Great Depression (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Long phase of falling prices and slower growth after the 1873 crash.
- **Explanation:** Not a collapse but a slowdown; it brought protectionism, cartels and state intervention. Not to be confused with the Depression of 1929–33.
- **German:** Große Depression
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** Not the 1929–33 Great Depression.

### Griff nach der Weltmacht
<a id="griff-nach-der-weltmacht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1961
- **Short:** Fritz Fischer's 1961 book (literally 'Grab for World Power') on German war aims.
- **Explanation:** It argued Germany deliberately pursued war for dominance on the continent and set off the Fischer controversy.
- **German:** Griff nach der Weltmacht
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Grimm's Law
<a id="grimm-s-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Set of sound changes that define the Germanic language family, named after Jacob Grimm.
- **Explanation:** Regular shifts of consonants (for example Latin pater vs English father) that separate Germanic languages from related Indo-European ones. It is why linguists can still use 'Germanic' meaningfully for languages.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Großdeutsch
<a id="grossdeutsch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** großdeutsch
- **Short:** 'Greater German' solution: a united Germany including Austria.
- **Explanation:** Favoured by many Catholics and southerners. It proved impossible because Austria would not split its multinational empire; after 1866 Austria was excluded.
- **German:** großdeutsch
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Gründerzeit
<a id="grunderzeit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1871–1873
- **Short:** 'Founders' period': speculative boom after 1871, ending in the 1873 crash.
- **Explanation:** Fuelled by the French war indemnity, it saw many new companies founded, followed by a crash and a long slowdown.
- **German:** Gründerzeit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Grundherrschaft
<a id="grundherrschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Manorial lordship · manorial lordship
- **Short:** Manorial lordship: a lord's rights over land and the peasants who worked it.
- **Explanation:** Dues in grain, money, chickens and eggs, labour services, and fees on inheritance and marriage. German historians often prefer this precise term to 'feudalism'.
- **German:** Grundherrschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Grundschule
<a id="grundschule"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1920–
- **Short:** Common four-year primary school for all children, introduced in 1920.
- **Explanation:** A Weimar reform: children of all backgrounds attended the same school for four years before being divided among secondary school types.
- **German:** Grundschule
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gruppe 47
<a id="gruppe-47"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1947–1967
- **Short:** Circle of West German writers central to post-war German literature.
- **Explanation:** Founded in 1947 by Hans Werner Richter, it met to read and criticise new work; members and guests included Heinrich Böll and Günter Grass. It helped shape a literature that confronted war, guilt and the Nazi past.
- **German:** Gruppe 47
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Guelph fund
<a id="guelph-fund"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Confiscated fortune of Hanover's royal family, used by Bismarck as a secret fund.
- **Explanation:** Bismarck used it among other things to pay Ludwig II of Bavaria secretly.
- **German:** Welfenfonds
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Guelphs
<a id="guelphs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Hanoverian loyalists who resented Prussia's 1866 annexation.
- **Explanation:** Named after Hanover's royal dynasty (the Welfen), they formed a minority party in the Empire.
- **German:** Welfen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### guest workers
<a id="guest-workers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Dates:** 1955–1973
- **Also written as:** Gastarbeiter · Guest workers · guest worker
- **Short:** Foreign workers recruited by West Germany 1955–73, officially as temporary labour.
- **Explanation:** Workers recruited through agreements with Italy, Spain, Greece, Turkey, Morocco, Portugal, Tunisia and Yugoslavia. Policy assumed they would rotate home, but after the 1973 recruitment stop many settled and brought families, turning West Germany into a de facto country of immigration.
- **German:** Gastarbeiter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### guild
<a id="guild"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** guilds · Guilds · Zünfte · Innungen · Guild
- **Short:** Association of craft masters regulating entry, training, quality and prices.
- **Explanation:** Guilds were also confraternities and militia units; they excluded outsiders, many women and the 'dishonourable'.
- **German:** Zünfte, Innungen
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### guns vs butter
<a id="guns-vs-butter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1935–1936
- **Short:** The trade-off between military spending and consumer goods.
- **Explanation:** Rearmament held consumption down, causing visible tensions by 1935–36.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Gutsbezirke
<a id="gutsbezirke"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** until 1927
- **Short:** 'Estate districts': rural areas in Prussia administered by their noble owners.
- **Explanation:** A remnant of noble lordship that survived until 1927.
- **German:** Gutsbezirke
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Gutsherrschaft
<a id="gutsherrschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** East Elbian system in which nobles farmed estates with serf labour and ruled their peasants.
- **Explanation:** Lords held police and court powers; peasants owed labour services and needed the lord's consent to marry or leave. Labour scarcity after the Thirty Years' War helped lords tighten it. The crown–Junker bargain in Prussia rested on it.
- **German:** Gutsherrschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### gymnasium
<a id="gymnasium"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Gymnasium
- **Short:** German academic secondary school preparing for university.
- **Explanation:** Humboldt's humanist gymnasium centred on classical languages and self-formation (Bildung).
- **German:** Gymnasium
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### gymnastics movement
<a id="gymnastics-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** from 1811
- **Short:** Jahn's nationalist gymnastics clubs, founded in 1811.
- **Explanation:** They combined physical training with anti-French nationalism among young men.
- **German:** Turnbewegung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### gynaecea
<a id="gynaecea"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Workshops on large estates where women produced textiles.
- **Explanation:** Evidence that peasant women's labour was organised on Carolingian manors.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB

## H

### H-Soz-Kult
<a id="h-soz-kult"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** German-language online forum for historians publishing book reviews and conference reports.
- **Explanation:** A widely used academic platform for history in the German-speaking world; the KB draws on its reviews, for instance on German knowledge of the Holocaust and on the myth of the Wars of Liberation.
- **German:** H-Soz-Kult
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Habitual criminals
<a id="habitual-criminals"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** habitual criminals
- **Short:** Nazi label for repeat offenders, who could be held in camps without trial.
- **Explanation:** The police could send people with past convictions to concentration camps without trial, as so-called preventive detention.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Habsburgs
<a id="habsburgs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** Habsburg
- **Short:** Dynasty ruling Austria, Bohemia and Hungary that held the imperial crown almost continuously.
- **Explanation:** Their lands formed a multi-lingual composite monarchy centred partly outside the Empire. Their rivalry with France and, from 1740, with Prussia shaped German history until 1866.
- **German:** Habsburger
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hall of Mirrors
<a id="hall-of-mirrors"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Grand hall of Versailles palace where the German Empire was proclaimed (1871).
- **Explanation:** Wilhelm I was proclaimed Emperor there before princes and officers, with no parliamentarians invited.
- **German:** Spiegelsaal
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hallstein Doctrine
<a id="hallstein-doctrine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1955–1969
- **Short:** West German policy of breaking relations with states that recognised the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Named after diplomat Walter Hallstein. It enforced the Federal Republic's claim to be Germany's sole legitimate representative; it was abandoned with Ostpolitik.
- **German:** Hallstein-Doktrin
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hambach Festival
<a id="hambach-festival"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1832
- **Also written as:** Hambach festival · Hambacher Fest
- **Short:** Mass rally of 1832 at Hambach Castle demanding unity, freedom and popular sovereignty.
- **Explanation:** Some 20,000–30,000 people marched with black-red-gold flags. It was followed by federal repression.
- **German:** Hambacher Fest
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Hamburg Institute for Social Research
<a id="hamburg-institute-for-social-research"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Hamburg Institute
- **Short:** Private research institute that organised the Wehrmacht exhibition.
- **Explanation:** Its exhibition of 1995–99 caused a major public debate in Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hamster trips
<a id="hamster-trips"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** hamster trips
- **Short:** Trips to the countryside to barter valuables for food.
- **Explanation:** In the hunger years after 1945, city dwellers travelled to the countryside to barter clothes, jewellery or other valuables with farmers for food.
- **German:** Hamsterfahrten
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hanse
<a id="hanse"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** c. 12th–17th c.
- **Also written as:** Hanseatic League
- **Short:** Network of merchants and later up to about 200 towns trading around the North Sea and Baltic.
- **Explanation:** Active c. 12th–17th centuries, with offices in London, Bruges, Bergen and Novgorod. It negotiated privileges and fought Denmark but had no army, treasury or fixed membership — power without statehood.
- **German:** Hanse
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: 'network' vs 'organisation' debate.

### Hanseatic cities
<a id="hanseatic-cities"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** The free cities of Hamburg, Bremen and Lübeck, heirs of the medieval Hanseatic League.
- **Explanation:** After 1815 some of them partly rolled back Jewish emancipation granted earlier.
- **German:** Hansestädte
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hansetage
<a id="hansetage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Diets of the Hanse towns.
- **Explanation:** The Hanse's common assemblies. Beyond them it had no army, no treasury and no fixed membership — one reason historians call it a network rather than a state.
- **German:** Hansetage
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### hard currency
<a id="hard-currency"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** hard-currency
- **Short:** Freely exchangeable Western money such as the D-Mark or US dollar.
- **Explanation:** Currency that could buy goods on world markets. The GDR was always short of it and obtained it through Intershops, prisoner buyouts, transit fees and Western loans.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hartz reforms
<a id="hartz-reforms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2003–2005
- **Also written as:** Hartz · Hartz IV
- **Short:** 2003–05 labour-market reforms that merged unemployment assistance and welfare (Hartz IV).
- **Explanation:** Named after Peter Hartz, who chaired the commission that proposed them. They merged unemployment assistance and welfare into 'Hartz IV' and expanded low-wage 'mini-jobs'. Credited with job growth, blamed for insecurity; Hartz IV was replaced by the Bürgergeld in 2023.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Debated: credited with job growth vs blamed for insecurity and low wages.

### Haskala
<a id="haskala"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** late 18th c.
- **Short:** The Jewish Enlightenment, led by Moses Mendelssohn in Berlin.
- **Explanation:** It sought to combine Jewish tradition with Enlightenment learning and German culture.
- **German:** Haskala
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)

<a id="haus-der-geschichte"></a>

### Heerschildordnung
<a id="heerschildordnung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Theoretical ranking of nobles by whom they could receive fiefs from.
- **Explanation:** It placed every noble below the king — a hierarchy in theory.
- **German:** Heerschildordnung
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)

### Hegemony
<a id="hegemony"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** hegemony
- **Short:** Dominance of one state over others in an alliance or federation.
- **Explanation:** Prussia exercised de facto hegemony in the Empire through its size and its votes in the Bundesrat.
- **German:** Hegemonie

### Heimarbeit
<a id="heimarbeit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Paid home work, often done by women for low piece rates.
- **Explanation:** A major form of women's work in the industrial era.
- **German:** Heimarbeit

### Heimat films
<a id="heimat-films"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1950s
- **Also written as:** Heimat
- **Ambiguous forms:** Heimat (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Popular 1950s German films idealising rural homeland life.
- **Explanation:** Film genre (Heimatfilm) of sentimental stories set in idyllic mountain or village landscapes. Very popular in 1950s West Germany, the films offered escape and harmony after war and destruction.
- **German:** Heimatfilm
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Heinrich Böll Foundation
<a id="heinrich-boll-foundation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** Heinrich-Böll-Stiftung
- **Short:** Political foundation associated with the German Greens.
- **Explanation:** Named after the novelist Heinrich Böll. The KB cites its report on the September 2026 Saxony-Anhalt state election.
- **German:** Heinrich-Böll-Stiftung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Helsinki Final Act
<a id="helsinki-final-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1975
- **Also written as:** Helsinki
- **Ambiguous forms:** Helsinki (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 1975 East–West agreement linking security and borders with human rights.
- **Explanation:** Signed at the Conference on Security and Co-operation in Europe. Its 'Basket III' on human rights and contacts gave East Germans an internationally signed text to cite in exit applications.
- **German:** KSZE-Schlussakte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hep-Hep riots
<a id="hep-hep-riots"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1819
- **Also written as:** Hep-Hep
- **Short:** Wave of anti-Jewish riots in many German towns in 1819.
- **Explanation:** The riots, named after a mocking cry against Jews, struck many towns in 1819, while Jewish emancipation was being partly rolled back after 1815.
- **German:** Hep-Hep-Krawalle
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hereditary enemy
<a id="hereditary-enemy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** hereditary enemy
- **Short:** The idea that France was Germany's eternal, 'inherited' enemy.
- **Explanation:** A theme of anti-French nationalism since the Napoleonic Wars, spread by writers like Arndt.
- **German:** Erbfeind
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hereditary Farm Law
<a id="hereditary-farm-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1933
- **Short:** 1933 law protecting mid-sized farms but banning their sale or division.
- **Explanation:** It made certain farms 'hereditary farms' that passed undivided to one heir and could not be sold or mortgaged, in line with 'blood and soil' ideology.
- **German:** Reichserbhofgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Herero
<a id="herero"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Cattle-herding people of Namibia, victims of genocide in 1904–08.
- **Explanation:** Germany recognised the killing as genocide in 2021.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Herero and Nama genocide
<a id="herero-and-nama-genocide"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1904–1908
- **Short:** Colonial war in German South West Africa that became genocide; about 100,000 deaths.
- **Explanation:** Germany recognised it as genocide in 2021 and announced a €1.1 billion aid package; Herero and Nama representatives dispute implementation. Death-toll estimates vary.
- **German:** Völkermord an den Herero und Nama
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** Death-toll estimates vary; compensation disputed.

### heresy
<a id="heresy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Religious belief condemned by the Church.
- **Explanation:** Medieval people were not uniformly pious and obedient: heresy, anticlericalism, revolts and lawsuits were all common.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Herr im Hause
<a id="herr-im-hause"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Master in one's own house': paternalist employers rejecting unions.
- **Explanation:** Employers like Krupp combined company welfare with hostility to unions.
- **German:** Herr im Hause
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Herrschaft
<a id="herrschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Lordship': power over land and people, the state that mattered for most medieval people.
- **Explanation:** It came in layers — land (Grundherrschaft), courts (Gerichtsherrschaft), persons (Leibherrschaft) and territory (Landesherrschaft) — each possibly held by different lords.
- **German:** Herrschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Hessian Courier
<a id="hessian-courier"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1834
- **Short:** Büchner's 1834 revolutionary pamphlet: 'Peace to the huts! War on the palaces!'
- **Explanation:** A secret pamphlet written by Georg Büchner urging the peasants of Hesse to rise against their princes. It is a key text of Vormärz democratic radicalism.
- **German:** Der Hessische Landbote
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### hidden unemployment
<a id="hidden-unemployment"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Workers kept on payrolls without real work, so they do not appear as unemployed.
- **Explanation:** In the GDR, guaranteed jobs meant many enterprises employed more people than needed; this became visible as open unemployment after 1990.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### High industrialisation
<a id="high-industrialisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** c. 1895–1914
- **Also written as:** high industrialisation
- **Short:** Phase of rapid growth in chemicals, electrics and steel before 1914.
- **Explanation:** Germany became Europe's leading industrial economy.
- **German:** Hochindustrialisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Hindenburg myth
<a id="hindenburg-myth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The cult around Hindenburg as Germany's saviour after the 1914 victory at Tannenberg.
- **Explanation:** His popularity helped the army command maintain its power in the war and later helped him win the presidency.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hindenburg Programme
<a id="hindenburg-programme"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1916
- **Short:** 1916 plan for a massive increase in munitions production.
- **Explanation:** Launched by the new army command, it pushed the war economy to extremes and relied on state-industry-union cooperation and forced labour.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### historicism
<a id="historicism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Historicism
- **Short:** View that each people and age must be understood on its own terms.
- **Explanation:** Herder's idea that every people has its own spirit, expressed in language and culture.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Historikerstreit
<a id="historikerstreit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1986
- **Short:** The 1986 'historians' dispute' over whether the Holocaust could be compared with Soviet crimes.
- **Explanation:** Public controversy triggered when Ernst Nolte suggested the Holocaust was a reaction to Bolshevik crimes. Jürgen Habermas and most historians rejected this as relativising Nazi crimes; the dispute reaffirmed the Holocaust's central place in German memory.
- **German:** Historikerstreit
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Historikerstreit 2.0
<a id="historikerstreit-2-0"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 2021–2022
- **Short:** 2021–22 debate over Holocaust singularity sparked by A. Dirk Moses.
- **Explanation:** Named after the 1986 historians' dispute; it challenged the centrality of Holocaust singularity in German memory culture.
- **German:** Historikerstreit 2.0
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED.

### Hitler myth
<a id="hitler-myth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Ian Kershaw's term for Hitler's personal popularity, separate from dislike of the party.
- **Explanation:** Surveillance reports show strong approval of Hitler personally, while Germans grumbled about 'little Hitlers', shortages and church policy. Kershaw called this the 'Hitler myth'; it eroded after Stalingrad and under bombing.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Hitler over Germany
<a id="hitler-over-germany"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1932
- **Short:** Nazi 1932 election campaign in which Hitler flew between rallies by plane.
- **Explanation:** An example of the Nazis' modern propaganda methods.
- **German:** Hitler über Deutschland
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hitler putsch
<a id="hitler-putsch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 8–9 November 1923
- **Short:** Hitler's failed attempt to seize power in Munich in November 1923.
- **Explanation:** During the crisis year 1923 Hitler and Ludendorff tried to launch a march on Berlin from Munich; police stopped it. Hitler was tried and briefly imprisoned, and afterwards pursued power through elections and alliances rather than a coup.
- **German:** Hitlerputsch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hitler salute
<a id="hitler-salute"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** The compulsory raised-arm greeting with 'Heil Hitler'.
- **Explanation:** The compulsory raised-arm greeting with 'Heil Hitler'. Refusing it was a small act of everyday nonconformity — 'Resistenz' rather than resistance.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Hitler Youth
<a id="hitler-youth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1926–1945
- **Also written as:** Hitlerjugend
- **Short:** The Nazi party's youth organisation.
- **Explanation:** It mobilised young people through uniforms, marches and activities; after 1933 it was part of the mass organisations that structured youth and leisure, and its leaders also helped watch and report on others.
- **German:** Hitlerjugend (HJ)

<a id="hitlers-parteigenossen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 2020
- **Short:** Falter's 2020 book on Nazi party members.
- **Explanation:** Confirms a broad social base with middle-class over-representation.
- **German:** Hitlers Parteigenossen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)

### Hitlers Wähler
<a id="hitlers-wahler"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1991

- **Explanation:** Key study showing who voted Nazi.

### Hoechst
<a id="hoechst"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1863–1999
- **Short:** Chemical company near Frankfurt, founded 1863.
- **Explanation:** Part of the German chemical boom.

### Hofrat
<a id="hofrat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** A prince's court council that helped govern his territory.
- **Explanation:** In a 16th-century principality such as Electoral Saxony, the prince ruled with his court council, a church board (consistory) and the estates.
- **German:** Hofrat
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)

### Hoftage
<a id="hoftage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Court assemblies where medieval kings met their princes.
- **Explanation:** One of the ways itinerant kings maintained power: consensus and decisions were reached at these gatherings.
- **German:** Hoftage
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)

### Hohenzollerns
<a id="hohenzollerns"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Dynasty ruling Brandenburg, then Prussia; German emperors 1871–1918.
- **Explanation:** From a poor, scattered electorate they built a disproportionately large army and disciplined administration, gaining a royal title in 1701.
- **German:** Hohenzollern

### Holocaust
<a id="holocaust"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1941–1945
- **Also written as:** Shoah
- **Short:** The Nazi murder of about six million European Jews.
- **Explanation:** Persecution escalated from exclusion (1933) through robbery and expulsion to systematic mass murder from 1941, by shooting and in death camps. It required the cooperation of ministries, railways, army, police and companies. Historians agree on facts and scale but debate decision-making and motives.
- **German:** Holocaust / Shoah
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Holocaust singularity
<a id="holocaust-singularity"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The view that the Holocaust is unique and not comparable to other crimes.
- **Explanation:** Its central place in German memory culture was challenged in 'Historikerstreit 2.0'.
- **German:** Singularität des Holocaust
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)

### Holy Roman Emperor
<a id="holy-roman-emperor"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** Emperor · emperor
- **Ambiguous forms:** Emperor · emperor (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Elected head of the Holy Roman Empire, almost always a Habsburg in the early modern period.
- **Explanation:** Chosen by the electors and bound by an electoral capitulation agreed before each election. His power was limited by the Diet and the princes; Francis II laid down the title in 1806.
- **German:** Kaiser

### Hossbach memorandum
<a id="hossbach-memorandum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1937
- **Short:** Record of a 1937 meeting where Hitler outlined plans for expansion.
- **Explanation:** It is key evidence that Hitler intended war.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### host desecration
<a id="host-desecration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** False accusation that Jews abused the consecrated communion bread.
- **Explanation:** Used to justify massacres such as the Rintfleisch pogrom (1298).
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)

### Hottentot election
<a id="hottentot-election"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1907
- **Short:** 1907 Reichstag election after parliament refused war funds, won by pro-colonial parties.
- **Explanation:** Named with a derogatory term for the Nama; the government dissolved the Reichstag and campaigned on colonialism.
- **German:** Hottentottenwahlen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
