### Lèse-majesté
<a id="lese-majeste"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** lèse-majesté
- **Short:** The crime of insulting the monarch.
- **Explanation:** Prosecutions for lèse-majesté were used against critics in the Empire.
- **German:** Majestätsbeleidigung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### levelled middle-class society
<a id="levelled-middle-class-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1953
- **Also written as:** Levelled middle-class society
- **Short:** Schelsky's contested label for 1950s West Germany as one broad middle class.
- **Explanation:** Phrase (nivellierte Mittelstandsgesellschaft) coined by sociologist Helmut Schelsky in 1953. It captured rising wages, mass consumption and social mobility, but critics note that inequality of wealth persisted.
- **German:** nivellierte Mittelstandsgesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: the KB marks this characterisation as contested.

### Liberal nationalism
<a id="liberal-nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** liberal nationalism
- **Short:** Nationalism demanding unity together with a constitution and civil rights.
- **Explanation:** Before 1848 German nationalism was mostly liberal and oppositional: unity and freedom against the princes.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Liberalism
<a id="liberalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** liberalism · liberals
- **Short:** Ideology of constitutions, rule of law, press freedom and property-based representation.
- **Explanation:** Carried by the educated and propertied middle classes, German liberalism before 1848 was tied to the demand for national unity. After 1866 the National Liberals, who backed Bismarck, split from the left-liberal Progress Party.
- **German:** Liberalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Lied der Deutschen
<a id="lied-der-deutschen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1841
- **Short:** Hoffmann von Fallersleben's 1841 'Song of the Germans'; its third stanza is today's national anthem.
- **Explanation:** Written after the Rhine crisis, its first line 'Deutschland, Deutschland über alles' was a call for unity above the many states.
- **German:** Lied der Deutschen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### lieu de mémoire
<a id="lieu-de-memoire"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** French term: a 'site of memory' where a group's collective memory is anchored.
- **Explanation:** Coined by the French historian Pierre Nora. Heidrun Derks argues the Varus battle fits many criteria of such a site, shaped by reinterpretations since the 16th century, but that the concept has limits.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Limes
<a id="limes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** upper Limes · Upper German-Raetian Limes · Roman frontier
- **Short:** Fortified, guarded Roman frontier line between the Rhine and the Danube.
- **Explanation:** Not a wall to keep barbarians out but a monitored border where people crossed, goods were traded and customs collected. The upper Limes was abandoned around 260 CE. It is now a UNESCO World Heritage site.
- **German:** Limes
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Little Hitlers
<a id="little-hitlers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** little Hitlers
- **Short:** Popular nickname for local Nazi party officials, who were often resented.
- **Explanation:** Germans who admired Hitler often complained about the arrogance and corruption of these petty functionaries.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### local elections of May 1989
<a id="local-elections-of-may-1989"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Also written as:** falsified local elections · Local elections
- **Short:** GDR local elections of 7 May 1989, whose falsification opposition groups documented.
- **Explanation:** Official results claimed 98.85% 'yes'. Opposition activists observed the vote counts and proved fraud, a spark for the protests of autumn 1989.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Locarno Treaties
<a id="locarno-treaties"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1925
- **Also written as:** Locarno
- **Short:** 1925 agreements in which Germany accepted its western borders with France and Belgium.
- **Explanation:** Negotiated by Foreign Minister Gustav Stresemann, the treaties (signed in Locarno, Switzerland) were guaranteed by Britain and Italy and opened the way to Germany joining the League of Nations in 1926 — a high point of the republic's stable middle years.
- **German:** Verträge von Locarno
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### longhouse
<a id="longhouse"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** longhouses
- **Short:** Long timber building in which a farming family and its animals lived under one roof.
- **Explanation:** Typical dwelling in the early Germanic-speaking world, excavated for example at Feddersen Wierde. Houses shared with animals remained common in many German regions through the Middle Ages.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### LPG
<a id="lpg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Collective farm in East Germany.
- **Explanation:** 'Landwirtschaftliche Produktionsgenossenschaft' (agricultural production cooperative): the collectives into which GDR farmers were pressed, part of the state-run planned economy.
- **German:** Landwirtschaftliche Produktionsgenossenschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Lübeck law
<a id="lubeck-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** Town law of Lübeck adopted by many towns around the Baltic.
- **Explanation:** Spread with the eastern settlement and the Hanse.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ludwigsburg Central Office
<a id="ludwigsburg-central-office"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1958–
- **Short:** West German office set up in 1958 to investigate Nazi crimes.
- **Explanation:** The Central Office of the State Justice Administrations for the Investigation of National Socialist Crimes, based in Ludwigsburg near Stuttgart. Its founding in 1958 ended a period of very limited prosecution of Nazi perpetrators.
- **German:** Zentrale Stelle der Landesjustizverwaltungen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Lutheranism
<a id="lutheranism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Lutheran · Lutherans
- **Short:** The Protestant church and teaching that follows Martin Luther.
- **Explanation:** Legalised alongside Catholicism in 1555; its creed is the Augsburg Confession. In Lutheran territories the prince headed the church and pastors became state-appointed officials.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Lütten Klein
<a id="lutten-klein"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2019
- **Short:** Steffen Mau's 2019 book on East German society, named after a Rostock housing estate.
- **Explanation:** Uses the estate where Mau grew up to explain East Germans' experiences of transformation after 1990 and their sense of devaluation.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: one side of the debate on causes of East German grievance.

### Lützow Free Corps
<a id="lutzow-free-corps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1813–1814
- **Short:** Volunteer corps against Napoleon whose black-red-gold uniforms gave Germany its colours.
- **Explanation:** Its black uniforms with red trim and gold buttons inspired the colours of the Jena student fraternity (1815) and later the national flag.
- **German:** Lützowsches Freikorps
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Luxembourg Agreement
<a id="luxembourg-agreement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1952
- **Short:** 1952 agreement in which West Germany agreed to pay compensation to Israel.
- **Explanation:** It was an early step in West Germany's material restitution for the Holocaust.
- **German:** Luxemburger Abkommen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Luxembourg dynasty
<a id="luxembourg-dynasty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** Luxembourgs
- **Short:** Dynasty that provided several emperors in the 14th–15th centuries, based in Bohemia.
- **Explanation:** Like later Habsburgs, the Luxembourg emperors were powerful because of their own dynastic lands (Bohemia), not imperial institutions.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## M

### Maastricht
<a id="maastricht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1992
- **Short:** The 1992 Maastricht Treaty that founded the European Union and planned the euro.
- **Explanation:** Treaty signed in the Dutch city of Maastricht, deepening European integration. Germany's commitment to it helped reassure neighbours worried about a united Germany.
- **German:** Vertrag von Maastricht
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Maastricht Treaty
<a id="maastricht-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1992
- **Also written as:** Maastricht
- **Ambiguous forms:** Maastricht (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 1992 treaty founding the European Union and planning the euro.
- **Explanation:** Signed as united Germany committed to deeper integration, partly to reassure neighbours. In 1993 the Constitutional Court's 'Maastricht' ruling asserted its right to review EU powers.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** 'Maastricht 1993' in the KB refers to the Constitutional Court's ruling on the treaty.

### Magdeburg Ivories
<a id="magdeburg-ivories"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Ottonian ivory panels (c. 970–980) made for Otto I's archbishopric of Magdeburg.
- **Explanation:** Sacred art advertising a Christian, Roman-style empire ruled through the Church.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Magdeburg law
<a id="magdeburg-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** Town law of Magdeburg adopted by hundreds of towns in central and eastern Europe.
- **Explanation:** A leading example of legal transfer during the Ostsiedlung.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### magnates
<a id="magnates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Great aristocrats — the most powerful landholding nobles.
- **Explanation:** Carolingian kings ruled in cooperation with magnates, who attended royal assemblies and dominated local power.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Magyars
<a id="magyars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Magyar
- **Short:** Hungarian horseback raiders who attacked East Francia in the 9th–10th centuries.
- **Explanation:** Their raids forced regional leaders to organise defence, strengthening the dukes.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mainz Information Bureau
<a id="mainz-information-bureau"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1833
- **Short:** Secret-police office of 1833 coordinating spies against opposition in the Confederation.
- **Explanation:** Set up after the Hambach Festival and other unrest, it coordinated informers across German states — part of Metternich's surveillance system.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Maji Maji war
<a id="maji-maji-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1905–1907
- **Also written as:** Maji Maji
- **Short:** Uprising in German East Africa crushed with famine as a weapon.
- **Explanation:** Estimates of deaths reach up to ~200,000–300,000, but are uncertain.
- **German:** Maji-Maji-Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** Death-toll estimates uncertain.

### Malicious Practices Law
<a id="malicious-practices-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1934
- **Short:** 1934 law punishing critical remarks and jokes about the regime.
- **Explanation:** It made 'malicious' criticism of the state or party a crime, so even a joke could lead to prosecution.
- **German:** Heimtückegesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Malmö armistice
<a id="malmo-armistice"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Short:** September 1848 truce between Prussia and Denmark over Schleswig.
- **Explanation:** Prussia signed it without the Frankfurt Assembly, which had to accept it — exposing that the Assembly had no power of its own.
- **German:** Waffenstillstand von Malmö
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### mandatory currency exchange
<a id="mandatory-currency-exchange"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** GDR rule forcing Western visitors to exchange a set sum of D-Marks per day.
- **Explanation:** Zwangsumtausch: a source of hard currency for the GDR and an obstacle to family visits.
- **German:** Zwangsumtausch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### manor
<a id="manor"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** manors · Villikation
- **Short:** Large estate where dependent peasants worked the lord's land as well as their own.
- **Explanation:** In the early medieval manor (Villikation), peasants held a farm (Hufe) and owed labour on the lord's demesne — for unfree tenants up to three days a week — plus dues in kind.
- **German:** Villikation
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/03_medieval_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### manslaughter
<a id="manslaughter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Unlawful killing without the intent that defines murder.
- **Explanation:** Charge on which, after 1990, German courts convicted GDR border guards and political leaders, including Egon Krenz and Günter Schabowski, for deaths at the border.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### March ministries
<a id="march-ministries"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Also written as:** March ministry
- **Short:** Liberal governments appointed by frightened princes in March 1848.
- **Explanation:** Examples include the Camphausen–Hansemann ministry in Prussia. They lifted censorship and promised constitutions, but were dismissed as the crowns recovered.
- **German:** Märzministerien
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### March Revolution
<a id="march-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Also written as:** Märzrevolution · March 1848 · 1848 revolution
- **Short:** Wave of uprisings across the German states in March 1848.
- **Explanation:** Triggered by the Paris February revolution: petitions, mass meetings and street fighting forced princes to appoint liberal ministers, lift censorship and promise constitutions, and led to the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **German:** Märzrevolution
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Marcomanni
<a id="marcomanni"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Germanic-speaking group that formed a large, short-lived confederation under Maroboduus.
- **Explanation:** A group that moved into Bohemia (today's Czech Republic) around the turn of the era. Under Maroboduus they led one of the early, short-lived confederations.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### margrave
<a id="margrave"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Short:** Ruler of a border province (a 'march'); ranked like a duke.
- **Explanation:** The margrave of Brandenburg was one of the seven prince-electors.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mark
<a id="mark"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** from 1873
- **Also written as:** mark
- **Short:** German currency introduced in 1873.
- **Explanation:** Unification brought a uniform currency, the Mark, replacing the states' currencies.
- **German:** Mark
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Marriage loan
<a id="marriage-loan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** marriage loans
- **Short:** State loan to newlyweds from 1933, conditional on the wife leaving her job.
- **Explanation:** Designed to take women out of the labour market and raise the birth rate; the amount owed was reduced with each child born.
- **German:** Ehestandsdarlehen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Marriage of iron and rye
<a id="marriage-of-iron-and-rye"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1879
- **Also written as:** marriage of iron and rye
- **Short:** Alliance of heavy industry and East Elbian grain producers behind the 1879 tariffs.
- **Explanation:** It united industrial and agrarian elites around protectionism.
- **German:** Bündnis von Eisen und Roggen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Marshall Plan
<a id="marshall-plan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1948–1952
- **Also written as:** ERP
- **Short:** US aid programme for rebuilding Western Europe after the Second World War.
- **Explanation:** American European Recovery Program. For West Germany it mattered for foreign currency and confidence, but it was modest compared with the economy's size; most economic historians give it only a limited direct role in the 'economic miracle'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: its role in the economic miracle is debated (most see it as limited).

### Martial law
<a id="martial-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** martial law
- **Short:** Military rule replacing ordinary law in an emergency.
- **Explanation:** The 1848 Basic Rights abolished the death penalty except under martial law.
- **German:** Kriegsrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Marxism
<a id="marxism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Marxist · anti-Marxism
- **Short:** Socialist theory of Karl Marx; in Nazi use, a label for both SPD and KPD.
- **Explanation:** Anti-Marxism was central to Nazi appeal; Marxist theories of fascism, in turn, saw Nazism as an instrument of capitalism.
- **German:** Marxismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Marxism-Leninism
<a id="marxism-leninism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Marxist-Leninist
- **Short:** Soviet form of Marxism: a vanguard party leads the working class to communism.
- **Explanation:** Official state ideology of the GDR and the Soviet bloc. It added to Marx's theory Lenin's idea of a disciplined party leading society, justifying the SED's monopoly of power.
- **German:** Marxismus-Leninismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Masonic lodges
<a id="masonic-lodges"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Freemasons' clubs, meeting places for enlightened nobles and educated townspeople.
- **Explanation:** With reading societies and journals, the lodges formed part of the network through which Enlightenment ideas spread across Germany's many small states.
- **German:** Freimaurerlogen
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### mass organisations
<a id="mass-organisations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** State-controlled organisations for youth, workers, women and others in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Bodies such as the Free German Youth and the Free German Trade Union Federation, which organised people's lives under SED guidance and held fixed seats in the Volkskammer.
- **German:** Massenorganisationen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mass strike
<a id="mass-strike"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** mass strikes · mass strike
- **Short:** Strike by very many workers across trades as a political weapon.
- **Explanation:** Rosa Luxemburg advocated it; the 1905 Ruhr strike was a big example.
- **German:** Massenstreik
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### massacre of Verden
<a id="massacre-of-verden"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 782
- **Short:** Charlemagne's execution of Saxons at Verden in 782 during the conquest of Saxony.
- **Explanation:** The single source reports 4,500 victims; historians question the number because of textual problems and propaganda purpose, but the violence of the conquest is not in doubt.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: the scale (4,500) rests on one source and is disputed.

### Matricular contributions
<a id="matricular-contributions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1871–1918
- **Also written as:** matricular contributions
- **Short:** Payments from the federal states to fund the Reich.
- **Explanation:** The Reich lived on customs, indirect taxes and these contributions; direct taxes belonged to the states.
- **German:** Matrikularbeiträge
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### May Laws
<a id="may-laws"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1873
- **Short:** Prussian laws of 1873 bringing clergy training and appointments under state control.
- **Explanation:** Central to the Kulturkampf; resistance led to imprisoned or exiled priests and bishops.
- **German:** Maigesetze
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### mediatisation
<a id="mediatisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1803–1806
- **Also written as:** mediatised
- **Short:** Placing formerly independent small territories under larger states.
- **Explanation:** About 45 imperial cities, imperial knights and counts lost self-rule in 1803–06 but kept some privileges.
- **German:** Mediatisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Mefo bills
<a id="mefo-bills"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** IOUs used to secretly finance Nazi rearmament.
- **Explanation:** They let the regime hide the scale of rearmament spending.
- **German:** Mefo-Wechsel
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Meldepflicht
<a id="meldepflicht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Legal duty to register one's home address with the local authorities.
- **Explanation:** Everyone living in Germany must register their residence, a typical everyday contact between citizen and state.
- **German:** Meldepflicht
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Meldungen aus dem Reich
<a id="meldungen-aus-dem-reich"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** 'Reports from the Reich': secret SS surveillance reports on public mood.
- **Explanation:** Compiled by the SD, they show approval of Hitler, grumbling about officials and mixed reactions to violence against Jews.
- **German:** Meldungen aus dem Reich
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### MEMO studies
<a id="memo-studies"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Survey series measuring how Germans remember the Nazi past.
- **Explanation:** The Multidimensional Remembrance Monitor. It finds broad support for remembrance but also a tendency to believe one's own ancestors were helpers or victims rather than perpetrators.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### memory culture
<a id="memory-culture"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Memory culture
- **Short:** The public practice of remembering Nazi crimes, central to German identity.
- **Explanation:** German 'Erinnerungskultur': memorials, education and rituals of remembrance of the Nazi past. Surveys show broad acceptance but also a tendency to see one's own ancestors as helpers or victims rather than perpetrators; its meaning in a diverse society is debated.
- **German:** Erinnerungskultur
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### mercantilism
<a id="mercantilism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Economic policy boosting a state's wealth through exports, manufactures and trade surpluses.
- **Explanation:** Its German form, cameralism, focused on raising the prince's revenue by promoting population, manufactures and agriculture.
- **German:** Merkantilismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Merovingians
<a id="merovingians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** c. 480–751
- **Also written as:** Merovingian
- **Short:** First Frankish royal dynasty (c. 480–751), deposed by the Carolingians.
- **Explanation:** Under the Merovingian kings the Franks took over the former Roman provinces and then gradually the lands beyond the Rhine. In 751 the Carolingians deposed them with papal approval.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Metropolitan Museum of Art
<a id="metropolitan-museum-of-art"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** The Metropolitan Museum of Art
- **Short:** Major art museum in New York whose open-access images appear in this KB.
- **Explanation:** Its public-domain (CC0) collection supplies images such as Caspar David Friedrich's 'Two Men Contemplating the Moon'.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)

### Middle Ages
<a id="middle-ages"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Also written as:** early Middle Ages
- **Short:** The period of European history between antiquity and the early modern age, roughly 500–1500.
- **Explanation:** In the KB's German history it runs from the Frankish kingdoms through the Holy Roman Empire to the eve of the Reformation. The early Middle Ages are the Frankish and Carolingian centuries.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)

### middle states
<a id="middle-states"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Medium-sized German states (Bavaria, Württemberg, Baden) enlarged under Napoleon.
- **Explanation:** They became sovereign, centralised and bureaucratic, and remained important between Austria and Prussia in the 19th century.
- **German:** Mittelstaaten
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)

- **Basis:** KB

### Mietskaserne
<a id="mietskaserne"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Mietskasernen · mietskaserne
- **Short:** 'Rental barracks': dense Berlin tenement blocks around dark courtyards.
- **Explanation:** Built to house millions of workers moving to industrial cities; crowded and poorly sanitised.
- **German:** Mietskaserne
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)

- **Basis:** KB

### milieu
<a id="milieu"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** milieus
- **Short:** A social world with its own organisations and loyalties, e.g. Catholic or socialist.
- **Explanation:** Weimar voters largely stayed loyal to their milieu's party: Catholics to the Centre, organised workers to the SPD or KPD — a ceiling for the Nazi vote.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### militant democracy
<a id="militant-democracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A democracy's right to defend itself by banning or monitoring enemies of the constitution.
- **Explanation:** An anti-Weimar principle: parties can be banned, but only by the Constitutional Court and under high thresholds (the NPD was not banned in 2017), and extremists are monitored by domestic intelligence. It frames debates on an AfD ban.
- **German:** wehrhafte Demokratie
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### militarism
<a id="militarism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** militaristic · militarist
- **Short:** Dominance of military values and institutions in society and politics.
- **Explanation:** Prussia's crown–Junker–army bargain is often seen as its root; whether Prussian militarism explains later German catastrophes is debated.
- **German:** Militarismus
- **Main chapter:** [`themes/militarism.md`](../themes/militarism.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Military cabinet
<a id="military-cabinet"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** military cabinet
- **Short:** The Kaiser's personal office for army appointments, outside parliamentary control.
- **Explanation:** It was part of the army's independence from ministerial and parliamentary oversight.
- **German:** Militärkabinett
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### military entrepreneurs
<a id="military-entrepreneurs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Military entrepreneurs
- **Short:** Commanders who raised and ran armies as private businesses for rulers.
- **Explanation:** Wallenstein and Mansfeld financed armies through 'contributions' from occupied territories — an extreme form of privatised violence. Some rose to great wealth.
- **German:** Kriegsunternehmer
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### military instruction
<a id="military-instruction"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1978–1989
- **Short:** Compulsory military education in GDR schools from 1978.
- **Explanation:** Wehrunterricht, introduced despite church protests, part of the militarised education of the late GDR.
- **German:** Wehrunterricht
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Military Service Modernisation Act
<a id="military-service-modernisation-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2025
- **Short:** 2025 law keeping service voluntary but requiring questionnaires and medical screening for young men.
- **Explanation:** Passed by the Bundestag on 5 December 2025 (323–272). 18-year-old men must fill in a questionnaire, men born from 2008 face compulsory medical screening, and parliament may activate compulsory service by separate law.
- **German:** Wehrdienstmodernisierungsgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### mini-jobs
<a id="mini-jobs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Small, low-paid jobs with reduced taxes and social contributions.
- **Explanation:** Marginal employment with low monthly earnings, largely exempt from tax and social contributions. The Hartz reforms expanded them, adding to low-wage and insecure work.
- **German:** Minijobs
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### minimum wage
<a id="minimum-wage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 2015–
- **Also written as:** statutory minimum wage
- **Short:** Legal lowest hourly pay, introduced in Germany only in 2015.
- **Explanation:** Before 2015 wages were set only by collective bargaining. The statutory minimum wage was a major change for low-wage workers.
- **German:** Mindestlohn
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Minister-president
<a id="minister-president"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** Minister-President · minister-president
- **Short:** Head of a state government, e.g. of Prussia.
- **Explanation:** Bismarck was appointed Prussian minister-president in 1862; the Reich Chancellor was usually also Prussian Minister-President.
- **German:** Ministerpräsident
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ministeriales
<a id="ministeriales"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Unfree administrators and warriors who rose into the lower nobility in the 12th–13th centuries.
- **Explanation:** A distinctly German phenomenon and a path of social mobility through service.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### mint rights
<a id="mint-rights"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** The right to strike coins and profit from it.
- **Explanation:** A source of royal and later princely revenue in the Empire.
- **German:** Münzrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### missi dominici
<a id="missi-dominici"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Short:** 'Lord's envoys': pairs of royal inspectors, usually a bishop and a count.
- **Explanation:** Sent out by Carolingian kings to check on local officials and enforce royal decrees.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Mitläufer
<a id="mitlaufer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Followers
- **Ambiguous forms:** Followers (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 'Follower': denazification category IV for nominal Nazis.
- **Explanation:** Denazification category IV covered nominal party members and supporters who received light penalties. The label came to stand for the many Germans who went along with the regime.
- **German:** Mitläufer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mitläuferfabrik
<a id="mitlauferfabrik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** follower factories
- **Short:** 'Follower factory': mocking name for denazification tribunals.
- **Explanation:** Critics mocked the denazification tribunals as 'follower factories' because they classified so many people as mere followers and thus cleared them.
- **German:** Mitläuferfabrik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mittelstand
<a id="mittelstand"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** The lower middle class: artisans, shopkeepers, small farmers.
- **Explanation:** Fearful of big capital and socialism, it was susceptible to antisemitic and protectionist politics.
- **German:** Mittelstand
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### mixed constitution
<a id="mixed-constitution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A political order in which power is shared among monarch, nobles and others.
- **Explanation:** Gotthard uses it for the Holy Roman Empire, whose emperor, electors, princes and cities shared power.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mixed marriage bans
<a id="mixed-marriage-bans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1905
- **Also written as:** mixed marriages
- **Short:** Bans on marriages between Germans and colonised people.
- **Explanation:** Imposed in South West Africa (1905), East Africa and Samoa.
- **German:** Mischehenverbot
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### mixed-member proportional representation
<a id="mixed-member-proportional-representation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Electoral system combining constituency seats with party-list seats so parliament mirrors the vote.
- **Explanation:** The Bundestag's voting system: part of the members are elected in local constituencies and the rest from party lists, with seats balanced so that each party's share roughly matches its share of votes.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### mixed-member proportional system
<a id="mixed-member-proportional-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Electoral system combining constituency seats with party-list seats to give proportional results.
- **Explanation:** Germans cast one vote for a local candidate and one for a party list; the party vote determines each party's share of Bundestag seats.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Modell Deutschland
<a id="modell-deutschland"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1970s
- **Short:** 'Model Germany': West Germany's export-oriented, consensus-based economic model.
- **Explanation:** Slogan for the West German combination of export-strong manufacturing (cars, machinery, chemicals), social partnership and a stable currency, presented in the 1970s as a model for others.
- **German:** Modell Deutschland
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Modernist theories
<a id="modernist-theories"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Theories holding that nations are products of modernity, print and the state.
- **Explanation:** Associated with Gellner, Anderson and Hobsbawm; applied to Germany by Otto Dann and Langewiesche. Critics like Caspar Hirschi point to older roots.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: pre-modern roots of national identity are debated.

### Monday demonstrations
<a id="monday-demonstrations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Short:** Weekly mass protests in Leipzig in autumn 1989 that brought down the SED regime.
- **Explanation:** They grew out of Monday peace prayers at Leipzig's Nikolaikirche. On 9 October 1989 about 70,000 people marched and the security forces did not shoot — the turning point of the Peaceful Revolution. Their chant 'We are the people' later became 'We are one people'.
- **German:** Montagsdemonstrationen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Monetary, Economic and Social Union
<a id="monetary-economic-and-social-union"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Also written as:** currency union · Currency union
- **Short:** 1 July 1990 introduction of the D-Mark and West German economic rules in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Wages, salaries and pensions were converted at 1:1, savings at 1:1 up to limits, otherwise 2:1. It boosted purchasing power, but with low productivity and collapsing East European markets it made much GDR industry uncompetitive overnight.
- **German:** Währungs-, Wirtschafts- und Sozialunion
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether the conversion rate and speed were avoidable costs is debated.

### money creation
<a id="money-creation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** printing money
- **Short:** The state producing new money, e.g. by printing banknotes, to pay its bills.
- **Explanation:** Used to finance WWI and later the 1923 passive resistance in the Ruhr, it drove the inflation and then the hyperinflation that wiped out savings.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### money rent
<a id="money-rent"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** money rents
- **Short:** Rent paid in cash instead of labour services.
- **Explanation:** Labour services were increasingly commuted to money rents as the money economy spread.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Moscow Treaty
<a id="moscow-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1970
- **Short:** 1970 treaty between West Germany and the Soviet Union declaring post-war borders inviolable.
- **Explanation:** First Ostpolitik treaty. Borders were called 'inviolable' (not to be changed by force), not 'immutable', leaving peaceful change — such as unification — possible.
- **German:** Moskauer Vertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Mother's Cross
<a id="mother-s-cross"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Nazi medal for mothers of many children.
- **Explanation:** Part of the regime's ideology of motherhood and its drive to raise the birth rate among 'Aryan' Germans.
- **German:** Mutterkreuz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### multiculturalism
<a id="multiculturalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Idea that different cultural groups should live together while keeping their own identities.
- **Explanation:** In German debates it is set against integration, assimilation and the idea of a 'Leitkultur'.
- **German:** Multikulturalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Municipal Ordinance
<a id="municipal-ordinance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1808
- **Short:** Prussian law of 1808 creating elected town councils.
- **Explanation:** It gave self-government to property-owning citizens.
- **German:** Städteordnung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Munt
<a id="munt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Medieval guardianship: a man's legal authority over women and dependants in his household.
- **Explanation:** Under it women were legally subordinate, even though they played real economic roles on farms, in crafts and in trade.
- **German:** Munt
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Musterung
<a id="musterung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Compulsory medical screening of potential military recruits.
- **Explanation:** Reintroduced by the 2025 Military Service Modernisation Act for men born from 2008.
- **German:** Musterung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB

## N

### Nama
<a id="nama"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** People of southern Namibia, victims of genocide in 1904–08.
- **Explanation:** Von Trotha's order of April 1905 targeted them.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Napoleonic Wars
<a id="napoleonic-wars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1803–1815
- **Also written as:** Napoleonic wars · Napoleonic war
- **Short:** Wars between Napoleon's France and European coalitions that reshaped Germany.
- **Explanation:** French occupation and the wars of liberation of 1813–15 gave German nationalism an anti-French, sometimes ethnic edge.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Nathan the Wise
<a id="nathan-the-wise"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1779
- **Short:** Lessing's 1779 play pleading for tolerance between Jews, Christians and Muslims.
- **Explanation:** It expresses the idea that truth can be found in all religions.
- **German:** Nathan der Weise
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National Assembly
<a id="national-assembly"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1919–1920
- **Short:** Parliament elected in January 1919 to write the Weimar constitution.
- **Explanation:** Women voted and stood for election for the first time: 37 of 423 deputies were women (about 9%).
- **German:** Nationalversammlung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### national conservatism
<a id="national-conservatism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** National conservatism
- **Short:** Right-wing politics combining nationalism with authoritarian, monarchist traditions.
- **Explanation:** Carried by the DNVP, it was hostile to the republic, especially after Hugenberg radicalised the party in 1928.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National Democratic Party of Germany
<a id="national-democratic-party-of-germany"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1964–
- **Also written as:** NPD
- **Short:** Far-right party the Constitutional Court declined to ban in 2017 as too insignificant.
- **Explanation:** A neo-Nazi-leaning party. In 2017 the Constitutional Court found it anti-constitutional but too insignificant to ban; in 2024 it was excluded from state party financing — a precedent in the debate on banning the AfD.
- **German:** Nationaldemokratische Partei Deutschlands (NPD)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National Front
<a id="national-front"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1949–1990
- **Short:** GDR umbrella organisation of all parties and mass organisations under SED leadership.
- **Explanation:** Alliance through which the SED controlled the other 'bloc parties' and mass organisations, presenting them to voters on a single 'unity list'.
- **German:** Nationale Front
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National Liberals
<a id="national-liberals"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1867–1918
- **Short:** Liberal party that backed Bismarck after 1866, prioritising unity and economic freedom.
- **Explanation:** Formed from liberals who accepted the Indemnity Act; they belonged to the liberal-Protestant bourgeois milieu.
- **German:** Nationalliberale Partei
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### National People's Army
<a id="national-people-s-army"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1956–1990
- **Also written as:** NVA
- **Short:** The GDR's armed forces, part of the Warsaw Pact; conscription from 1962.
- **Explanation:** The Nationale Volksarmee (NVA), founded in 1956, the year after West Germany's Bundeswehr. It was dissolved at unification in 1990. A party army controlled by the ruling SED, alongside paramilitary youth training in the GDR.
- **German:** Nationale Volksarmee (NVA)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National Socialism
<a id="national-socialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1920–1945
- **Also written as:** Nazis · Nazism · Nazi
- **Ambiguous forms:** Nazis · Nazi (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Nazism: Hitler's ideology of racial antisemitism, national community and conquest.
- **Explanation:** Its core ideas were racial antisemitism, the Volksgemeinschaft, Lebensraum, anti-Marxism, the leader principle, revision of Versailles and national rebirth.
- **German:** Nationalsozialismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### National System of Political Economy
<a id="national-system-of-political-economy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1841
- **Short:** Friedrich List's 1841 book arguing for protective tariffs to build national industry.
- **Explanation:** It argued that Germany needed tariffs to catch up with industrial Britain, linking economic development to national unity.
- **German:** Das nationale System der politischen Ökonomie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### nationalisation
<a id="nationalisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Taking private companies into state ownership.
- **Explanation:** In the Soviet zone and GDR, industry was expropriated and nationalised after a 1946 referendum in Saxony, creating the state-owned 'people's enterprises' (VEB).
- **German:** Verstaatlichung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Nationalism
<a id="nationalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** nationalism · nationalist · nationalists
- **Short:** The belief that a people sharing language and culture should form one state.
- **Explanation:** In Germany it began around 1800 as an idea of educated minorities, became a liberal opposition movement before 1848 and turned conservative, state-centred and imperialist after 1871.
- **German:** Nationalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Nationalverein
<a id="nationalverein"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1859–1867
- **Also written as:** National Association
- **Short:** Liberal association (1859) campaigning for German unity under Prussia without Austria.
- **Explanation:** It showed liberal nationalism turning to Prussia as the engine of unity.
- **German:** Nationalverein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### NATO
<a id="nato"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1949–
- **Short:** Western military alliance led by the USA; West Germany joined in 1955.
- **Explanation:** North Atlantic Treaty Organization. West Germany's entry in 1955 tied it militarily to the West. In 1990 the Two-plus-Four Treaty allowed united Germany to stay in NATO, with restrictions on the former GDR; whether the West promised not to expand NATO eastward is contested.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### NATO expansion assurances
<a id="nato-expansion-assurances"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Also written as:** not one inch
- **Short:** Contested question whether the West promised in 1990 not to expand NATO eastward.
- **Explanation:** Verbal assurances were made in talks (e.g. James Baker's 'not one inch' in February 1990), but there was no written commitment. Mary Elise Sarotte documents the exchanges; interpretations remain politically charged and are used by Russia.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: existence and meaning of the assurances are debated.

### natural law
<a id="natural-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Rationalist natural law
- **Short:** Idea that reason can derive universal laws and rights independent of religion or custom.
- **Explanation:** After the religious wars, thinkers like Pufendorf, Thomasius and Wolff used it to argue for toleration and rational government.
- **German:** Naturrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Naval race
<a id="naval-race"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1898–1912
- **Also written as:** naval race
- **Short:** Competition between Germany and Britain to build battleships.
- **Explanation:** It worsened relations with Britain before 1914.
- **German:** Flottenwettrüsten
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Navy League
<a id="navy-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1898–1934
- **Short:** Mass lobby for the battle fleet, with ~1 million members.
- **Explanation:** It mobilised public support for naval expansion.
- **German:** Deutscher Flottenverein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Nazi student league
<a id="nazi-student-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1926–1945
- **Short:** The Nazi party's student organisation, which won university elections before 1933.
- **Explanation:** Its election victories at universities before 1933 show how widely nationalism and Nazi enthusiasm had spread among students.
- **German:** Nationalsozialistischer Deutscher Studentenbund (NSDStB)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Nazi-Soviet pact
<a id="nazi-soviet-pact"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1939
- **Short:** August 1939 non-aggression pact between Hitler and Stalin.
- **Explanation:** A secret protocol divided Eastern Europe into spheres; it cleared the way for the invasion of Poland.
- **German:** Hitler-Stalin-Pakt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### NBER
<a id="nber"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** National Bureau of Economic Research, a US economic research organisation.
- **Explanation:** It published the study by Galofré-Vilà and colleagues linking austerity to Nazi vote gains.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)

### NDPD
<a id="ndpd"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1948–1990
- **Short:** National Democratic Party of Germany, an East German bloc party for former nominal Nazis.
- **Explanation:** Founded in 1948 in the Soviet zone to integrate former nominal Nazis and officers into the new system under the SED's control, after denazification was declared complete.
- **German:** National-Demokratische Partei Deutschlands
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Negative integration
<a id="negative-integration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** negative integration
- **Short:** Holding society together by uniting it against supposed internal enemies; or workers' separate counter-society.
- **Explanation:** Wehler used it for mobilising against 'enemies of the Reich'; Guenther Roth (1963) for workers excluded from state power but integrated into their own SPD world.
- **German:** negative Integration
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### negative majority
<a id="negative-majority"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1932
- **Short:** A parliamentary majority made up of parties that reject the system and cannot govern together.
- **Explanation:** In July 1932 the NSDAP and KPD together held a majority, making parliamentary government impossible.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### neo-Nazis
<a id="neo-nazis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Post-1945 groups reviving Nazi ideas and symbols.
- **Explanation:** The KB lists them with the AfD as carriers of radical-right ethnic nationalism in reunited Germany; racist attacks such as those in Mölln and Solingen and the NSU murders came from this milieu.
- **German:** Neonazis
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Neohumanism
<a id="neohumanism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Educational ideal based on classical Greek and Roman culture and self-formation.
- **Explanation:** Wilhelm von Humboldt's version led to the University of Berlin (1810) and the humanist gymnasium.
- **German:** Neuhumanismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### net contributor
<a id="net-contributor"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** A member state that pays more into the EU budget than it receives.
- **Explanation:** Germany is the largest net contributor to the EU budget.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Neues Forum
<a id="neues-forum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1989–
- **Short:** 'New Forum': leading East German citizens' movement founded in September 1989.
- **Explanation:** Founded by Bärbel Bohley, Jens Reich and others to open a public dialogue on reform; it became the largest opposition movement of autumn 1989 and later joined Alliance 90.
- **German:** Neues Forum
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New Building
<a id="new-building"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1920s
- **Short:** Modernist architecture movement of the 1920s, known for public housing estates.
- **Explanation:** Its housing estates were part of Weimar's social reforms and cultural modernity.
- **German:** Neues Bauen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New Course
<a id="new-course"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1890–1894
- **Short:** Policy after Bismarck's dismissal that sought to win workers with labour protection.
- **Explanation:** Brought industrial courts, Sunday rest and child labour limits.
- **German:** Neuer Kurs
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`

### New Economic System
<a id="new-economic-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1963–1970
- **Short:** Ulbricht's 1960s reform giving GDR enterprises more autonomy and profit incentives.
- **Explanation:** Partial decentralisation of the planned economy, allowing some market-like incentives; it was ended by Honecker.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New Era
<a id="new-era"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1858–1862
- **Short:** Liberal thaw in Prussia under the regency of the future Wilhelm I.
- **Explanation:** Liberals revived and the National Association campaigned for Prussian-led unity — until the constitutional conflict of 1862.
- **German:** Neue Ära
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### new federal states
<a id="new-federal-states"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 1990–
- **Also written as:** neue Bundesländer · new Länder
- **Short:** The five eastern states formed from the GDR's territory in 1990.
- **Explanation:** Brandenburg, Mecklenburg-Vorpommern, Saxony, Saxony-Anhalt and Thuringia, re-created when the GDR joined the Federal Republic. With unified Berlin they raised the number of Länder to 16. Three decades on they still lag the West in wages and wealth.
- **German:** neue Bundesländer
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
