- **Basis:** KB+K

### new intelligentsia
<a id="new-intelligentsia"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Dates:** 1950s–1960s
- **Short:** GDR professional elite recruited from workers' and peasants' children.
- **Explanation:** Educated class created by party-controlled education to replace the old elites, producing high social mobility in the 1950s–60s that closed down by the 1970s–80s.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New Left
<a id="new-left"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1960s–1970s
- **Short:** Left-wing student and intellectual movement of the 1960s, critical of both capitalism and Soviet communism.
- **Explanation:** Current of the 1968 generation that combined anti-authoritarianism, Marxist theory and protest against the Vietnam War and old hierarchies. Parts of it later turned to dogmatism or, on a small fringe, to terrorism.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### new social movements
<a id="new-social-movements"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1970s–1980s
- **Short:** The environmental, women's and peace movements of 1970s–80s West Germany.
- **Explanation:** Grassroots citizens' movements outside the established parties, focused on the environment, women's rights and peace. They transformed West German society and gave rise to the Greens.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New teachers
<a id="new-teachers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** new teachers
- **Short:** Rapidly trained replacement teachers in the Soviet zone.
- **Explanation:** In the Soviet zone many teachers were dismissed as Nazis and replaced by quickly trained 'new teachers', part of breaking the old elites.
- **German:** Neulehrer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### New Woman
<a id="new-woman"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1920s
- **Short:** Weimar-era image of the independent modern urban woman.
- **Explanation:** Associated with office work, fashion and sport in the cities; women's employment was nonetheless attacked during the Depression.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### niche society
<a id="niche-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Nischengesellschaft · niches
- **Short:** Description of GDR life as a public show of conformity with private retreats.
- **Explanation:** Term (Nischengesellschaft) for how many East Germans outwardly conformed while withdrawing into private 'niches' — family, friends, gardens, West TV — where the state intruded less.
- **German:** Nischengesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Night of the Long Knives
<a id="night-of-the-long-knives"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 30 June–2 July 1934
- **Short:** Hitler's murder of SA leaders and conservative opponents in 1934.
- **Explanation:** Röhm, SA leaders, Schleicher and Papen's aides were killed; the army leadership welcomed it, and Carl Schmitt justified it.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ninety-five Theses
<a id="ninety-five-theses"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1517
- **Also written as:** theses
- **Ambiguous forms:** theses (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Luther's 1517 propositions against indulgences, which launched the Reformation.
- **Explanation:** The famous image of Luther nailing them to a church door is debated; he sent them to his archbishop, and the posting became a symbol only later.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### NKVD
<a id="nkvd"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Soviet secret police and interior ministry.
- **Explanation:** The Soviet secret police ran 'special camps' in the Soviet zone after 1945, holding more than 150,000 Germans — Nazis, but also innocents and later political opponents.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### nomenklatura
<a id="nomenklatura"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Communist system in which the party approved appointments to all important posts.
- **Explanation:** List-based system of appointments: every significant position in state, economy and society was filled only with SED approval, ensuring the party controlled all elites.
- **German:** Nomenklatur
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Nord Stream 2
<a id="nord-stream-2"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Baltic Sea gas pipeline from Russia to Germany, abandoned after 2022.
- **Explanation:** Built despite objections from allies after Russia seized Crimea, it came to symbolise Germany's dependence on Russian gas. It never went into operation.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** Controversial: criticised as deepening energy dependence on Russia.

### normal year
<a id="normal-year"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1624
- **Short:** The year 1624, used in 1648 to fix who owned church property and where worship was allowed.
- **Explanation:** Minorities who had worshipped privately in 1624 kept that right, giving them some legal protection.
- **German:** Normaljahr
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### normative state
<a id="normative-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Normenstaat
- **Short:** In Fraenkel's model, the sphere where ordinary law still worked.
- **Explanation:** Courts, contracts and property law continued for ordinary affairs.
- **German:** Normenstaat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### North German Confederation
<a id="north-german-confederation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1867–1871
- **Short:** Prussian-led federal state north of the Main River, forerunner of the 1871 Empire.
- **Explanation:** It had a Reichstag elected by universal male suffrage and a Bundesrat, passed liberal economic laws, and completed Jewish legal emancipation (1869).
- **German:** Norddeutscher Bund
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Not One Inch
<a id="not-one-inch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2021
- **Short:** Mary Elise Sarotte's 2021 book on NATO expansion after the Cold War.
- **Explanation:** Study documenting the 1990 exchanges between Western and Soviet leaders about NATO's future, including James Baker's 'not one inch' remark.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether NATO non-enlargement assurances were 'given' in 1990 is debated.

### Notbischof
<a id="notbischof"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Emergency bishop': the Protestant prince acting as head of his territory's church.
- **Explanation:** Since Protestant lands had no Catholic bishops, the prince stepped in — at first supposedly as an emergency measure, in practice permanently.
- **German:** Notbischof
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### November criminals
<a id="november-criminals"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Nationalist slur for the politicians who ended the war and founded the republic in November 1918.
- **Explanation:** The right blamed them for defeat and the Versailles treaty. Together with the stab-in-the-back myth, the phrase fused nationalism with hatred of the republic, Marxism and Jews.
- **German:** Novemberverbrecher
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### November pogrom
<a id="november-pogrom"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 9–10 November 1938
- **Also written as:** pogrom · Novemberpogrom · 1938 pogrom · November 1938 pogrom
- **Ambiguous forms:** pogrom (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Nationwide violence against Jews on 9–10 November 1938: synagogues burned, shops wrecked.
- **Explanation:** About 30,000 Jewish men were sent to camps and at least 91 people were killed officially. Jews were then made to pay a 1 billion Reichsmark 'atonement' levy. It happened in full public view.
- **German:** Novemberpogrom
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### November Revolution
<a id="november-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1918–1919
- **Also written as:** November revolution · revolution of November 1918
- **Short:** The 1918 revolution that ended the German monarchy and created a republic.
- **Explanation:** It began as a sailors' mutiny against a suicidal naval sortie, spread through workers' and soldiers' councils led mainly by moderate socialists, and led to the Kaiser's abdication and the Weimar Republic. It was not a Bolshevik plot.
- **German:** Novemberrevolution
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### NS People's Welfare
<a id="ns-people-s-welfare"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** NSV
- **Short:** Nazi party welfare organisation that ran the Winter Relief collections.
- **Explanation:** The NSV provided welfare only to those considered racially 'valuable', combining charity with party control.
- **German:** Nationalsozialistische Volkswohlfahrt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### NS-Frauenschaft
<a id="ns-frauenschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** The Nazi party's women's organisation.
- **Explanation:** It spread the regime's ideal of women as mothers and homemakers and organised women's activities.
- **German:** NS-Frauenschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### NSDAP
<a id="nsdap"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1920–1945
- **Also written as:** Nazis · Nazi · Nazi-era · Nazi party
- **Ambiguous forms:** Nazis · Nazi (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The Nazi party (National Socialist German Workers' Party), led by Hitler.
- **Explanation:** A tiny Munich group in 1919–20, it rose from 2.6% (1928) to 37% (July 1932) but never won a free majority. From July 1933 it was the only legal party and one of the competing power centres of the dictatorship.
- **German:** Nationalsozialistische Deutsche Arbeiterpartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### NSU
<a id="nsu"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 2000–2011
- **Short:** Neo-Nazi terror group that murdered ten people across Germany (2000–07), uncovered 2011.
- **Explanation:** The 'National Socialist Underground', a right-wing terror cell. Its series of murders, mostly of people of immigrant origin, went unrecognised by the authorities until 2011, causing a major scandal.
- **German:** Nationalsozialistischer Untergrund
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)

### Nuremberg Chronicle
<a id="nuremberg-chronicle"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1493
- **Also written as:** Schedelsche Weltchronik
- **Short:** Illustrated world chronicle printed in Nuremberg in 1493.
- **Explanation:** Its woodcut city views show how imperial cities imagined themselves: walled, spired and self-governing.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)

### Nuremberg Laws
<a id="nuremberg-laws"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1935
- **Short:** 1935 racial laws that stripped German Jews of citizenship and banned marriages with non-Jews.
- **Explanation:** Consisting of the Reich Citizenship Law and the 'Law for the Protection of German Blood and Honour', they gave legal form to racial exclusion.
- **German:** Nürnberger Gesetze
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

### Nuremberg trials
<a id="nuremberg-trials"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1945–1949
- **Also written as:** Nuremberg Trials · International Military Tribunal
- **Short:** Allied trials of leading Nazis in Nuremberg, 1945–46, followed by 12 US trials.
- **Explanation:** The International Military Tribunal tried 22 major defendants and passed 12 death sentences, setting precedents for 'crimes against humanity'.

### Nuremberg–Fürth railway
<a id="nuremberg-furth-railway"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1835
- **Also written as:** Nuremberg–Fürth
- **Short:** The first German railway line, opened in 1835 between Nuremberg and Fürth in Bavaria.
- **Explanation:** A short line in Bavaria that marked the start of railway-led industrialisation. By 1850 Germany had about 6,000 km of track; railways pulled coal, iron and engineering along and, with the Customs Union, created a common market.
- **German:** Ludwigseisenbahn
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)

### occupation of the Ruhr
<a id="occupation-of-the-ruhr"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1923–1925
- **Short:** French and Belgian occupation of Germany's industrial Ruhr region from 1923.
- **Explanation:** The government answered with passive resistance financed by printing money, which turned inflation into hyperinflation.
- **German:** Ruhrbesetzung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### October Edict
<a id="october-edict"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1807
- **Short:** Prussian decree of 9 Oct 1807 abolishing hereditary subjection from 1810.
- **Explanation:** It also allowed free choice of occupation and free sale of land between nobles, burghers and peasants.
- **German:** Oktoberedikt
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### October reforms
<a id="october-reforms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** October 1918
- **Short:** Constitutional changes of October 1918 that made Germany a parliamentary monarchy.
- **Explanation:** From 28 October the chancellor needed the Reichstag's confidence and the military was subordinated to civilian government — reforms demanded by Ludendorff to shift responsibility for defeat.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### of the German Nation
<a id="of-the-german-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Nationis Germanicae
- **Short:** Addition to the Empire's name in the 15th century (Latin Nationis Germanicae).
- **Explanation:** It signalled both a German core and the loss of wider imperial claims.

### Office for National Security
<a id="office-for-national-security"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1989–1990
- **Short:** Short-lived renamed Stasi (Nov 1989), dissolved under public pressure.
- **Explanation:** Successor organisation to the Ministry for State Security under Modrow. Citizens' occupations of its offices and pressure from the Round Table led to its dissolution in 1989–90.
- **German:** Amt für Nationale Sicherheit

### officer corps
<a id="officer-corps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** The body of professional army officers, largely drawn from conservative elites.
- **Explanation:** Preserved by the Ebert–Groener pact, it remained monarchist or authoritarian, and in the Reichswehr formed a semi-independent force hostile to the republic.

### oil crisis
<a id="oil-crisis"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1973; 1979
- **Also written as:** oil shocks
- **Short:** Sharp rises in oil prices in 1973 and 1979 that ended the post-war boom.
- **Explanation:** When Arab oil producers cut supplies in 1973 (and again after the 1979 Iranian revolution), prices soared. In West Germany the crisis triggered the stop on recruiting guest workers and ushered in slower growth and rising unemployment.

- **Explanation:** It carried out Hitler's orders rather than acting as an independent command.

### oligarchy
<a id="oligarchy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** oligarchic · Oligarchies
- **Short:** Rule by a small group of the rich or powerful.
- **Explanation:** Medieval towns were oligarchies, sometimes broadened by guilds.

<a id="on-the-civil-improvement-of-the-jews"></a>

- **Dates:** 1781

<a id="on-the-jews-and-their-lies"></a>

### Open secret
<a id="open-secret"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** open secret
- **Short:** Something widely known but not openly talked about — here, the mass murder of the Jews.
- **Explanation:** Bajohr and Pohl argue news of the killings spread via soldiers' letters, men on leave, BBC broadcasts and rumour; many Germans knew enough, and chose not to know more.

- **Dates:** 22 June 1941
- **Also written as:** invasion of the Soviet Union in 1941 · Invasion of USSR · invasion of the Soviet Union
- **Short:** Germany's invasion of the Soviet Union on 22 June 1941.
- **Explanation:** Planned as a war of annihilation to conquer 'living space' in the East. With it began the systematic murder of Soviet Jews by the Einsatzgruppen and other units, and the mass deaths of Soviet prisoners of war. The campaign stalled before Moscow in 1941.

<a id="operation-reinhard"></a>

- **Dates:** 1942–1943
- **Short:** Nazi plan to murder the Jews of occupied Poland in camps, 1942–43.
- **Explanation:** At Bełżec, Sobibór and Treblinka about 1.7 million people were killed.

### option model
<a id="option-model"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2000–2024
- **Short:** Rule requiring German-born children of immigrants to choose one citizenship as young adults.
- **Explanation:** The 2000 reform gave such children German citizenship at birth (partial ius soli) but made them choose between German and their parents' nationality. The 2024 reform generally allowed dual citizenship.
- **German:** Optionsmodell

<a id="order-police"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Order Police battalions · police battalions
- **Short:** Germany's regular uniformed police, whose battalions took part in mass murder.
- **Explanation:** Order Police battalions shot or deported hundreds of thousands of Jews; Reserve Police Battalion 101 is the best-known example.
- **German:** Ordnungspolizei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

### Ordinary Men
<a id="ordinary-men"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1992
- **Also written as:** ordinary men
- **Ambiguous forms:** ordinary men (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Christopher Browning's 1992 study of a German police battalion that murdered Jews in Poland.
- **Explanation:** Browning showed that middle-aged reserve policemen, offered the chance not to shoot, mostly shot anyway — explaining participation mainly by situation, conformity and group pressure. Daniel Goldhagen instead stressed 'eliminationist antisemitism'. The phrase now stands for how ordinary people became perpetrators.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

### ordoliberalism
<a id="ordoliberalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Ordoliberal
- **Short:** German economic school stressing firm rules, competition and monetary stability.

### Organised capitalism
<a id="organised-capitalism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** organised capitalism
- **Short:** Capitalism shaped by cartels, big banks, interest groups and the state.
- **Explanation:** Describes the German economy c.1895–1914.
- **German:** organisierter Kapitalismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Origins of the Final Solution
<a id="origins-of-the-final-solution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2004
- **Short:** Christopher Browning's 2004 history of how the Nazi decision for genocide developed.
- **Explanation:** A standard account of Nazi Jewish policy 1939–42, recommended in the KB as the starting point for the intentionalism vs functionalism debate. It argues for a stepwise decision process driven by Hitler's authority and by radicalisation from below.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: how and when the Holocaust was decided is debated.

### Ostalgie
<a id="ostalgie"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 1990s–
- **Short:** Nostalgia for aspects of everyday life in the former GDR.
- **Explanation:** A blend of 'Ost' (east) and 'Nostalgie'. After the upheavals of the 1990s, many East Germans looked back fondly on GDR products and routines — less a wish for the dictatorship than a reaction to lost jobs and devaluation of their past.
- **German:** Ostalgie
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ostarbeiter
<a id="ostarbeiter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Eastern workers': Soviet civilians deported to Germany for forced labour.
- **Explanation:** They and Polish workers were branded with badges and treated worse than Western Europeans under the Nazi racial hierarchy.
- **German:** Ostarbeiter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Osthilfe
<a id="osthilfe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** 'Eastern aid': state subsidies to indebted estates in eastern Germany, which became a scandal.
- **Explanation:** Meant to rescue East Elbian agriculture in the farm crisis, it benefited large landowners and was mired in accusations of misuse.
- **German:** Osthilfe
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ostpolitik
<a id="ostpolitik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1969–1973
- **Short:** Brandt's policy of treaties and reconciliation with the GDR and Eastern Europe.
- **Explanation:** 'Eastern policy' that recognised post-war borders and the GDR's existence in practice while keeping the German question open. Whether it mainly stabilised or undermined the GDR is debated; it also reduced neighbours' fears of Germany.
- **German:** Neue Ostpolitik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: its weighting as stabilising or undermining the GDR is debated.

### Ostsiedlung
<a id="ostsiedlung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 12th–14th c.
- **Short:** Medieval German eastern settlement (12th–14th c.), often invited by Slavic princes.
- **Explanation:** Settlers received inheritable tenure, fixed rents and self-government; many towns got Magdeburg or Lübeck law. Nationalists later called it a 'Drang nach Osten'; research now sees a Europe-wide colonisation producing mixed societies.
- **German:** Ostsiedlung
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED historically: nationalist narratives vs current European-colonisation view.

### Ottonians
<a id="ottonians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 919–1024
- **Also written as:** Ottonian
- **Short:** Saxon royal dynasty (919–1024) that revived the western imperial title in 962.
- **Explanation:** Elected kings who ruled by consensus with dukes and through the Church. Len Scales argues their imperial turn first made a 'German people' visible.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### outlawry
<a id="outlawry"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** ban
- **Ambiguous forms:** ban (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Removing someone from the protection of the law.
- **Explanation:** The imperial ban placed a person or ruler outside the law; enforcing it still depended on the princes.
- **German:** Acht
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### own resources
<a id="own-resources"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Revenues flowing directly to the EU budget, such as customs duties.
- **Explanation:** Together with member-state contributions, they fund the EU budget.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## P

### Pan-European Picnic
<a id="pan-european-picnic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Short:** 19 August 1989 event at the Austro-Hungarian border where about 600 East Germans escaped.
- **Explanation:** Peace gathering on the Hungarian–Austrian border during which a gate was opened and East Germans crossed to Austria, signalling that the fortified East–West border (the 'Iron Curtain') was cracking.
- **German:** Paneuropäisches Picknick
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pan-German League
<a id="pan-german-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1891–1939
- **Short:** Radical nationalist pressure group founded in 1891.
- **Explanation:** It pushed the government toward aggressive expansion and was völkisch and antisemitic.
- **German:** Alldeutscher Verband
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pan-Germans
<a id="pan-germans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Radical nationalist lobby demanding large territorial annexations.
- **Explanation:** In WWI they were among the annexationist lobbies that opposed a negotiated peace.
- **German:** Alldeutscher Verband
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### panic of the middle class
<a id="panic-of-the-middle-class"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Older theory that frightened middle classes, e.g. white-collar staff, drove the Nazi vote.
- **Explanation:** Voting research found white-collar employees voted Nazi less than average, contrary to this theory.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### papacy
<a id="papacy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** pope · papal
- **Short:** The office and government of the pope, bishop of Rome and head of the Latin Church.
- **Explanation:** Its alliance with the Frankish kings (751, 800) tied German kingship to Rome. Its conflict with emperors, above all the Investiture Controversy (1075–1122), shaped the Empire for centuries.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Paragraph 175
<a id="paragraph-175"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1871–1994
- **Short:** Section of the German criminal code punishing homosexual acts between men.
- **Explanation:** The Nazis sharpened it in 1935; about 50,000 men were convicted and thousands sent to camps. It remained in force in West Germany long after 1945.
- **German:** Paragraph 175
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Paragraph 218
<a id="paragraph-218"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** §218
- **Short:** Section of the German criminal code making abortion a crime.
- **Explanation:** It remained in force in Weimar despite women's new political rights.
- **German:** §218
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### paramilitary leagues
<a id="paramilitary-leagues"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** paramilitaries
- **Short:** Uniformed party or veterans' fighting groups, such as the SA.
- **Explanation:** They fought in the streets and intimidated opponents, making political violence part of everyday Weimar life; hundreds were killed in street clashes in 1932.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### parental allowance
<a id="parental-allowance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2007–
- **Short:** State payment to parents who reduce work to care for a new baby, since 2007.
- **Explanation:** The 'Elterngeld' partly replaces lost income for parents after a birth and encourages fathers to take leave too.
- **German:** Elterngeld
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### parish
<a id="parish"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** parishes
- **Short:** Local church district with its own priest.
- **Explanation:** The parish linked every village to the Church and was for most people one of the few institutions they met.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/01_reformation.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md` · `medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Parliamentarisation
<a id="parliamentarisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1918
- **Also written as:** parliamentarisation
- **Short:** October 1918 reform making the government responsible to the Reichstag.
- **Explanation:** It came just before the revolution and the Kaiser's abdication.
- **German:** Parlamentarisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### parliamentary army
<a id="parliamentary-army"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Principle that the Bundestag must approve armed deployments of the Bundeswehr.
- **Explanation:** Confirmed by the Constitutional Court in 1994: the government cannot send soldiers into armed missions without parliament's consent.
- **German:** Parlamentsarmee
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Parliamentary Council
<a id="parliamentary-council"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1948–1949
- **Also written as:** Parlamentarischer Rat
- **Short:** Assembly of state politicians that drafted the Basic Law near Bonn in 1948–49.
- **Explanation:** Body of delegates chosen by the West German state parliaments, meeting in Bonn under Allied supervision. It wrote the Basic Law, the Federal Republic's constitution, adopted in May 1949.
- **German:** Parlamentarischer Rat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### partible inheritance
<a id="partible-inheritance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Dividing an inheritance among all sons instead of passing it to one heir.
- **Explanation:** Frankish kings divided their realm among sons, producing repeated divisions and civil wars, including the partition of 843.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### participatory dictatorship
<a id="participatory-dictatorship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Participatory dictatorship
- **Short:** Fulbrook's label for the GDR: many citizens took part in running the system.
- **Explanation:** Mary Fulbrook's concept stressing that the GDR was not only imposed from above; many people participated, and everyday life was often 'normal'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: one of several competing characterisations of the GDR.

### Participatory integration
<a id="participatory-integration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** participatory integration
- **Short:** View that workers were gradually included in the Empire through unions, insurance and local politics.
- **Explanation:** The counterpart to Roth's 'negative integration'.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Particularism
<a id="particularism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** particularist · particularism
- **Short:** Loyalty to one's own state over the nation.
- **Explanation:** Southern German particularism, often Catholic and großdeutsch, resented Prussian-led unity.
- **German:** Partikularismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### partitions of Poland
<a id="partitions-of-poland"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1772–1795
- **Short:** Division of Poland among Russia, Prussia and Austria in 1772, 1793 and 1795.
- **Explanation:** They added millions of Polish Catholic subjects to Prussia and Austria.
- **German:** Teilungen Polens
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Party of Democratic Socialism
<a id="party-of-democratic-socialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1989–2007
- **Also written as:** PDS
- **Short:** Successor to the GDR's ruling communist party, anchored in eastern Germany.
- **Explanation:** The PDS emerged from the SED, the GDR's ruling party, in 1989–90 and gave East Germans a regional voice in united Germany. In 2007 it merged into Die Linke.
- **German:** Partei des Demokratischen Sozialismus (PDS)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### passive resistance
<a id="passive-resistance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1923
- **Short:** Non-violent refusal to cooperate, here with the occupiers of the Ruhr.
- **Explanation:** The German state paid striking workers by printing money, fuelling the 1923 hyperinflation.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### patricians
<a id="patricians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** patrician
- **Short:** Wealthy leading families dominating town councils.
- **Explanation:** In Nuremberg they excluded the guilds from power after 1349.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### patrimonial court
<a id="patrimonial-court"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Court run by a landlord over the peasants on his estate.
- **Explanation:** East of the Elbe the lord was his peasants' judge — a power the nobles kept even after the reforms.
- **German:** Patrimonialgericht
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Patrimonial rights
<a id="patrimonial-rights"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** patrimonial rights
- **Short:** Lords' powers over peasants on their estates, including local justice and police.
- **Explanation:** Many ended in 1848; Prussian estate owners kept police powers until 1872.
- **German:** Patrimonialgerichtsbarkeit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Paulskirche Constitution
<a id="paulskirche-constitution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1849
- **Also written as:** Paulskirche constitution
- **Short:** The 1849 constitution for a united Germany drawn up by the Frankfurt Assembly.
- **Explanation:** It provided a hereditary emperor, a two-chamber parliament elected by universal male suffrage and basic rights. Never implemented, it became a model for the Weimar Constitution and the Basic Law.
- **German:** Paulskirchenverfassung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Pauperism
<a id="pauperism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1830s–1840s
- **Also written as:** Pauperismus · pauperism
- **Short:** Mass poverty before industrialisation: people unable to live even from hard work.
- **Explanation:** The Brockhaus encyclopedia of 1846 defined it as a large class earning 'at most the barest livelihood through the most strenuous work'. It came mostly from population growth outpacing jobs, and fuelled the 1848 revolutions.
- **German:** Pauperismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### peace movement
<a id="peace-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1981–1983
- **Short:** Mass protest in West Germany (1981–83) against stationing new US nuclear missiles.
- **Explanation:** Large protest movement against NATO's plan to deploy American Pershing missiles in West Germany, part of the new social movements and of Cold War anxiety in the early 1980s.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Peace of Augsburg
<a id="peace-of-augsburg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1555
- **Short:** 1555 settlement letting each German ruler choose Lutheranism or Catholicism for his territory.
- **Explanation:** It made religion territorial, gave dissenting subjects a right to emigrate, and excluded Calvinists. Disputes over its unresolved clauses — church lands, the 'ecclesiastical reservation', Calvinism — fed the crisis that exploded in 1618.
- **German:** Augsburger Religionsfrieden
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Peace of Stralsund
<a id="peace-of-stralsund"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1370
- **Short:** 1370 treaty ending the Hanse's war against Denmark.
- **Explanation:** By winning a war against the kingdom of Denmark, the Hanse showed that a network of towns without a state could still exercise great power.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Peace of Tilsit
<a id="peace-of-tilsit"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1807
- **Short:** Peace imposed by Napoleon in 1807 that halved Prussia's territory.
- **Explanation:** The humiliation triggered the Prussian reforms. Tilsit is today Sovetsk in Russia.
- **German:** Friede von Tilsit
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Peace of Westphalia
<a id="peace-of-westphalia"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1648
- **Also written as:** Westphalia · Westphalian settlement
- **Ambiguous forms:** Westphalia (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Treaties ending the Thirty Years' War that rebuilt the Empire on religious parity and law.
- **Explanation:** It recognised Calvinists, fixed 1624 as the 'normal year' for church property, confirmed rulers' territorial authority within imperial law and made France and Sweden guarantors. It did not, most historians say, create modern 'sovereign states'.
- **German:** Westfälischer Friede
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: IR textbooks vs historians on whether it founded a system of sovereign states.

### peace prayers
<a id="peace-prayers"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1980s–1989
- **Short:** Regular church services for peace, notably in Leipzig's Nikolaikirche, that became a base for protest.
- **Explanation:** Friedensgebete gave opposition groups a protected space under church roofs; in autumn 1989 the Monday demonstrations started from them.
- **German:** Friedensgebete
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Peace Resolution
<a id="peace-resolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** July 1917
- **Short:** July 1917 Reichstag resolution calling for a negotiated peace without annexations.
- **Explanation:** Passed by the SPD, Centre and Progressives, it showed a parliamentary majority opposed to the annexationist war aims of the army command and the nationalist lobbies.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Peaceful Revolution
<a id="peaceful-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1989–90
- **Short:** The non-violent uprising of East Germans in 1989 that brought down the SED regime.
- **Explanation:** Mass flight, church-based opposition and street demonstrations forced the regime to give way. It was peaceful because demonstrators kept it so and security forces did not shoot. Historians debate how far it was a revolution by citizens or a collapse from above.
- **German:** Friedliche Revolution
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: revolution vs collapse/implosion is debated.

### peasant emancipation
<a id="peasant-emancipation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** Bauernbefreiung
- **Short:** Abolition of peasants' personal subjection to lords and their labour services.
- **Explanation:** In Prussia it came at a price — land cessions or debts — so nobles expanded their holdings and many smallholders became landless labourers.
- **German:** Bauernbefreiung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Peasants' War
<a id="peasants-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1524–1526
- **Short:** Mass uprising of peasants and townspeople in southern and central Germany, crushed with tens of thousands killed.
- **Explanation:** Rebels demanded 'godly law' — elected pastors, an end to serfdom, fair rents — in the Twelve Articles. Luther sided with the princes. Estimates of the dead run around 70,000–100,000. Historians disagree whether it was a class war or a 'revolution of the common man'.
- **German:** Bauernkrieg
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: interpreted as early bourgeois revolution (Engels, GDR) vs revolution of the common man (Blickle).

### People's Court
<a id="people-s-court"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1934–1945
- **Also written as:** Volksgerichtshof
- **Short:** Nazi special court for political cases such as treason.
- **Explanation:** It passed thousands of death sentences, including against the White Rose and 20 July plotters.
- **German:** Volksgerichtshof
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### People's Party
<a id="people-s-party"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Democratic party in Württemberg critical of Prussian-led unification.
- **Explanation:** It represented democratic opposition to unification under Prussian leadership.
- **German:** Volkspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### people's party of protest
<a id="peoples-party-of-protest"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Volkspartei des Protests
- **Short:** Falter's description of the NSDAP as a protest party drawing voters from all classes.
- **Explanation:** It challenges older class-based theories: the Nazis had a 'middle-class belly' but also many working-class voters, and were disproportionately Protestant and rural.
- **German:** Volkspartei des Protests
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### People's Police
<a id="people-s-police"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1945–1990
- **Short:** The GDR's regular police force.
- **Explanation:** The Volkspolizei ('Vopo'), which, alongside the Stasi and the border troops, enforced SED rule.
- **German:** Volkspolizei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### People's Receiver
<a id="people-s-receiver"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Also written as:** Volksempfänger
- **Short:** Cheap Nazi-era radio set that brought propaganda, music and Hitler's speeches into homes.
- **Explanation:** The 'people's radio' (model VE 301, introduced in 1933) was sold at a low price so that millions of households could listen. It combined leisure with Goebbels' propaganda.
- **German:** Volksempfänger
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### perestroika
<a id="perestroika"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1985–1991
- **Short:** 'Restructuring': Gorbachev's reform of the Soviet economy and political system.
- **Explanation:** Russian word for Gorbachev's reform programme, which the ageing SED leadership refused to follow.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### permanent missions
<a id="permanent-missions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1974–1990
- **Short:** Embassy-like offices the two German states exchanged instead of embassies.
- **Explanation:** Ständige Vertretungen set up after the Basic Treaty, reflecting the West German view that relations between the two German states were special, not foreign relations.
- **German:** Ständige Vertretungen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Perpetual Public Peace
<a id="perpetual-public-peace"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1495
- **Short:** 1495 law permanently banning feud within the Empire.
- **Explanation:** It ended nobles' legal right to feud, in principle reducing aristocratic violence.
- **German:** Ewiger Landfriede
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pershing missiles
<a id="pershing-missiles"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1983
- **Short:** US nuclear-armed missiles deployed in West Germany from 1983 despite mass protests.
- **Explanation:** Medium-range American nuclear missiles (Pershing II) stationed in West Germany under a NATO decision, answering Soviet missiles. Their deployment triggered the West German peace movement of 1981–83.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Persil certificates
<a id="persil-certificates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Affidavits clearing someone of Nazism, named after a washing powder.
- **Explanation:** Named after a popular washing powder, these affidavits from friends, clergy or colleagues 'washed clean' people accused of Nazi involvement in denazification hearings.
- **German:** Persilschein
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### personal law
<a id="personal-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Principle that each person was judged by the law of his own people or group.
- **Explanation:** In the Frankish realm a person lived under Frankish, Alamannic, Bavarian or Saxon law according to origin, not territory.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Personal rule
<a id="personal-rule"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** personal rule
- **Short:** Wilhelm II's claim to govern personally rather than through ministers.
- **Explanation:** Historians see it as erratic and constrained by bureaucracy, military and parliament.
- **German:** persönliches Regiment
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Pfarrernotbund
<a id="pfarrernotbund"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1933
- **Short:** 'Pastors' Emergency League' of about 7,000 Protestant pastors, founded 1933.
- **Explanation:** It opposed Nazi interference in the church and was the core of the later Confessing Church.
- **German:** Pfarrernotbund
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pfarrhaus
<a id="pfarrhaus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** The Protestant parsonage — the married pastor's household.
- **Explanation:** Because Protestant pastors married, the parsonage became a new educated middle-class milieu and for centuries a major source of German intellectuals.
- **German:** Pfarrhaus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Photochrom
<a id="photochrom"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** photochrom
- **Short:** Early colour-printing process turning black-and-white photos into colour prints.
- **Explanation:** Used around 1890–1900 for picture postcards, such as the view of Berlin's Unter den Linden.
- **German:** Photochrom
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### physiocracy
<a id="physiocrats"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 18th c.
- **Also written as:** physiocratic
- **Short:** French-inspired economic doctrine that agriculture is the true source of wealth.
- **Explanation:** With cameralism, physiocratic ideas shaped Enlightenment economic reform, such as agricultural improvement.
- **German:** Physiokratie
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Piece-rates
<a id="piece-rates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** piece-rates
- **Short:** Pay per item produced rather than per hour.
- **Explanation:** Common in 19th-century factories.
- **German:** Akkordlohn
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pietism
<a id="pietism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** late 17th–18th c.
- **Short:** Protestant renewal movement stressing personal piety, education and discipline.
- **Explanation:** Founded by Spener and centred on Francke's Halle, it strongly influenced Prussian officials' ethic of duty.
- **German:** Pietismus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### PISA
<a id="pisa"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2000–
- **Short:** International OECD study comparing school students' skills across countries.
- **Explanation:** Germany's weak results in 2001 (the 'PISA shock') started lasting debates about the strong link between parental background and educational success.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md` · `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Piusvereine
<a id="piusvereine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Short:** Catholic associations founded in 1848, named after Pope Pius IX.
- **Explanation:** They were early organisations of political Catholicism, alongside the first German Catholic Congress.
- **German:** Piusvereine
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Place in the sun
<a id="place-in-the-sun"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1897
- **Also written as:** place in the sun
- **Short:** Bülow's 1897 demand for German colonies and world power.
- **Explanation:** It captured imperialism as national prestige.
- **German:** Platz an der Sonne
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### planned economy
<a id="planned-economy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Planned economy
- **Short:** Economy where the state sets production, prices and investment through central plans.
- **Explanation:** The GDR's economic system: five-year plans, state-owned enterprises and state-set prices. It guaranteed jobs and cheap basics but suffered from low productivity, shortages and growing debt.
- **German:** Planwirtschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Plattenbau
<a id="plattenbau"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Prefabricated concrete-slab apartment block, typical of GDR housing estates.
- **Explanation:** Buildings made of factory-produced concrete panels. Honecker's 1973 housing programme built about 2 million units this way, while old city centres decayed.
- **German:** Plattenbau
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Plebiscitary dictatorship
<a id="plebiscitary-dictatorship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** plebiscitary dictatorship
- **Short:** Gellately's description of Nazi rule as a dictatorship resting on popular consent.
- **Explanation:** The term implies the regime depended on, and actively won, the backing of most Germans. Richard Evans and others argue this underrates terror.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: consent vs coercion debate.

### plebiscite
<a id="plebiscite"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** plebiscites · Plebiscites
- **Short:** A direct popular vote on a question (a referendum).
- **Explanation:** Vote in which the whole electorate decides an issue directly. Because plebiscites had been used demagogically in Weimar and Nazi times, the Basic Law allows no federal referendums except on redrawing state borders.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Plural vote
<a id="plural-vote"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** plural vote
- **Short:** Voting system giving some voters (e.g. the wealthy or educated) extra votes.
- **Explanation:** Saxony used it for its state parliament.
- **German:** Pluralwahlrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Plutocracy
<a id="plutocracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** plutocracy · plutocracies
- **Short:** Rule by the rich; Nazi propaganda term for Britain and the United States.
- **Explanation:** It was used to attack Western democracies and was linked to antisemitic stereotypes.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### pogrom
<a id="pogrom"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** pogroms
- **Short:** Violent mass attack on a Jewish community.
- **Explanation:** Medieval pogroms struck German Jews in 1096, 1298, 1336–38 and 1348–50.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Politburo
<a id="politburo"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** The small top leadership body of the ruling communist party (about 20 members in the GDR).
- **Explanation:** Political Bureau of the SED's Central Committee, where real decisions in the GDR were taken. After 1990 several Politburo members, including Egon Krenz and Günter Schabowski, were convicted for deaths at the border.
- **German:** Politbüro
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Political Catholicism
<a id="political-catholicism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** from 1837
- **Also written as:** political Catholicism
- **Short:** Organised Catholic politics defending the Church's rights, later embodied by the Centre Party.
- **Explanation:** It emerged after the Cologne church conflict of 1837, grew through Catholic associations in 1848, and after 1870 formed the Centre Party, which the Kulturkampf strengthened.
- **German:** politischer Katholizismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Polizeiordnungen
<a id="polizeiordnungen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Police ordinances': princely decrees regulating morals, trade and dress.
- **Explanation:** 'Police' then meant good order in general. These ordinances show the growing reach of the early modern state into daily life.
- **German:** Polizeiordnungen
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### poll tax
<a id="poll-tax"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** poll taxes
- **Short:** Tax charged per head of population.
- **Explanation:** Along with land taxes, customs at the frontier and requisitions for the army, poll taxes funded Roman rule in the provinces.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### polycracy
<a id="polycracy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Polycracy · polycratic
- **Short:** Rule by many competing power centres.
- **Explanation:** Broszat and Mommsen described the Nazi state as a jungle of rival agencies with overlapping jurisdictions.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### polyptychs
<a id="polyptychs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Early medieval estate surveys listing lands, tenants and their dues.
- **Explanation:** They survive for some abbeys and show how the most organised Carolingian estates worked.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### polytechnic school
<a id="polytechnic-school"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1959–1990
- **Also written as:** polytechnic schooling
- **Short:** The GDR's unified ten-year school for all pupils, mixing general and practical education.
- **Explanation:** Introduced in 1959, it gave East Germans a high level of basic education, but schooling was politicised and access to higher education depended on class origin and political loyalty.
- **German:** Polytechnische Oberschule
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### polytheism
<a id="polytheism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Polytheistic
- **Short:** Belief in and worship of many gods.
- **Explanation:** Early Germanic-speaking religion was polytheistic and varied from place to place, centred on sacred groves and offerings in bogs and lakes, with no organised priesthood like the Roman or later Christian Church.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Popular sovereignty
<a id="popular-sovereignty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** popular sovereignty
- **Short:** The principle that political power comes from the people, not the monarch.
- **Explanation:** Demanded at the Hambach Festival and by democrats such as Hecker and Struve, it clashed with the princes' claim to rule by dynastic right.
- **German:** Volkssouveränität
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Porajmos
<a id="porajmos"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Short:** Romani term for the Nazi genocide of the Sinti and Roma.
- **Explanation:** Survivors faced continued discrimination after the war; the Federal Republic officially recognised the genocide only in 1982.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### post-Roman law codes
<a id="post-roman-law-codes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Written law collections of the Frankish and other kingdoms, in Latin, from the 6th century.
- **Explanation:** Written under Roman influence, they recorded customary law of groups such as Franks, Alamanni, Bavarians and Saxons. They placed women under male guardianship and made legal status depend on a person's group ('personal law').
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Potsdam Conference
<a id="potsdam-conference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1945
- **Also written as:** Potsdam
- **Ambiguous forms:** Potsdam (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** July–August 1945 Allied summit on post-war Germany.
- **Explanation:** It agreed on the 'four Ds', and moved German lands east of the Oder–Neisse line to Polish and Soviet administration.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Pragmatic Sanction
<a id="pragmatic-sanction"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1713
- **Short:** Habsburg law of 1713 allowing a daughter to inherit all the monarchy's lands.
- **Explanation:** It allowed Maria Theresa's succession in 1740, which Prussia exploited to invade Silesia.
- **German:** Pragmatische Sanktion
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)

### Pre-Parliament
<a id="pre-parliament"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1848
- **Short:** Assembly of liberal and democratic politicians that prepared elections to the National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** It met in Frankfurt in spring 1848 and arranged the elections to the all-German National Assembly.
- **German:** Vorparlament
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### prefect
<a id="prefect"></a>

- **Kind:** term · office/title
- **Also written as:** Prefect
- **Short:** French-style regional governor appointed by the central government.
- **Explanation:** Rhine Confederation states adopted prefect-style officials to run their new, uniform provinces.
- **German:** Präfekt
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### prerogative state
<a id="prerogative-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Maßnahmenstaat
- **Short:** In Fraenkel's model, the sphere where police and party acted without legal limits.
- **Explanation:** Anything declared 'political' could be moved into it.
- **German:** Maßnahmenstaat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### presentism
<a id="presentism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** presentist
- **Short:** Judging the past by present-day ideas and concerns.
- **Explanation:** Critics call comparing the Holy Roman Empire to the EU presentist.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### presidential cabinets
<a id="presidential-cabinets"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1930–1933
- **Also written as:** Präsidialkabinette
- **Short:** Governments of 1930–33 that ruled with the president's backing, not a parliamentary majority.
- **Explanation:** Under Brüning, Papen and Schleicher, laws were made by emergency decree and the Reichstag was dissolved when it objected, so real power passed to President Hindenburg's small circle — which then appointed Hitler.
- **German:** Präsidialkabinette
