- **Basis:** KB+K

### SA ban
<a id="sa-ban"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** April–June 1932
- **Short:** Government ban on the Nazi stormtroopers in spring 1932.
- **Explanation:** Papen lifted it in June 1932, part of his courting of the Nazis.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Sahra Wagenknecht Alliance
<a id="sahra-wagenknecht-alliance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 2024–
- **Also written as:** BSW
- **Short:** Left-conservative party split from Die Linke in 2024; narrowly missed the Bundestag in 2025.
- **Explanation:** Founded in 2024 by a breakaway group from Die Linke around politician Sahra Wagenknecht. It combines left economic positions with scepticism about immigration and the EU, and won 4.97% in 2025, just below the 5% threshold.
- **German:** Bündnis Sahra Wagenknecht (BSW)
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Salians
<a id="salians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1024–1125
- **Also written as:** Salian
- **Short:** Royal dynasty (1024–1125) that followed the Ottonians; included Henry IV.
- **Explanation:** Like the Ottonians they ruled through bishops; under Henry IV they fought the Investiture Controversy.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### salons
<a id="salons"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Salons
- **Short:** Private gatherings hosted by women for conversation among writers, nobles and officials.
- **Explanation:** Jewish women's salons in Berlin (Rahel Varnhagen, Henriette Herz, c. 1790–1806) were spaces of mixed sociability.
- **German:** Salons
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### same-sex marriage
<a id="same-sex-marriage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 2017–
- **Short:** Legal right of same-sex couples to marry in Germany, since 2017.
- **Explanation:** Introduced by the Bundestag in 2017, it replaced the earlier registered partnership for new couples.
- **German:** Ehe für alle
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Saxon Wars
<a id="saxon-wars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 772–804
- **Also written as:** Charlemagne's Saxon Wars
- **Short:** Charlemagne's thirty-year war to conquer and forcibly Christianise the Saxons.
- **Explanation:** The wars incorporated Saxony (roughly today's north-west and central-north Germany) into the Frankish empire and forced its people to convert. They were brutal and long; by their end, the Franks ruled almost all the lands that later formed the German kingdom.
- **German:** Sachsenkriege
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Saxons
<a id="saxons"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Saxon
- **Ambiguous forms:** Saxon (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Confederation of northern Germany, conquered by Charlemagne with war and forced conversion.
- **Explanation:** One of the larger groupings appearing from the 3rd century. Charlemagne's thirty-year war against them involved massacre, deportation and forced baptism. From 919 a Saxon dynasty (the Ottonians) ruled the East Frankish kingdom.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Schengen area
<a id="schengen-area"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Also written as:** Schengen
- **Short:** European zone without passport controls at internal borders.
- **Explanation:** Named after the Luxembourg village where the agreement was signed. It turned Germany's borders with its neighbours into signs rather than barriers.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Schießbefehl
<a id="schiessbefehl"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** order to shoot · Shoot-to-kill · shoot-to-kill
- **Short:** 'Order to shoot': GDR border guards' practice of firing on people trying to escape.
- **Explanation:** There was no single written order covering all cases, but guards were instructed to prevent 'border breaches' by all means including firearms and were rewarded for it. After 1990 courts convicted guards and Politburo members for manslaughter.
- **German:** Schießbefehl
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Schlafgänger
<a id="schlafganger"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Lodgers who rented only a bed in someone else's flat.
- **Explanation:** Common in crowded industrial cities of the Empire.
- **German:** Schlafgänger
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)

### Schmalkaldic League
<a id="schmalkaldic-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1531–1547
- **Short:** Defensive alliance of Protestant princes and cities formed in 1531.
- **Explanation:** Formed to protect the Reformation against the Catholic emperor Charles V; it was defeated in the Schmalkaldic War of 1546–47.
- **German:** Schmalkaldischer Bund
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)

### Schmalkaldic War
<a id="schmalkaldic-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1546–1547
- **Short:** Charles V's war against the Protestant Schmalkaldic League, which he won but could not exploit.
- **Explanation:** A military victory for the emperor that still failed to restore religious unity; the princes' revolt of 1552 and the Peace of Augsburg (1555) followed.
- **German:** Schmalkaldischer Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Scholz coalition
<a id="scholz-coalition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 2021–2024
- **Short:** SPD-led three-party government (2021–24) that collapsed over budget conflicts.
- **Explanation:** The coalition of SPD, Greens and FDP under Olaf Scholz. After a 2023 Constitutional Court ruling voided a €60 billion budget reallocation, budget conflicts ended it in November 2024.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### School of the nation
<a id="school-of-the-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** school of the nation
- **Short:** The idea of the army as educator of the nation, teaching obedience.
- **Explanation:** Conscription was meant to shape young men as loyal subjects.
- **German:** Schule der Nation

### Schutzbriefe
<a id="schutzbriefe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Letters of protection' letting Jews live in a territory, usually for payment.
- **Explanation:** Jewish communities were small and depended on these princely privileges.
- **German:** Schutzbriefe

### Schutztruppe
<a id="schutztruppe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** German colonial military force.
- **Explanation:** It fought colonial wars and enforced rule in the colonies.
- **German:** Schutztruppe
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)

### scriptorium
<a id="scriptorium"></a>

- **Explanation:** Carolingian scriptoria preserved liturgy and classical texts.

<a id="sd"></a>

- **Also written as:** Social Democratic Workers' Party

<a id="sdp"></a>

- **Dates:** 1989–1990
- **Short:** Social Democratic Party in the GDR, founded 7 October 1989; merged with the West German SPD.
- **Explanation:** Founding a social democratic party openly challenged the SED's claim to have absorbed the Social Democrats in 1946. It soon renamed itself SPD and merged with the western party in 1990.

### Second industrial revolution
<a id="second-industrial-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** c. 1870–1914
- **Also written as:** second industrial revolution
- **Short:** Industrial phase driven by chemicals, electrical engineering and steel.
- **Explanation:** Germany became a world leader in these science-based industries.

### Second World War
<a id="second-world-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1939–1945
- **Also written as:** WWII
- **Short:** War begun by Germany's invasion of Poland in 1939; ended in Europe in May 1945.
- **Explanation:** Germany launched it deliberately to win 'living space'. It ended with unconditional surrender and the collapse of the German state.

### sector border
<a id="sector-border"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1945–1961
- **Short:** The line between the Soviet sector of Berlin and the three Western sectors.
- **Explanation:** Until 1961 it was open, so East Germans could simply cross into West Berlin and fly or travel on to West Germany — the gap the Wall closed.
- **German:** Sektorengrenze
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### secularisation
<a id="secularisation-church-lands"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** Secularisation · secularised
- **Short:** Transfer of church lands and property to secular (non-church) rulers.
- **Explanation:** It happened twice on a large scale: when Protestant princes seized monasteries in the Reformation, and in 1803, when almost all church states in the Empire were abolished and handed to secular princes.
- **German:** Säkularisation
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)

### secularisation
<a id="secularisation-religion"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Decline of religious belief and church membership in society.
- **Explanation:** In Germany the two large churches' members fell below half the population in the 2020s, a rapid change in a country long shaped by confessional divides.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

<a id="sed"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1946–1989/90
- **Also written as:** Socialist Unity Party · SED Politburo
- **Short:** Socialist Unity Party: the ruling communist party of the GDR.

<a id="sedan-day"></a>

<a id="seizure-of-power"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1933–1934
- **Also written as:** Machtergreifung
- **Short:** The Nazis' takeover and consolidation of power in 1933–34.

<a id="self-coordination"></a>

<a id="self-determination-law"></a>

- **Dates:** 2024
- **Short:** 2024 law letting people change their legal gender entry by declaration.
- **Explanation:** It replaced older rules requiring expert reports and court procedures to change the registered gender and first name.

### Separate spheres
<a id="separate-spheres"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** separate spheres
- **Short:** Bourgeois ideal that men belong to public life, women to home and family.
- **Explanation:** This ideal hardened in the 19th century and helped exclude women from politics, though women were active in charitable and patriotic associations.

### September Programme

- **Short:** Chancellor Bethmann Hollweg's 1914 plan for a German-dominated Central Europe after victory.

### Septennat
<a id="septennat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1874
- **Short:** Seven-year approval of the army budget, limiting parliament's yearly control.
- **Explanation:** It removed about three-quarters of Reich spending from annual parliamentary control; later terms were five years.

### serfdom
<a id="serfdom"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Leibeigenschaft · serfs · serf · Leibeigene
- **Short:** Legal bondage tying peasants to a lord, who controlled their labour, movement and marriage.
- **Explanation:** Denounced by rebel peasants in 1525, it hardened east of the Elbe after the Thirty Years' War. Joseph II abolished personal serfdom in Bohemia in 1781; Prussia ended hereditary subjection from 1810 on terms that favoured landlords.

### servi camerae
<a id="servi-camerae"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** 'Servants of the chamber': Jews' legal status as the emperor's protected dependants.
- **Explanation:** From the 13th century the emperor was the Jews' formal protector, in return for taxes. Emperors pawned these rights to princes and towns, leaving Jews vulnerable in crises.

### Settlement Commission
<a id="settlement-commission"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** from 1886
- **Short:** Prussian body buying land in Polish areas for German settlers.
- **Explanation:** Part of the Germanisation policy in Prussia's eastern provinces.
- **German:** Ansiedlungskommission
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Settler colony
<a id="settler-colony"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** settler colonies
- **Short:** Colony where Europeans settled and took land.
- **Explanation:** South West Africa was one, with extensive land expropriation.
- **German:** Siedlungskolonie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Seven Years' War
<a id="seven-years-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1756–1763
- **Short:** European war in which Prussia defended its conquest of Silesia against Austria and allies.
- **Explanation:** Prussia nearly collapsed in 1759–61 but survived, backed by British subsidies. The war made Frederick II a European celebrity.
- **German:** Siebenjähriger Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ShUM
<a id="shum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Jewish communities of Speyer, Worms and Mainz, among Europe's most important in the Middle Ages.
- **Explanation:** The name comes from the Hebrew initials of the three cities. They suffered crusader massacres in 1096. Their sites became a UNESCO World Heritage site in 2021.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### sick man of Europe
<a id="sick-man-of-europe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Label for Germany around 2000, when its economy grew slowly and unemployment was high.
- **Explanation:** A phrase used about Germany around 2000, when slow growth and high unemployment followed the costs of unification. The Hartz labour-market reforms were a response; jobs grew after 2005.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Siemens
<a id="siemens"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Berlin electrical-engineering company, a leader of the 'second industrial revolution'.
- **Explanation:** Founded by Werner von Siemens, it symbolised Germany's rise in electrical engineering.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Silent bourgeois revolution
<a id="silent-bourgeois-revolution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** silent bourgeois revolution
- **Short:** Blackbourn and Eley's view that the German middle class succeeded economically and culturally.
- **Explanation:** It challenges the idea that the bourgeoisie failed in Germany.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Silent dictatorship
<a id="silent-dictatorship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1916–1918
- **Short:** Historian Martin Kitchen's term for the WWI army command's domination of German politics.
- **Explanation:** It describes how the generals governed large areas of life while the civilian constitution formally stayed in place. Other historians stress the limits of the army's power.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: some historians (e.g. Afflerbach) stress the limits of the army command's power.

### Silent heroes
<a id="silent-heroes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** silent heroes
- **Short:** Germans who hid or helped Jews during persecution.
- **Explanation:** Some thousands of Germans helped; about 1,700 Jews survived hidden in Berlin.
- **German:** stille Helden
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Silesian Wars
<a id="silesian-wars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1740–1763
- **Also written as:** Wars for Silesia
- **Short:** Wars between Prussia and Austria over Silesia, 1740–63.
- **Explanation:** They cost Prussia perhaps 10% of its population and began Austro-Prussian dualism.
- **German:** Schlesische Kriege
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Silesian weavers' revolt
<a id="silesian-weavers-revolt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1844
- **Also written as:** Silesian linen weavers
- **Short:** 1844 uprising of starving Silesian linen weavers, crushed by the Prussian army.
- **Explanation:** The weavers had been ruined by mechanised British competition. Their revolt became a symbol of early industrial misery through Heine's poem and Hauptmann's play 'The Weavers'.
- **German:** Weberaufstand
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### silver penny
<a id="silver-penny"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** The main coin of Charlemagne's monetary reform.
- **Explanation:** Charlemagne's reform created a silver penny coinage; money and long-distance trade continued, but on a small scale.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Simplicissimus
<a id="simplicissimus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1668
- **Short:** Grimmelshausen's 1668 novel, the classic literary image of the Thirty Years' War.
- **Explanation:** It follows a naive boy through the war's violence and chaos.
- **German:** Der abenteuerliche Simplicissimus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### single market
<a id="single-market"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** EU single market
- **Short:** EU area in which goods, services, capital and people move freely.
- **Explanation:** Germany's export model depends above all on it; most German trade is within the EU.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Sinti and Roma
<a id="sinti-and-roma"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Roma · Roma and Sinti
- **Short:** Romani peoples persecuted and murdered by the Nazis on racial grounds.
- **Explanation:** They were put in camps, deported and murdered; at least 250,000, possibly up to 500,000 Roma were killed.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Sippenhaft
<a id="sippenhaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** clan liability
- **Short:** 'Clan liability': punishing relatives for a person's actions.
- **Explanation:** After the 20 July plot, families of conspirators were arrested.
- **German:** Sippenhaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Slavs
<a id="slavs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Slavic
- **Short:** Peoples speaking Slavic languages who lived east of the Elbe in the early Middle Ages.
- **Explanation:** Slavic raids pressed the Frankish east in the 9th–10th centuries. Later, Slavic princes invited German settlers in the Ostsiedlung, creating mixed societies; modern Germans also partly descend from Slavic populations.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SM-70
<a id="sm-70"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** until 1983–84
- **Short:** Automatic firing device mounted on the inner-German border fence.
- **Explanation:** Directional mine that fired metal fragments when a trip-wire was touched. The GDR dismantled them in 1983–84.
- **German:** Selbstschussanlage SM-70
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Social Darwinism
<a id="social-darwinism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** social Darwinism
- **Short:** Belief that nations and races struggle for survival like species.
- **Explanation:** Spread among intellectuals and radical nationalists in the Empire.
- **German:** Sozialdarwinismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Social Democratism
<a id="social-democratism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** SED charge used to purge members who held on to social democratic ideas.
- **Explanation:** After the forced merger of 1946, former SPD members in the SED were persecuted under this label.
- **German:** Sozialdemokratismus
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### social fascism
<a id="social-fascism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 1928–1933
- **Also written as:** social fascists
- **Short:** Communist slogan branding the Social Democrats as 'social fascists'.
- **Explanation:** From 1928 the KPD, following Moscow, attacked the SPD as its main enemy, contributing to the left's paralysis against the Nazis; East German historians later downplayed this.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### social federal state
<a id="social-federal-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Basic Law's definition of Germany as a federal state committed to social welfare (Art. 20).
- **Explanation:** The constitution obliges the state to social justice and security. In practice this means an extensive, insurance-based welfare state under long-term pressure from ageing.
- **German:** sozialer Bundesstaat
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/03_modern_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Social imperialism
<a id="social-imperialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** social imperialism
- **Short:** Wehler's thesis: colonial expansion as a diversion from domestic class conflict.
- **Explanation:** Elites supposedly used imperialism to rally support and avoid reform. It is contested.
- **German:** Sozialimperialismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED.

### social insurance
<a id="social-insurance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1880s–
- **Also written as:** Social insurance
- **Short:** Compulsory insurance (health, accident, pensions) paid for by contributions from workers and employers.
- **Explanation:** Model introduced under Bismarck in the 1880s and continued in the Federal Republic: insurance funded by contributions from wages rather than taxes. It is one of the strongest continuities in German social policy.
- **German:** Sozialversicherung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### social market economy
<a id="social-market-economy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1948–
- **Also written as:** Social market economy · Soziale Marktwirtschaft
- **Short:** West Germany's economic model: free markets combined with social security and social partnership.
- **Explanation:** Term coined by Alfred Müller-Armack and promoted by Ludwig Erhard. It combined competition (a cartel law from 1957) with social insurance, co-determination and an independent central bank guarding price stability. It was extended to East Germany in 1990.
- **German:** Soziale Marktwirtschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### social militarisation
<a id="social-militarisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Social militarisation
- **Short:** Büsch's thesis that Prussia's army system militarised rural society and bred obedience.
- **Explanation:** Critics say it is too top-down, other armies were similar, and the canton system was negotiated.
- **German:** Sozialmilitarisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED

### social partnership
<a id="social-partnership"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** social partners
- **Short:** Cooperation between unions and employers in managing wages and working conditions.
- **Explanation:** Unions and employers ('social partners') settle wages and conditions through collective bargaining and co-determination, a lasting feature of Germany's consensus model.
- **German:** Sozialpartnerschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Social question
<a id="social-question"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 19th century
- **Also written as:** social question
- **Short:** The problem of industrial workers' poverty, insecurity and housing.
- **Explanation:** It was the central domestic conflict of the Empire, answered by both repression and social insurance.
- **German:** soziale Frage
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Social-moral milieus
<a id="social-moral-milieus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Lepsius's stable political-cultural blocs, each with party, press and associations.
- **Explanation:** Conservative-Protestant, liberal-Protestant, Catholic and Social Democratic milieus persisted until 1933.
- **German:** sozialmoralische Milieus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Socialism
<a id="socialism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** socialism
- **Short:** Ideology seeking common or state control of the economy for workers' benefit.
- **Explanation:** Early German socialism grew among artisans before 1848; after 1863 it became the organised labour movement and the SPD.
- **German:** Sozialismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### socialist nation
<a id="socialist-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1970s
- **Short:** SED doctrine from the 1970s that the GDR was a separate nation from West Germany.
- **Explanation:** Replaced the earlier goal of a 'Germany united and socialist'; the 1974 constitution erased references to the German nation.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Socialist Spring
<a id="socialist-spring"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1960
- **Short:** The 1960 campaign that forced the last private farmers in the GDR into collective farms.
- **Explanation:** Final, coercive push of agricultural collectivisation, which drove many farmers to flee to the West before the Wall was built.
- **German:** Sozialistischer Frühling
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Society for German-Soviet Friendship
<a id="society-for-german-soviet-friendship"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1947–1990
- **Short:** GDR mass organisation promoting friendship with the Soviet Union.
- **Explanation:** One of the largest mass organisations in the GDR, which spread a positive image of the Soviet Union and Soviet culture.
- **German:** Gesellschaft für Deutsch-Sowjetische Freundschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Society of associations
<a id="society-of-associations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** society of associations
- **Short:** Description of the Empire as a society organised through countless clubs.
- **Explanation:** Choirs, sports clubs, church groups and workers' culture clubs structured everyday life.
- **German:** Vereinsgesellschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### sole legitimate representative
<a id="sole-legitimate-representative"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1949–1969
- **Also written as:** Sole legitimate representative
- **Short:** West Germany's claim to speak for all Germans, denying the GDR's legitimacy.
- **Explanation:** Alleinvertretungsanspruch: because only the FRG had a freely elected government, it claimed to represent Germany as a whole. The Hallstein Doctrine enforced it until Ostpolitik.
- **German:** Alleinvertretungsanspruch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### solidarity surcharge
<a id="solidarity-surcharge"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1991–
- **Also written as:** solidarity tax
- **Short:** Extra income-tax charge introduced after unification to help pay for rebuilding the East.
- **Explanation:** An additional levy on income tax paid in both East and West. It became a symbol of the cost of unity to West Germans.
- **German:** Solidaritätszuschlag ('Soli')
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Sonderweg
<a id="sonderweg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** special path · Special Path
- **Short:** 'Special path': thesis that Germany deviated from Western democracy toward Nazism.
- **Explanation:** Wehler argued pre-industrial elites blocked democratisation in the Empire. Critics deny a 'normal' Western path; Kocka defended a milder version. Its application to 1848 and 1871 is widely rejected in strong form.
- **German:** Sonderweg
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: historians disagree how far Germany took a special path; strong form largely rejected.

### Sopade
<a id="sopade"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** The exiled SPD leadership and its secret reports on life in Nazi Germany.
- **Explanation:** Social Democratic informants sent reports abroad on the public mood, a key source alongside the regime's own surveillance reports.
- **German:** Sopade

### Sorbs
<a id="sorbs"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Sorbian
- **Short:** Slavic-speaking minority in eastern Germany (Lusatia).
- **Explanation:** Small West Slavic people living in Lusatia in Saxony and Brandenburg. The GDR officially promoted their language and culture.
- **German:** Sorben

### sovereignty
<a id="sovereignty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Westphalian system · Westphalian myth · sovereign
- **Short:** Supreme, independent authority of a state, answerable to no higher power.
- **Explanation:** Textbooks often claim the 1648 peace founded a 'Westphalian system' of sovereign states; historians such as Osiander call this a myth, since the treaties never mention sovereignty and German territories stayed under imperial law.
- **German:** Souveränität

### Soviet prisoners of war
<a id="soviet-prisoners-of-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Soviet POWs
- **Short:** Red Army soldiers captured by Germany; about 3.3 million died.
- **Explanation:** The Wehrmacht deliberately starved and neglected them, making them one of the largest victim groups.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)

### Soviet special camps
<a id="soviet-special-camps"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1945–1950
- **Short:** Camps where the Soviets held over 150,000 Germans after 1945.
- **Explanation:** The Soviet secret police (NKVD) held over 150,000 Germans in these camps after 1945: many Nazis, but also innocents and later political opponents. More than 40,000 died.
- **German:** Speziallager
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spanish influenza
<a id="spanish-influenza"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1918–1920
- **Short:** The worldwide flu pandemic at the end of WWI.
- **Explanation:** Some 150,000 German civilians are estimated to have died of war-related Spanish influenza, in addition to deaths attributed to the blockade.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spartacist uprising
<a id="spartacist-uprising"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** January 1919
- **Also written as:** Spartacist
- **Short:** Communist uprising in Berlin in January 1919, crushed by government-backed Freikorps.
- **Explanation:** The SPD-led government used the army and right-wing Freikorps to crush the rising; Rosa Luxemburg and Karl Liebknecht were murdered. The bloodshed created a lasting, bitter split between Social Democrats and Communists.
- **German:** Spartakusaufstand
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spartacus League
<a id="spartacus-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Revolutionary socialist group led by Karl Liebknecht and Rosa Luxemburg.
- **Explanation:** It opposed the war and sought a socialist revolution on the Russian model; at the turn of 1918/19 it became the core of the new Communist Party (KPD).
- **German:** Spartakusbund
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spätaussiedler
<a id="spataussiedler"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Late-resettler
- **Short:** Ethnic Germans from the former Soviet Union and Eastern Europe who resettled in Germany.
- **Explanation:** 'Late resettlers': descendants of German communities in the former USSR and Eastern Europe, entitled to come to Germany. About 2.5 million arrived from the former USSR after 1990.
- **German:** Spätaussiedler
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SPD
<a id="spd"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1875– (roots 1863)
- **Also written as:** Social Democratic Party · Social Democrats · Social Democracy · Social Democrat · Social Democratic
- **Short:** Social Democratic Party of Germany, the workers' party; largest party by 1912.
- **Explanation:** Formed at Gotha in 1875, banned 1878–90 but still elected, it grew into the world's largest party organisation. Marxist in theory, reformist in practice; it voted for war credits in 1914 and led the republic in 1918–19.
- **German:** Sozialdemokratische Partei Deutschlands
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### special courts
<a id="special-courts"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Also written as:** Sondergerichte
- **Short:** Nazi courts for political offences with minimal legal protections.
- **Explanation:** With the People's Court they passed an estimated 16,000 death sentences.
- **German:** Sondergerichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`

### special fund
<a id="special-fund"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Off-budget borrowing funds for the Bundeswehr (2022) and for infrastructure and climate (2025).
- **Explanation:** A 'Sondervermögen' is a separate pot of borrowed money outside the normal budget. In 2022 a €100 billion fund was created for the Bundeswehr; in 2025 a €500 billion fund for infrastructure and climate, exempt from the debt brake.
- **German:** Sondervermögen
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spiegel affair
<a id="spiegel-affair"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1962
- **Short:** 1962 scandal when police raided Der Spiegel magazine; defence minister Strauss had to resign.
- **Explanation:** After the news magazine Der Spiegel published a critical report on West German defence, police searched its offices and arrested journalists on treason charges. Public protest forced defence minister Franz Josef Strauss out — a key test of press freedom in the young democracy.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### spring 1918 offensive
<a id="spring-1918-offensive"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1918
- **Short:** Germany's last great attack on the Western Front, in spring 1918.
- **Explanation:** Its failure convinced the army command that the war was lost, leading Ludendorff to demand an armistice.
- **German:** Frühjahrsoffensive
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Spruchkammer
<a id="spruchkammer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Spruchkammern
- **Short:** German denazification tribunal classifying people into five categories.
- **Explanation:** German-staffed tribunals in the US zone judged each person's Nazi past and placed them in one of five categories, from I 'major offenders' to V 'exonerated'. So many were cleared that they were mocked as 'follower factories'.
- **German:** Spruchkammer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Sputnik
<a id="sputnik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Soviet digest magazine banned by the GDR in 1988 for its reformist content.
- **Explanation:** The ban on a magazine from the GDR's own protector showed how far the SED leadership resisted Gorbachev's reforms.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SRP
<a id="srp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1949–1952
- **Short:** Socialist Reich Party, a neo-Nazi party banned by the Constitutional Court in 1952.
- **Explanation:** The Sozialistische Reichspartei, a successor movement to the Nazi party. Its 1952 ban was the first party ban under the 'militant democracy' provisions of the Basic Law.
- **German:** Sozialistische Reichspartei
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SS
<a id="ss"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1925–1945
- **Short:** Nazi elite organisation led by Himmler that controlled police, camps and the genocide.
- **Explanation:** Starting as auxiliary police in 1933, it became a 'state within the state' — police, Gestapo, camps, racial resettlement, the Waffen-SS and businesses — the core of the Holocaust machinery.
- **German:** Schutzstaffel
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SS state
<a id="ss-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** The SS as a 'state within the state' controlling police, camps and genocide.
- **Explanation:** Under Himmler it became the core of the genocide machinery.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### SSW
<a id="ssw"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Party of the Danish minority in Schleswig-Holstein, exempt from the 5% threshold.
- **Explanation:** Thanks to minority protections following the Bonn–Copenhagen Declarations (1955), it can win seats without passing the 5% hurdle that other parties need.
- **German:** Südschleswigscher Wählerverband
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Staatenhaus
<a id="staatenhaus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1849
- **Short:** 'House of States': planned upper chamber representing the states in the 1849 constitution.
- **Explanation:** Together with the Volkshaus it would have formed a two-chamber Reichstag.
- **German:** Staatenhaus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Staatsgerichtshof
<a id="staatsgerichtshof"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1921–1933
- **Short:** The Weimar Republic's constitutional court for disputes between the Reich and the states.
- **Explanation:** The KB places it on the path from the old imperial courts, where subjects could sue princes, to today's Federal Constitutional Court. It ruled, for example, on the 1932 federal takeover of Prussia.
- **German:** Staatsgerichtshof für das Deutsche Reich
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Staatslexikon
<a id="staatslexikon"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1834–1843
- **Short:** Political encyclopedia of Rotteck and Welcker, the bible of southern German liberalism.
- **Explanation:** It spread liberal ideas of constitution, rule of law and the nation as a community of citizens.
- **German:** Staatslexikon
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Staatsräson
<a id="staatsrason"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Reason of state': Merkel's 2008 term for Israel's security as a German core interest.
- **Explanation:** A state's fundamental interest. In 2008 Merkel declared Israel's security part of Germany's Staatsräson, reflecting the Holocaust's place at the core of national identity.
- **German:** Staatsräson
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### stab-in-the-back myth
<a id="stab-in-the-back-myth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** stabbed in the back · Dolchstoßlegende
- **Short:** The false claim that the army was undefeated in WWI and betrayed by revolutionaries at home.
- **Explanation:** Born at the moment of defeat, it ignored that the army command itself declared the war lost before the revolution. It was widely believed on the right and poisoned the Weimar Republic.
- **German:** Dolchstoßlegende
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Stadtluft macht frei
<a id="stadtluft-macht-frei"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Town air makes free': 19th-century phrase for the rule that a year in town freed a serf.
- **Explanation:** Many town laws freed a serf after 'a year and a day' in town, but the catchy phrase was coined by 19th-century legal historians, and lords often reclaimed runaways.
- **German:** Stadtluft macht frei
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Stahlhelm
<a id="stahlhelm"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Nationalist WWI veterans' league ('Steel Helmet').
- **Explanation:** In February 1933 it became auxiliary police in Prussia alongside the SA and SS.
- **German:** Stahlhelm
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Stalin Note
<a id="stalin-note"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1952
- **Short:** Stalin's 1952 offer of a united but neutral Germany, rejected by the West.
- **Explanation:** Soviet proposal for German unification with neutrality. Adenauer prioritised Western integration and rejected it. Historians debate whether it was a real chance or a propaganda manoeuvre; most, backed by Soviet archives, now lean towards manoeuvre.
- **German:** Stalin-Note
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether the offer was serious is still debated.

### Stalinism
<a id="stalinism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** Stalinist
- **Short:** Stalin's system of one-party dictatorship, terror, personality cult and command economy.
- **Explanation:** Methods of rule developed in the Soviet Union under Stalin and exported to the GDR: purges, show trials, secret police and central planning.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Stände
<a id="stande"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** estates
- **Ambiguous forms:** estates (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 'Estates': the social orders of those who pray, fight and work.
- **Explanation:** Medieval society was organised in estates; a person's legal status depended on birth, lord, place and religion. Later the word also meant the groups represented in assemblies.
- **German:** Stände
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### standing army
<a id="standing-army"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** standing armies
- **Short:** A permanent professional army kept in peacetime.
- **Explanation:** After 1648 larger princes kept standing armies, which required permanent taxes and bureaucracies — the heart of the 'fiscal-military state'.
- **German:** stehendes Heer
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`

### Stasi
<a id="stasi"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1950–1990
- **Also written as:** Ministry for State Security · Ministerium für Staatssicherheit · MfS
- **Short:** The GDR's secret police and intelligence service, the 'shield and sword of the party'.
- **Explanation:** Ministry for State Security, with 91,015 full-time staff and about 189,000 unofficial informants in 1989. It spied on citizens, kept about 6 million files and used 'Zersetzung' (psychological harassment). Citizens occupied its offices in 1989–90 to stop file destruction.
- **German:** Ministerium für Staatssicherheit (MfS)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### state arbitration
<a id="state-arbitration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1923–1933
- **Also written as:** Arbitration
- **Short:** Weimar system in which state officials settled wage disputes between unions and employers.
- **Explanation:** When the two sides could not agree, state arbitrators could impose binding wage settlements. The KB lists it as part of the Weimar state's role in the economy.
- **German:** Schlichtung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### state of siege
<a id="state-of-siege"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Also written as:** State of siege
- **Short:** Emergency law giving military commanders wide powers over civilian life.
- **Explanation:** Based on an 1851 Prussian law, it let regional generals control the press, meetings and food supply in WWI and suspended or curtailed civil rights.
- **German:** Belagerungszustand
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### State within the state
<a id="state-within-the-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** state within the state
- **Short:** Description of the SPD's all-embracing counter-society.
- **Explanation:** Workers were excluded from state power but organised their own world of party, unions, clubs and press.
- **German:** Staat im Staate
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### state–church agreement
<a id="statechurch-agreement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1978
- **Short:** 1978 agreement between the GDR state and the Protestant church.
- **Explanation:** Meeting of 6 March 1978 between Honecker and church leaders that granted the church some autonomy, creating a space that later sheltered peace and environmental groups.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Staufer
<a id="staufer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1138–1254
- **Short:** Royal dynasty (1138–1254) of Frederick Barbarossa and Frederick II; also called Hohenstaufen.
- **Explanation:** Staufer rulers sought power in Italy, while in Germany Frederick II's privileges recognised the princes' territorial lordship.

### Sterilisation Law
<a id="sterilisation-law"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1933
- **Also written as:** Forced sterilisation
- **Short:** July 1933 law allowing forced sterilisation of people deemed 'hereditarily ill'.
- **Explanation:** About 400,000 people were forcibly sterilised from 1934, based on 'racial hygiene' ideas widely accepted in medicine.
- **German:** Gesetz zur Verhütung erbkranken Nachwuchses

### Stern
<a id="stern"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1948–
- **Short:** West German weekly magazine that printed the 1971 abortion confession 'We've had abortions!'.
- **Explanation:** Popular illustrated weekly. Its 1971 cover, on which hundreds of women, some of them famous, declared 'We've had abortions!', was a key moment in the West German women's movement's campaign against the abortion ban.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

### Stiftung Berliner Mauer
<a id="stiftung-berliner-mauer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Berlin Wall Foundation, which runs the Bernauer Straße memorial and researches Wall victims.
- **Explanation:** Foundation whose research project with the ZZF established that at least 140 people died at the Berlin Wall.
- **German:** Stiftung Berliner Mauer

<a id="stinneslegien-agreement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1918
- **Also written as:** Stinnes–Legien · Stinnes–Legien agreement
- **Short:** 1918 pact in which employers recognised trade unions as bargaining partners.
- **Explanation:** Named after industrialist Hugo Stinnes and union leader Carl Legien. It established collective bargaining and is a root of Germany's social partnership.

<a id="strasbourg-oaths"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 842
- **Short:** Oaths of 842 recorded in early Romance and early German.
- **Explanation:** Sworn by two grandsons of Charlemagne and their armies, they show a language boundary within the Frankish elite — not two nations.

### strategic autonomy
<a id="strategic-autonomy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** European strategic autonomy
- **Short:** Idea that Europe should be able to act, including militarily, without depending on others.
- **Explanation:** Debated especially since US reliability came into question and Russia invaded Ukraine.

### Strategic bombing
<a id="strategic-bombing"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** strategic bombing
- **Short:** Air attacks on cities and industry to destroy war production and morale.
- **Explanation:** Allied bombing from 1942 destroyed most large German cities; estimates of civilian dead range from about 350,000 to 600,000.

### Strength Through Joy
<a id="strength-through-joy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Also written as:** KdF
- **Short:** Nazi leisure organisation offering cheap holidays, concerts and cruises.
- **Explanation:** KdF organised millions of short trips and some cruises, making the regime feel like it improved workers' lives. It also ran the savings scheme for the promised Volkswagen.

### Stunde Null
<a id="stunde-null"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** zero hour
- **Short:** 'Zero hour': the idea that 1945 was a complete new beginning.
- **Explanation:** Historians call it a myth: people, institutions and attitudes carried over, especially in the West, and it obscured Germans' participation in Nazi crimes.

### Sturm und Drang
<a id="sturm-und-drang"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology

- **Short:** 'Storm and Stress': literary revolt stressing emotion and genius against rationalism.
- **Explanation:** Part of the late 18th-century reaction against Enlightenment rationalism, whose ideas later fed cultural nationalism.
- **German:** Sturm und Drang
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
