### Stuttgart Declaration of Guilt
<a id="stuttgart-declaration-of-guilt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1945
- **Short:** Protestant Church's October 1945 confession of guilt.
- **Explanation:** In October 1945 the Protestant Church leadership admitted it had failed to resist Nazism. It was vague about specifics such as the murder of the Jews but was a significant early admission.
- **German:** Stuttgarter Schuldbekenntnis
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Subhuman
<a id="subhuman"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** subhuman
- **Short:** Nazi slur used to describe Slavs and Jews as less than human.
- **Explanation:** The label justified mass killing and colonisation in the East.
- **German:** Untermensch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### subsidies
<a id="subsidies"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Payments from one state to another to fund troops or wars.
- **Explanation:** German princes hired out troops for foreign subsidies (Hesse-Kassel to Britain); Britain paid Prussia subsidies in the Seven Years' War.
- **German:** Subsidien
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/01_germanic_societies.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### subsistence agriculture
<a id="subsistence-agriculture"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Subsistence agriculture
- **Short:** Farming mainly to feed one's own household rather than to sell.
- **Explanation:** The economic base of early Germanic-speaking societies, with cattle as the main form of wealth.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### substitute emperor
<a id="substitute-emperor"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Nickname for the strong Weimar president, seen as a replacement for the Kaiser.
- **Explanation:** The constitution's makers intended the president as a counterweight to parliament; his emergency powers made him, in practice, a monarch-like figure.
- **German:** Ersatzkaiser
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Suffrage
<a id="suffrage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** suffrage
- **Short:** The right to vote.
- **Explanation:** In 19th-century Germany suffrage varied: universal male suffrage for the Reichstag, but unequal systems like Prussia's three-class vote in the states. Women gained the vote only in 1918.
- **German:** Wahlrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### sumptuary laws
<a id="sumptuary-laws"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Short:** Laws regulating dress and consumption by social rank.
- **Explanation:** Townspeople's clothing was prescribed by their rank, part of the dense regulation of urban life by town councils and guilds.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### superfluous generation
<a id="superfluous-generation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Historian Detlev Peukert's term for young Weimar men facing unemployment.
- **Explanation:** Without jobs or prospects, these young men were drawn to youth movements and paramilitaries.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Supreme Army Command
<a id="supreme-army-command"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1914–1919
- **Also written as:** OHL · Third Supreme Army Command
- **Short:** The German army's high command in WWI; under Hindenburg and Ludendorff it dominated politics.
- **Explanation:** From August 1916 its 'third' leadership claimed control over domestic and foreign policy, forced out Chancellor Bethmann Hollweg, imposed unrestricted submarine warfare and set war aims. In September 1918 it admitted defeat itself — before the revolution.
- **German:** Oberste Heeresleitung (OHL)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Surplus of women
<a id="surplus-of-women"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** surplus of women
- **Short:** Post-war demographic imbalance with far more women than men.
- **Explanation:** Because millions of men had died or were prisoners of war, post-war Germany had far more women than men; women carried much of the survival work.
- **German:** Frauenüberschuss
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### sustainability
<a id="sustainability"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 18th century
- **Short:** The idea of using a resource only as fast as it regrows, first formulated in Saxon forestry.
- **Explanation:** German forest managers facing timber shortages for Saxony's mines formulated the principle of 'sustainable' use in the early 18th century (Hans Carl von Carlowitz, 1713). The KB lists this as a topic in environmental history.
- **German:** Nachhaltigkeit
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Swabian League
<a id="swabian-league"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Late-medieval alliance of towns in Swabia, southwest Germany, for defence and trade.
- **Explanation:** One of the town leagues through which cities sought security against princes and robber knights where the king could not help.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Swastika
<a id="swastika"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** swastika
- **Short:** The hooked cross, symbol of the Nazi party.
- **Explanation:** The hooked cross was the Nazi party's emblem and the national flag's central symbol from 1935. US troops blew up the giant swastika at the Nuremberg rally grounds in April 1945 — the symbolic end of the regime.
- **German:** Hakenkreuz
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Swing Youth
<a id="swing-youth"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Young people who loved jazz and swing music and rejected Hitler Youth conformity.
- **Explanation:** Mostly middle-class teenagers in cities like Hamburg, they were harassed and some were sent to camps. Historians see their behaviour as nonconformity ('Resistenz') rather than political resistance.
- **German:** Swingjugend
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### symbolic communication
<a id="symbolic-communication"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Communicating power through rituals, ceremonies and seating orders.
- **Explanation:** Althoff and Stollberg-Rilinger argue these rituals were the Empire's constitution: power was performed and negotiated rather than administered.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### System of avoided decisions
<a id="system-of-avoided-decisions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** system of avoided decisions
- **Short:** Mommsen's description of the Empire: monarchy and democracy coexisting unresolved.
- **Explanation:** Neither the monarchical nor the democratic principle won out in the 1871 constitution.
- **German:** System umgangener Entscheidungen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB

## T

### Take-off
<a id="take-off"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1840s–1873
- **Also written as:** take-off
- **Short:** The phase when industrial growth became self-sustaining, in Germany from the 1840s/1850s.
- **Explanation:** Economists' term for the breakthrough of industrialisation. In Germany it was driven by railways, coal and iron in the Rhineland, Saxony, Silesia and Berlin.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### tax-sharing system
<a id="tax-sharing-system"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Tax-sharing system
- **Short:** Arrangement dividing income, VAT and corporate taxes among federation, Länder and municipalities.
- **Explanation:** Most big taxes are shared between the levels of government by fixed formulas, tying them together financially.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### technical universities
<a id="technical-universities"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Higher schools of engineering and science that rose to university rank around 1900.
- **Explanation:** Their growth in the Kaiserreich supported Germany's world-leading science and industry.
- **German:** Technische Hochschulen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Teleology
<a id="teleology"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** teleological · teleology
- **Short:** Reading history as if it inevitably led to a known outcome.
- **Explanation:** The KB warns against teleological views such as 'the road to 1933 started in 1871' or 1848's failure leading straight to Nazism.
- **German:** Teleologie
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ten-Point Plan
<a id="ten-point-plan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Short:** Kohl's November 1989 plan for moving towards German unity via confederative structures.
- **Explanation:** Programme announced by Chancellor Kohl on 28 November 1989 proposing steps from cooperation and confederative structures to eventual unity.
- **German:** Zehn-Punkte-Plan
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### territorial estates
<a id="territorial-estates"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Territorial estates · Landstände · estates
- **Ambiguous forms:** estates (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Assemblies of nobles, towns and sometimes clergy or peasants that granted a prince taxes.
- **Explanation:** Their strength varied: weakened in Brandenburg-Prussia and Bavaria, strong in Württemberg and Mecklenburg. They defended privileges and could refuse taxes or sue their prince.
- **German:** Landstände
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/01_reformation.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### territorial towns
<a id="territorial-towns"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Also written as:** Territorial towns
- **Short:** Towns subject to a prince or bishop.
- **Explanation:** Towns under a territorial lord rather than the emperor. Their autonomy varied; many had charters and councils of their own.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Teutonici
<a id="teutonici"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Latin 'Germans': a name others used for the king's subjects in the Middle Ages.
- **Explanation:** Its use shows a 'German people' becoming visible to outsiders from the 10th century.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md`](../medieval/02_holy_roman_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### The Large Miseries of War
<a id="large-miseries-of-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1633
- **Also written as:** Large Miseries of War
- **Short:** Jacques Callot's 1633 etching series on soldiers' violence and punishment.
- **Explanation:** Scenes of pillaging and hangings show the war as experienced by villagers.
- **German:** Les Grandes Misères de la guerre
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### The Paradox of German Power
<a id="the-paradox-of-german-power"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2014
- **Short:** Hans Kundnani's 2014 book on Germany's role in Europe.
- **Explanation:** Cited in the KB's debate on whether Germany is a hegemon in Europe; interpretations of German leadership remain contested.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### The People's State
<a id="the-people-s-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2005
- **Short:** Mary Fulbrook's 2005 social history of East Germany.
- **Explanation:** Book arguing that the GDR was a 'participatory dictatorship' in which many citizens took part and everyday life was often 'normal'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### the Terror
<a id="the-terror"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1793–1794
- **Short:** Period of mass executions during the French Revolution.
- **Explanation:** It turned many German intellectuals from supporters of the Revolution into opponents.
- **German:** Terrorherrschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### The West
<a id="the-west"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** West · Western · the West
- **Ambiguous forms:** West · Western (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The liberal democracies of Western Europe and North America.
- **Explanation:** In the Sonderweg debate, Germany's path is compared with a supposed 'Western' path to bourgeois democracy.
- **German:** der Westen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/education.md` · `themes/religion.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### theodiscus
<a id="theodiscus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Late 8th-century Latin word for the vernacular language(s) east of the Romance zone; root of 'deutsch'.
- **Explanation:** It comes from a vernacular word meaning 'people' and referred to language, not to a nation — evidence that 'German' began as a linguistic label.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Theresian and Josephine reforms
<a id="theresian-josephine-reforms"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1740–1790
- **Short:** Reforms under Maria Theresa and Joseph II modernising the Habsburg monarchy from above.
- **Explanation:** They centralised administration, introduced compulsory schooling, reduced peasant labour services, abolished personal serfdom in Bohemia and granted religious toleration; much was withdrawn after revolts.
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### thing
<a id="thing"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Assembly of free, arms-bearing men in early Germanic-speaking societies.
- **Explanation:** Tacitus describes such assemblies deciding major matters, but he was idealising and practice varied. Women, the unfree and people without kin had no place in them.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `00_README.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** Highlight only the italicised term *thing*; elsewhere it is the ordinary English word.

### Third Reich
<a id="third-reich"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1933–1945
- **Short:** Common name for Nazi Germany, 1933–45.
- **Explanation:** The name presented the regime as successor to the medieval empire and Bismarck's empire.
- **German:** Drittes Reich
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### third way
<a id="third-way"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A path between Western capitalism and Soviet-style socialism.
- **Explanation:** Goal of many East German civil-rights activists in 1989–90: a democratic, ecological socialism in a reformed GDR rather than accession to the West.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Thirty Years' War
<a id="thirty-years-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1618–1648
- **Short:** Series of wars fought mostly on German soil over religion, the Empire's constitution and European power.
- **Explanation:** Mortality estimates range from about 20% to 30–40% of the Empire's people, mostly from disease and hunger, with huge regional variation. Later nationalists blamed it for German 'backwardness' — an interpretation now largely rejected.
- **German:** Dreißigjähriger Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `00_README.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: death toll (20–40%) and whether it was mainly religious or political.

### three orders
<a id="three-orders"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Clerical model of society divided into those who pray, fight and work.
- **Explanation:** A model written by clerics, not a description of reality: it presented inequality between clergy, nobles and peasants as willed by God.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Three-class suffrage
<a id="three-class-suffrage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1849–1918
- **Also written as:** three-class suffrage · three-class system · three-class
- **Short:** Prussian voting system giving the richest taxpayers most weight (1849–1918).
- **Explanation:** Voters were grouped into three classes by taxes paid; each class elected a third of the electors. A small wealthy minority outweighed the majority. It lasted until 1918.
- **German:** Dreiklassenwahlrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/democracy.md` · `themes/political_power.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### three-field agriculture
<a id="three-field-agriculture"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Three-field agriculture
- **Short:** Farming rotation of winter grain, summer grain and fallow.
- **Explanation:** It spread from the 9th to 12th centuries; village communities regulated the common fields.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Thuringians
<a id="thuringians"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Early medieval people of central Germany, later conquered by the Franks.
- **Explanation:** A grouping that appears in late antiquity, probably formed in response to Roman pressure. Their name lives on in today's state of Thuringia.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Thyssen
<a id="thyssen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Ruhr steel and mining company founded by August Thyssen.
- **Explanation:** Named as one of the great industrial enterprises of the Empire's bourgeoisie.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### tithe
<a id="tithe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** tithes · small tithe
- **Short:** Church tax of one tenth of produce or income.
- **Explanation:** Introduced with Christianisation, the tithe connected every village to the Church and was a constant burden for peasants.
- **German:** Zehnt
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### toleration
<a id="toleration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1930–1932
- **Also written as:** tolerated
- **Ambiguous forms:** tolerated (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The SPD's policy of not overturning Brüning's decrees, to avoid something worse.
- **Explanation:** Because the Reichstag could cancel decrees only by majority, the SPD's toleration kept Brüning's presidential cabinet in place.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
