### Toleration Patents
<a id="toleration-patents"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1781–1782
- **Also written as:** Toleration Patent
- **Short:** Joseph II's decrees granting religious toleration to Protestants, Orthodox Christians and Jews.
- **Explanation:** The 1782 patent for Vienna's Jews was part of an emancipation framed as conditional on Jews 'improving' themselves.
- **German:** Toleranzpatent
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### tonsure
<a id="tonsure"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** tonsured
- **Short:** Shaving of the head marking entry into the clergy or a monastery.
- **Explanation:** Carolingian rulers disposed of rivals by having them tonsured and sent to monasteries, removing them from politics.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Topf & Söhne
<a id="topf-sohne"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Erfurt engineering firm that built crematorium ovens for Auschwitz and other camps.
- **Explanation:** Its role shows how ordinary companies were involved in the genocide.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Toronto School
<a id="toronto-school"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Historians (Goffart, Gillett) who doubt early 'peoples' were real and the migrations as told.
- **Explanation:** A group of historians linked to Toronto who criticise the Vienna School: many 'peoples' were Roman categories or post-Roman constructions, and the migration narrative is overstated.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: debate with the Vienna School.

### Total war
<a id="total-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** total war
- **Short:** Mobilising every person and resource for the war effort.
- **Explanation:** After Stalingrad the regime proclaimed total war; in 1944 Goebbels became its plenipotentiary.
- **German:** totaler Krieg
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### totalitarianism
<a id="totalitarianism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Totalitarianism theory · Totalitarian dictatorship
- **Short:** Rule by a regime seeking total control over society and individuals.
- **Explanation:** Theory grouping Nazi and communist dictatorships by shared features. Applied to the GDR by Klaus Schroeder and the Bundestag inquiry commissions, it emphasises repression; most historians compare structures while stressing fundamental differences in aims and crimes.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether 'totalitarian' is the best characterisation of the GDR is debated.

### Trabant
<a id="trabant"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1957–1991
- **Short:** Small East German car, often ordered years in advance.
- **Explanation:** The GDR's standard car, with a plastic body and two-stroke engine. East Germans often waited many years for one — a symbol of the shortage economy.
- **German:** Trabant (Trabi)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### trade surplus
<a id="trade-surplus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** trade surpluses
- **Short:** When a country exports more than it imports.
- **Explanation:** Germany's large and persistent trade surpluses, based on its export model, have been criticised by EU partners as creating imbalances.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### transfer payments
<a id="transfer-payments"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** Transfers
- **Short:** Money moved from western to eastern Germany after 1990 to rebuild the East.
- **Explanation:** Public funds shifted from West to East through taxes, social insurance and special programmes. Net transfers since 1990 are estimated at well over €1.5–2 trillion, though estimates vary.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Transformation of the Roman World
<a id="transformation-of-the-roman-world"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Research programme stressing continuity and gradual change at the end of Roman rule.
- **Explanation:** A European research programme of the 1990s. Its 'transformation' view is set against Bryan Ward-Perkins's 'decline and fall'.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: transformation vs decline and fall.

### transit traffic
<a id="transit-traffic"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** transit fees
- **Short:** Travel between West Germany and West Berlin across GDR territory.
- **Explanation:** Road and rail traffic through the GDR to West Berlin; the Federal Republic paid the GDR transit fees, a significant hard-currency income.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### travel cadres
<a id="travel-cadres"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Privileged GDR citizens allowed to travel to the West for official purposes.
- **Explanation:** Reisekader: trusted functionaries, scientists, athletes and artists permitted to travel to non-socialist countries, while most East Germans could not.
- **German:** Reisekader
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treaties of Rome
<a id="treaties-of-rome"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1957
- **Short:** 1957 treaties that founded the European Economic Community (EEC); West Germany was a founder.
- **Explanation:** Signed by France, West Germany, Italy and the Benelux states, they created a common market and a European atomic energy community. They anchored the Federal Republic in Western Europe and are the basis of today's European Union.
- **German:** Römische Verträge
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treaty of Brest-Litovsk
<a id="treaty-of-brest-litovsk"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** March 1918
- **Short:** Harsh peace treaty Germany imposed on Soviet Russia in March 1918.
- **Explanation:** It placed huge eastern territories under German control and shows the regime's far-reaching war aims.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treaty of Frankfurt
<a id="treaty-of-frankfurt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1871
- **Also written as:** Frankfurt Peace
- **Short:** May 1871 peace ending the Franco-Prussian War.
- **Explanation:** France ceded Alsace-Lorraine and paid 5 billion francs, feeding a German speculative boom and lasting Franco-German enmity.
- **German:** Friede von Frankfurt
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Treaty of Lisbon
<a id="treaty-of-lisbon"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 2009
- **Also written as:** Lisbon
- **Ambiguous forms:** Lisbon (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** EU reform treaty (in force 2009) that sets today's EU institutional rules.
- **Explanation:** It reorganised how the EU works. The Constitutional Court's 2009 'Lisbon' ruling approved it but reserved the right to check EU acts against Germany's constitutional identity.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** 'Lisbon 2009' in the KB can mean the treaty or the Constitutional Court's ruling on it.

### Treaty of Lunéville
<a id="treaty-of-luneville"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1801
- **Also written as:** Lunéville
- **Short:** Peace of 1801 in which the Empire ceded the Rhine's left bank to France.
- **Explanation:** The German princes who lost land there were promised compensation — which led to the Reichsdeputationshauptschluss of 1803.
- **German:** Friede von Lunéville
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treaty of Verdun
<a id="treaty-of-verdun"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 843
- **Also written as:** Verdun
- **Ambiguous forms:** Verdun (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** 843 division of the Frankish empire among Charlemagne's grandsons.
- **Explanation:** Its eastern share, East Francia, became the core of the later German kingdom. Historians reject the idea that it 'created Germany and France': it divided an inheritance. Verdun lies in northeastern France.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treaty of Versailles
<a id="treaty-of-versailles"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1919
- **Also written as:** Versailles 1919 · Versailles · Versailles Treaty
- **Ambiguous forms:** Versailles (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** The 1919 peace treaty imposed on Germany after WWI.
- **Explanation:** It imposed reparations, limited the army to 100,000 men and contained the 'war guilt' clause. Resentment of it was universal among non-left Germans, but on its own it does not explain Nazism: the Nazis were marginal until 1930.
- **German:** Versailler Vertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Treuhandanstalt
<a id="treuhandanstalt"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1990–1994
- **Also written as:** Treuhand
- **Short:** Agency that privatised or closed about 8,500 East German state firms, 1990–94.
- **Explanation:** The trust agency took over the GDR's state enterprises (about 4 million jobs). Most privatised firms went to West German buyers, and it ended with a deficit of about DM 260 billion. For many East Germans it symbolises lost jobs; historians debate how much harm was avoidable.
- **German:** Treuhandanstalt
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: overwhelmed institution under impossible conditions vs avoidable harm serving West German interests.

### Turkish Germans
<a id="turkish-germans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Turkish-German
- **Short:** Germany's large community of Turkish origin, now in its third and fourth generation.
- **Explanation:** Descended mostly from guest workers recruited from Turkey after 1961. Many stayed permanently, making West Germany a country of immigration.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Turner
<a id="turner"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** from 1811
- **Also written as:** gymnasts
- **Short:** Nationalist gymnastics movement founded by Jahn in 1811.
- **Explanation:** Gymnastics clubs combined physical training with national politics. Since parties were illegal, such clubs served as proto-parties and celebrated a 'federative national consciousness'.
- **German:** Turner
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `themes/nationalism.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### turnip winter
<a id="turnip-winter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1916–17
- **Also written as:** Kohlrübenwinter · Steckrübenwinter
- **Short:** The hunger winter of 1916/17 when Germans lived largely on swede turnips.
- **Explanation:** The blockade, lack of fertiliser and labour, and a failed potato harvest left many city dwellers on official rations far below their needs. Hunger fuelled anger at profiteers and inequality, and later strikes.
- **German:** Kohlrübenwinter / Steckrübenwinter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### turnover tax
<a id="turnover-tax"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Also written as:** turnover taxes
- **Short:** Tax levied on the sale of goods, a major state revenue source in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** A sales-based tax; in the GDR it, together with enterprise surpluses, financed the state.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Twelve Articles
<a id="twelve-articles"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1525
- **Short:** The peasants' manifesto of 1525, demanding 'godly law' and an end to serfdom.
- **Explanation:** Drawn up by Swabian peasants, it demanded communal election of pastors, abolition of the small tithe, an end to serfdom ('Christ redeemed us all'), fair rents and access to woods, water and game.
- **German:** Zwölf Artikel
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### two kingdoms doctrine
<a id="two-kingdoms-doctrine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** two kingdoms
- **Short:** Luther's teaching separating spiritual freedom from a duty of obedience to worldly rulers.
- **Explanation:** Based on Romans 13 ('let every soul be subject to the higher powers'), it justified Luther's call to crush the rebelling peasants in 1525.
- **German:** Zwei-Reiche-Lehre
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Two Men Contemplating the Moon
<a id="two-men-contemplating-the-moon"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** c. 1825–30
- **Short:** Romantic painting by Caspar David Friedrich of two men in 'old German' dress.
- **Explanation:** The figures wear altdeutsche Tracht, a coded sign of liberal-national sympathy after the 1819 Carlsbad Decrees. The painting fuses nation, feeling and landscape — the Romantic mood of early German national feeling. This version is in the Metropolitan Museum of Art.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `03_MENTAL_MODEL.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/german_identity.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Two states, one nation
<a id="two-states-one-nation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1969
- **Short:** Brandt's 1969 formula: two German states, but still one German nation.
- **Explanation:** Accepted the GDR as a state while denying it was a foreign country, keeping the goal of national unity open.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Two-plus-Four Treaty
<a id="two-plus-four-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Also written as:** Two-plus-Four
- **Short:** 12 September 1990 treaty of the two German states and four Allies settling unification internationally.
- **Explanation:** It made borders final (including Oder–Neisse), capped German forces at 370,000, renounced nuclear, biological and chemical weapons, provided for Soviet withdrawal by 1994, let Germany choose its alliances and ended four-power rights.
- **German:** Zwei-plus-Vier-Vertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Tyrolean rebellion
<a id="tyrolean-rebellion"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1809–1810
- **Short:** 1809 uprising in Tyrol against Bavarian rule, led by Andreas Hofer.
- **Explanation:** It was crushed in 1809–10.
- **German:** Tiroler Volksaufstand
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## U

### Ubii
<a id="ubii"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** Germanic-speaking group resettled by the Romans on the left bank of the Rhine at Cologne.
- **Explanation:** An example of the mixed provincial population: Rome moved them to the Roman side of the Rhine, where they became part of the city that became Cologne.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ulm Einsatzgruppen trial
<a id="ulm-einsatzgruppen-trial"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1958
- **Short:** 1958 West German trial of members of a killing unit.
- **Explanation:** It revealed how many crimes had gone unpunished and led to a central office for investigating Nazi crimes.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ultra vires
<a id="ultra-vires"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Latin: 'beyond the powers' — an act exceeding an institution's legal authority.
- **Explanation:** The Constitutional Court reserves the right to declare EU acts ultra vires if they exceed powers the treaties grant, as in its 2020 PSPP ruling.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### ultramontanism
<a id="ultramontanism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** 19th c.
- **Short:** Catholic orientation toward the Pope in Rome ('beyond the mountains') rather than local rulers.
- **Explanation:** After losing its principalities in 1803, the German Catholic Church became more Rome-oriented and less aristocratic.
- **German:** Ultramontanismus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Umsiedler
<a id="umsiedler"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** resettlers
- **Short:** 'Resettlers': East German official term for expellees.
- **Explanation:** In East Germany, expellees from the lost eastern territories were officially called 'resettlers'. The communist state, allied with Poland and the USSR, did not allow them to mourn their lost homelands in public.
- **German:** Umsiedler
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Umweltbibliothek
<a id="umweltbibliothek"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1986–
- **Short:** 'Environmental Library' in East Berlin, an opposition centre sheltered by a church.
- **Explanation:** Opposition group based in the basement of a Protestant parish in East Berlin, collecting banned literature and producing underground newsletters on the environment and human rights.
- **German:** Umweltbibliothek
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Uncivil Society
<a id="uncivil-society"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2009
- **Short:** Stephen Kotkin's 2009 book stressing the implosion of communist elites in 1989.
- **Explanation:** Argues that communist regimes collapsed mainly because their ruling establishments imploded, rather than because of civil-society opposition.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Unconditional surrender
<a id="unconditional-surrender"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 7–8 May 1945
- **Also written as:** unconditional surrender
- **Short:** Germany's surrender without any terms, on 7–8 May 1945.
- **Explanation:** It ended the war in Europe and dissolved the German state; the Allies took over supreme authority.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### unemployment insurance
<a id="unemployment-insurance"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1927
- **Short:** State insurance paying benefits to the unemployed, introduced in 1927.
- **Explanation:** The dispute over funding it in the Depression broke the grand coalition in 1930, opening the door to decree government.
- **German:** Arbeitslosenversicherung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### UNESCO
<a id="unesco"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** United Nations cultural organisation that lists World Heritage sites.
- **Explanation:** Listed the Roman Limes and, in 2021, the ShUM sites of Speyer, Worms and Mainz as World Heritage.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `ancient/02_roman_frontier.md` · `medieval/03_medieval_society.md` · `themes/religion.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Ungleich vereint
<a id="ungleich-vereint"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2024
- **Short:** Steffen Mau's 2024 book on persisting East–West differences ('Unequally united').
- **Explanation:** Argues that East and West Germany remain distinct societies; cited in the KB's debate on eastern grievance and the radical right.
- **German:** Ungleich vereint
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: one side of the debate on causes of East German grievance.

### Unification Treaty
<a id="unification-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1990
- **Short:** 31 August 1990 treaty between the two German states setting the terms of accession.
- **Explanation:** Einigungsvertrag regulating how West German law, administration and institutions would be extended to the East on 3 October 1990.
- **German:** Einigungsvertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### United Nations
<a id="united-nations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1945–
- **Also written as:** UN
- **Short:** International organisation of states; both German states joined in 1973.
- **Explanation:** Admission of both the FRG and the GDR in 1973, after the Basic Treaty, marked international recognition of the GDR.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### unity lists
<a id="unity-lists"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1950–1989
- **Short:** Single lists of candidates in GDR elections, offering voters no real choice.
- **Explanation:** All parties and mass organisations ran on one joint list of the National Front with fixed seat quotas; voters could only approve it, so the SED's control was never at risk.
- **German:** Einheitslisten
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### unity of economic and social policy
<a id="unity-of-economic-and-social-policy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1971–1989
- **Short:** Honecker's policy of funding consumption and social benefits, largely by borrowing.
- **Explanation:** Programme announced in 1971 that expanded housing, subsidies and social benefits. Productivity did not keep pace, foreign debt grew, and by 1989 the economy was unsustainable.
- **German:** Einheit von Wirtschafts- und Sozialpolitik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Universal male suffrage
<a id="universal-male-suffrage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** universal male suffrage
- **Short:** The right of all adult men to vote, regardless of wealth.
- **Explanation:** Planned in 1849 and introduced for the Reichstag in 1867/1871 (men over 25) — one of Europe's broadest franchises, although parliament did not control the government.
- **German:** allgemeines Männerwahlrecht
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### University of Berlin
<a id="university-of-berlin"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** founded 1810
- **Also written as:** Berlin university
- **Short:** University founded in 1810 by Wilhelm von Humboldt as part of the Prussian reforms.
- **Explanation:** It embodied neohumanist ideals of education as self-formation. Today it is the Humboldt University.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### unofficial informants
<a id="unofficial-informants"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** IM · IMs
- **Short:** Ordinary citizens who secretly reported to the Stasi on people around them.
- **Explanation:** The 'inoffizielle Mitarbeiter' (IM): about 189,000 in 1989, or one per ~89 citizens. After 1990 many people discovered from their files that friends, colleagues or spouses had informed on them.
- **German:** Inoffizieller Mitarbeiter (IM)
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Unrechtsstaat
<a id="unrechtsstaat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'State without rule of law': a contested label for the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Used by the Bundestag and courts to characterise the GDR. Many East Germans reject it because it seems to dismiss their whole lives.
- **German:** Unrechtsstaat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: politically contested term.

### unrestricted submarine warfare
<a id="unrestricted-submarine-warfare"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** February 1917
- **Short:** Attacking all ships, including neutral ones, in waters around Britain without warning.
- **Explanation:** The army command imposed it in February 1917. It helped bring the United States into the war in April 1917.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Unser Kampf
<a id="unser-kampf"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 2008
- **Short:** 'Our Struggle': Götz Aly's controversial 2008 book on the 1968 movement.
- **Explanation:** Book in which Götz Aly calls the importance of 1968 an overrated myth and compares the students of 1968 with those of 1933. The title echoes Hitler's Mein Kampf.
- **German:** Unser Kampf
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Untertan
<a id="untertan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 1918
- **Short:** 'Subject' of a ruler; title of Heinrich Mann's satire of servile obedience in Imperial Germany.
- **Explanation:** Heinrich Mann's novel 'Der Untertan' (1918) mocked a man who bowed to those above and bullied those below. The 'Untertan mentality' became a cultural explanation of German obedience to authority, which the KB says is now treated cautiously.
- **German:** Untertan; Der Untertan
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: cultural explanations of German authoritarianism are now treated cautiously.

### Uprising of 17 June 1953
<a id="uprising-of-17-june-1953"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1953
- **Also written as:** 1953 uprising
- **Short:** East German workers' revolt crushed by Soviet tanks on 17 June 1953.
- **Explanation:** Strikes and protests that began in East Berlin over raised work quotas and spread across the GDR with political demands. Soviet troops crushed them — the 'workers' state' had to use force against workers.
- **German:** Volksaufstand vom 17. Juni 1953
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Urbanisation
<a id="urbanisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** urbanisation
- **Short:** Growth of cities as people move from the countryside.
- **Explanation:** In the Empire cities over 100,000 went from 8 to 48; by 1910, 21.3% lived in big cities.
- **German:** Urbanisierung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### US successor trials
<a id="us-successor-trials"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** successor trials
- **Short:** Twelve further US trials at Nuremberg of doctors, judges, industrialists, Einsatzgruppen and generals.
- **Explanation:** Held 1946–49 after the main tribunal, they showed the involvement of German elites.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### USHMM
<a id="ushmm"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** United States Holocaust Memorial Museum in Washington, D.C.
- **Explanation:** Its encyclopedia provides the victim estimates used in this chapter.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### USPD
<a id="uspd"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1917–1922
- **Short:** Independent Social Democrats: anti-war socialists who split from the SPD in 1917.
- **Explanation:** The split of the SPD over the war produced the USPD, which challenged the government during the war and joined the SPD in the Council of People's Deputies in November 1918.
- **German:** Unabhängige Sozialdemokratische Partei Deutschlands
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### usurpation
<a id="usurpation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** usurpations · usurpers
- **Short:** Seizing imperial power without legitimate appointment, often by army commanders.
- **Explanation:** In crises Roman frontier commanders repeatedly proclaimed themselves or their candidates emperor; usurpations helped weaken Rome in the 3rd century.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### usury
<a id="usury"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Lending money at interest, forbidden to Christians by the Church.
- **Explanation:** Together with exclusion from guilds, usury prohibitions pushed Jews into money-lending.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## V

### valley of the clueless
<a id="valley-of-the-clueless"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Nickname for the Dresden area, where West German TV could not be received.
- **Explanation:** Tal der Ahnungslosen: the region around Dresden, cut off from the Western broadcasts that most East Germans watched daily.
- **German:** Tal der Ahnungslosen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Varus battle
<a id="varus-battle"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 9 CE
- **Also written as:** Varus disaster · Varus defeat · Battle of the Teutoburg Forest
- **Short:** Destruction of three Roman legions by Arminius's warriors in 9 CE; later a national myth.
- **Explanation:** It ended Roman plans to conquer up to the Elbe. From the 16th and especially the 19th century it became a founding myth of the German nation; historians see a local power struggle. Its exact location is debated.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: Kalkriese is widely but not universally accepted as the battle site.

### vassal
<a id="vassal"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** vassals
- **Short:** Person bound to a lord by an oath of loyalty and service, usually in return for a fief.
- **Explanation:** Vassals owed loyalty, counsel and military service. In Germany the fiefs they held became hereditary and princes treated them as property.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Also in:** `medieval/01_carolingian_world.md` · `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### VAT
<a id="vat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Value-added tax: a sales tax charged on goods and services.
- **Explanation:** One of the main taxes shared between the federation, the Länder and the municipalities in Germany's tax-sharing system.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### VEB
<a id="veb"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** 'People's enterprise': a state-owned firm in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Volkseigener Betrieb ('people-owned enterprise'), the standard form of company in the GDR after nationalisation.
- **German:** Volkseigener Betrieb
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Verdachtsfall
<a id="verdachtsfall"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Suspected case': official label for a group suspected of extremism and monitored as such.
- **Explanation:** Classification by the domestic intelligence service allowing it to observe an organisation it suspects of anti-democratic aims. The AfD is classified as such a suspected case.
- **German:** Verdachtsfall
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Verein für Socialpolitik
<a id="verein-fur-socialpolitik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1872–
- **Short:** Association of reform-minded economists founded 1872.
- **Explanation:** Its members, the 'armchair socialists', urged state social policy.
- **German:** Verein für Socialpolitik
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Vereine
<a id="vereine"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** Associations · associations
- **Ambiguous forms:** Associations · associations (see `ambiguous_forms.md`)
- **Short:** Voluntary associations or clubs, e.g. gymnastics, singing and shooting clubs.
- **Explanation:** With political parties banned before 1848, clubs acted as unofficial national networks. In the Empire, Germany became a 'society of associations'.
- **German:** Vereine
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/03_modern_society.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vereinigte Stahlwerke
<a id="vereinigte-stahlwerke"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1926–
- **Short:** Giant German steel trust formed in 1926.
- **Explanation:** Like IG Farben (1925), it was one of the big concerns of the Weimar economy and an example of German 'organised capitalism', dominated by cartels and large firms.
- **German:** Vereinigte Stahlwerke AG
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vergangenheitsbewältigung
<a id="vergangenheitsbewaltigung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Coming to terms with the past': Germany's efforts to confront the Nazi era.
- **Explanation:** German word for the process of facing up to Nazi crimes. In West Germany it moved from silence and self-pity in the 1950s to confrontation from the 1960s through trials, the 1968 generation, the TV series Holocaust (1979) and Weizsäcker's 1985 speech.
- **German:** Vergangenheitsbewältigung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vergangenheitspolitik
<a id="vergangenheitspolitik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1996
- **Short:** 'Politics of the past': Norbert Frei's 1996 study of how early West Germany treated ex-Nazis.
- **Explanation:** Book by historian Norbert Frei on the amnesties, reintegration and limited prosecution of former Nazis in the early Federal Republic. The title became a standard term for such policies.
- **German:** Vergangenheitspolitik
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### veterans' associations
<a id="veterans-associations"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Also written as:** veterans' leagues
- **Short:** Clubs of former soldiers that spread military values and nationalism.
- **Explanation:** In the Kaiserreich they carried army prestige into society; after 1918 veterans' leagues were among the main carriers of revisionist, anti-republican nationalism.
- **German:** Kriegervereine
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### VfZ
<a id="vfz"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** since 1953
- **Short:** Vierteljahrshefte für Zeitgeschichte, a leading German journal of contemporary history.
- **Explanation:** Quarterly journal published by the Institute for Contemporary History (IfZ) in Munich. The KB cites articles from it, such as Ursula Büttner's 1989 essay on alternatives to Brüning's austerity and research on the Treuhand.
- **German:** Vierteljahrshefte für Zeitgeschichte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vienna Final Act
<a id="vienna-final-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1820
- **Short:** 1820 treaty that completed the German Confederation's constitution.
- **Explanation:** Together with the Federal Act of 1815 it framed the Confederation as a defensive league with the right to intervene in member states.
- **German:** Wiener Schlussakte
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vienna School
<a id="vienna-school"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Historians (Wolfram, Pohl) who see early peoples as formed around elite-carried traditions.
- **Explanation:** A school of early medieval history centred in Vienna. It holds that peoples were formed ('ethnogenesis') around 'nuclei of tradition' carried by elites, making identity flexible and political.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: challenged by the Toronto School.

### Vietnam War
<a id="vietnam-war"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1955–1975
- **Short:** US war in Vietnam, a major target of 1960s student protests worldwide.
- **Explanation:** Long war in which the United States fought communist North Vietnam and its allies in South Vietnam. Protests against it mobilised West German students in the late 1960s.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vikings
<a id="vikings"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** Viking
- **Short:** Scandinavian seaborne raiders who attacked Frankish lands in the 9th–10th centuries.
- **Explanation:** Their raids demanded local defence, which strengthened regional leaders (dukes) at the expense of the Carolingian kings.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/01_carolingian_world.md`](../medieval/01_carolingian_world.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### villa
<a id="villa"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** villas
- **Short:** Roman country estate, a farming business supplying towns and the army.
- **Explanation:** Rural villas and farms in the Rhine provinces supplied the Roman army with food.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### viticulture
<a id="viticulture"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Growing grapes for wine.
- **Explanation:** Introduced to the Moselle and Rhine by the Romans; the Roman/non-Roman line still shows on Germany's map of wine regions.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/02_roman_frontier.md`](../ancient/02_roman_frontier.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vocational Training Act
<a id="vocational-training-act"></a>

- **Kind:** term · law/decree
- **Dates:** 1969
- **Short:** 1969 law regulating Germany's apprenticeship (dual training) system.
- **Explanation:** It set national rules for training in companies combined with vocational schooling.
- **German:** Berufsbildungsgesetz
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volga Germans
<a id="volga-germans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Dates:** from 1760s
- **Short:** German settlers who emigrated to the Volga region of Russia from the 1760s.
- **Explanation:** Their emigration was one of several 18th-century outflows from the German territories, alongside Hungary and North America.
- **German:** Wolgadeutsche
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`](../early_modern/03_territorial_states.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volk
<a id="volk"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** German for 'people' or 'nation' — later charged with ethnic and racial meaning.
- **Explanation:** Herder saw each Volk as having a unique culture, in a plural, non-hierarchical sense. Nazi-era scholarship (e.g. Franz's 1940 estimates) used 'Volk' in an ethnic, racial sense.
- **German:** Volk
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Völkerschauen
<a id="volkerschauen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** human zoos
- **Short:** 'Human zoos': shows displaying colonised people, e.g. by Hagenbeck in Hamburg.
- **Explanation:** They spread racial hierarchies in popular culture.
- **German:** Völkerschauen
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md`](../nineteenth_century/08_colonialism.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Völkerwanderung
<a id="volkerwanderung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** migration of peoples
- **Short:** 'Migration of peoples': the traditional name for group movements across Europe c. 375–568.
- **Explanation:** The old image of whole peoples wandering intact across Europe is now considered too simple: groups formed and re-formed as they moved (ethnogenesis).
- **German:** Völkerwanderung
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: Vienna and Toronto Schools disagree on how far migrating 'peoples' were real communities.

### völkisch
<a id="volkisch"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** German term for racist, ethnic-nationalist politics centred on the 'Volk' (people/race).
- **Explanation:** The NSDAP began in 1919–20 as one of many tiny völkisch groups in Munich.
- **German:** völkisch
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `themes/nationalism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Völkischer Beobachter
<a id="volkischer-beobachter"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1920–1945
- **Short:** The Nazi party newspaper.
- **Explanation:** It celebrated the Enabling Act in March 1933.
- **German:** Völkischer Beobachter
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volksgemeinschaft
<a id="volksgemeinschaft"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Also written as:** people's community
- **Short:** 'People's community': the Nazi ideal of a united racial nation that excluded 'outsiders'.
- **Explanation:** The term promised recognition, security and unity to Germans the regime deemed racially and politically acceptable, while excluding Jews and others. Historians debate whether it was reality or propaganda and whether it is a useful concept.
- **German:** Volksgemeinschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Also in:** `04_QUESTIONS_WORTH_EXPLORING.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/german_identity.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether it was a reality or a propaganda fiction, and whether it is a useful analytic concept.

### Volksgenosse
<a id="volksgenosse"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** national comrade
- **Short:** 'National comrade': a racially and politically accepted member of the Nazi community.
- **Explanation:** Rights depended on having this status.
- **German:** Volksgenosse
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volkshaus
<a id="volkshaus"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1849
- **Short:** 'House of the People': planned elected lower chamber of the 1849 constitution.
- **Explanation:** It was to be elected by universal, equal, secret male suffrage.
- **German:** Volkshaus
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Volkskammer
<a id="volkskammer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–1990
- **Also written as:** People's Chamber
- **Short:** The GDR's parliament (People's Chamber), a rubber stamp until 1989–90.
- **Explanation:** Parliament elected on single 'unity lists' with about 99% approval; seats were pre-allotted to parties and mass organisations and votes were unanimous. After the first free election of 18 March 1990 it voted for accession to the Federal Republic.
- **German:** Volkskammer
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `themes/political_power.md` · `twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volksschule
<a id="volksschule"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Elementary school for the mass of children.
- **Explanation:** In the Empire's tiered school system, most attended only Volksschule.
- **German:** Volksschule
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/education.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Volkssturm
<a id="volkssturm"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1944–1945
- **Short:** Last-ditch militia of older men and boys created in 1944.
- **Explanation:** Poorly armed and trained, it was mobilised to defend the Reich in the final months.
- **German:** Volkssturm
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Volkswagen
<a id="volkswagen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** The Nazi 'people's car', promised to savers but never delivered to them.
- **Explanation:** Workers paid into a savings scheme run by Strength Through Joy, but the factory switched to war production and savers received no cars.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vollbauern
<a id="vollbauern"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Wealthy peasants holding a full farm.
- **Explanation:** The top layer of village society, above cottagers and landless labourers.
- **German:** Vollbauern
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Vormärz
<a id="vormarz"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** c. 1815/1830–1848
- **Short:** 'Pre-March': the period before the March Revolution of 1848.
- **Explanation:** Used for the years of censorship and growing liberal, national and democratic opposition leading up to 1848.
- **German:** Vormärz
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Also in:** `00_README.md` · `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Vorwärts
<a id="vorwarts"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** from 1876
- **Short:** 'Forward!': the SPD's central newspaper.
- **Explanation:** One of dozens of Social Democratic dailies.
- **German:** Vorwärts
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### vote of confidence
<a id="vote-of-confidence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** confidence votes
- **Short:** Parliamentary vote in which the Chancellor asks the Bundestag to confirm its support.
- **Explanation:** If the Chancellor loses it, the Federal President may dissolve the Bundestag and call new elections — as happened in 1972, 1982/83, 2005 and December 2024.
- **German:** Vertrauensfrage
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Vote of no confidence
<a id="vote-of-no-confidence"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** vote of no confidence
- **Short:** Parliamentary vote withdrawing support from a government.
- **Explanation:** In the Empire such a vote (1913) had no effect since the Chancellor answered to the Kaiser.
- **German:** Misstrauensvotum
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### votive deposits
<a id="votive-deposits"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Objects deliberately placed in bogs, lakes or sacred sites as offerings to gods.
- **Explanation:** A main archaeological trace of early Germanic-speaking religion, alongside sacred groves.
- **Main chapter:** [`ancient/01_germanic_societies.md`](../ancient/01_germanic_societies.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## W

### Waffen-SS
<a id="waffen-ss"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** The armed military branch of the SS.
- **Explanation:** Part of Himmler's SS empire.
- **German:** Waffen-SS
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wandel durch Annäherung
<a id="wandel-durch-annaherung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1963
- **Also written as:** Change through rapprochement
- **Short:** 'Change through rapprochement': Egon Bahr's formula for engaging the East to change it.
- **Explanation:** Idea, set out by Egon Bahr in 1963, that recognising the realities of division and building contacts would gradually transform the communist states — the basis of Ostpolitik.
- **German:** Wandel durch Annäherung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wandel durch Handel
<a id="wandel-durch-handel"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** change through trade
- **Short:** 'Change through trade': belief that economic ties would moderate Russia.
- **Explanation:** Part of Germany's Russia policy, combined with the Ostpolitik tradition and energy interests. Since 2022 it is widely judged a failure, though debate continues.
- **German:** Wandel durch Handel
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: judged a failure by many since 2022; debate continues.

### Wannsee Conference
<a id="wannsee-conference"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 20 January 1942
- **Also written as:** Wannsee
- **Short:** Meeting of officials on 20 January 1942 to coordinate the murder of Europe's Jews.
- **Explanation:** Held in a villa by Berlin's Wannsee lake, it did not decide on the Holocaust but organised a decision already made; mass shootings had been going on since mid-1941.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### war commissariat
<a id="war-commissariat"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Government office supplying and financing a standing army.
- **Explanation:** In Brandenburg it helped the Great Elector bypass the estates; it grew into the core of Prussian administration.
- **German:** Kriegskommissariat
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md`](../early_modern/04_prussia_and_austria.md)
- **Also in:** `early_modern/03_territorial_states.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### war council of December 1912
<a id="war-council-of-december-1912"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** December 1912
- **Also written as:** war council
- **Short:** A 1912 meeting of the Kaiser with military leaders, cited as evidence of German war planning.
- **Explanation:** Historians critical of Clark cite it as evidence of deliberate German risk-taking before 1914; its significance is part of the war-origins debate.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: its significance is debated.

### war credits
<a id="war-credits"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1914
- **Also written as:** War credits
- **Short:** Parliamentary approval of money to pay for the war.
- **Explanation:** The SPD's vote for war credits in 1914 was a turning point for the socialist movement and part of the political truce (Burgfrieden).
- **German:** Kriegskredite
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### war economy
<a id="war-economy"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1914–1918
- **Also written as:** War economy
- **Short:** Economy reorganised by the state to serve the needs of war.
- **Explanation:** In the First World War private firms worked under military direction, labour was steered by the Auxiliary Service Law and the war was financed by borrowing — debts that fed the inflation of 1923.
- **German:** Kriegswirtschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### war guilt clause
<a id="war-guilt-clause"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1919
- **Also written as:** Art. 231
- **Short:** Article 231 of the Versailles Treaty, blaming Germany and its allies for the war.
- **Explanation:** It was the basis for reparations and was widely resented; along with the stab-in-the-back myth it was believed across the non-left spectrum and poisoned Weimar politics.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### war loans
<a id="war-loans"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Dates:** 1914–1918
- **Also written as:** War loans · war bond drives
- **Short:** Money borrowed from citizens through war bonds to pay for the war.
- **Explanation:** Germany ran nine war bond drives and relied on borrowing and printing money rather than taxes. The resulting debts and excess money set up the post-war inflation.
- **German:** Kriegsanleihen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### War of annihilation
<a id="war-of-annihilation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Vernichtungskrieg · war of annihilation
- **Short:** The Nazi war in the East aimed at destroying enemies and civilians, not just armies.
- **Explanation:** Against Poland and the Soviet Union Germany sought to destroy 'Judeo-Bolshevism', starve millions and colonise land. The Wehrmacht participated in its crimes; it was inseparable from the Holocaust.
- **German:** Vernichtungskrieg
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/authoritarianism.md` · `themes/militarism.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### War Raw Materials Department
<a id="war-raw-materials-department"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1914
- **Short:** WWI office set up by Walther Rathenau to control scarce raw materials.
- **Explanation:** It was an early step in organising Germany's war economy, with the state directing industry.
- **German:** Kriegsrohstoffabteilung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wars of Liberation
<a id="wars-of-liberation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1813–1815
- **Short:** The wars of 1813–15 that ended French rule in Germany.
- **Explanation:** Later celebrated as a national uprising; Planert and others show this is largely a later myth. Many Germans fought for Napoleon, and southern states changed sides only in late 1813.
- **German:** Befreiungskriege
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `01_RESEARCH_METHOD.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB
- **Note:** CONTESTED: extent of popular national mobilisation (myth vs older national narrative).

### Warsaw Pact
<a id="warsaw-pact"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1955–1991
- **Short:** Soviet-led military alliance of Eastern European communist states, founded in 1955.
- **Explanation:** The Eastern bloc's answer to NATO. The GDR joined in 1955 and built up its National People's Army within it.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Warsaw Treaty
<a id="warsaw-treaty"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1970
- **Short:** 1970 treaty between West Germany and Poland recognising Poland's western border in practice.
- **Explanation:** Ostpolitik treaty accepting the Oder–Neisse line as Poland's western border and normalising relations.
- **German:** Warschauer Vertrag
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wartburg festival
<a id="wartburg-festival"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1817
- **Short:** 1817 student gathering at the Wartburg castle that burned 'un-German' books.
- **Explanation:** Organised by student fraternities, it was one of the first national-liberal demonstrations after 1815 and contributed to the repression of 1819.
- **German:** Wartburgfest
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md`](../nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### WASG
<a id="wasg"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 2005–2007
- **Short:** Left-wing party formed against the SPD's Agenda 2010 reforms; merged into Die Linke in 2007.
- **Explanation:** Founded mainly by disappointed Social Democrats and trade unionists in western Germany; its merger with the eastern PDS created Die Linke.
- **German:** Wahlalternative Arbeit und soziale Gerechtigkeit
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### We are one people
<a id="we-are-one-people"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1989–1990
- **Short:** Late-1989 slogan demanding German unification.
- **Explanation:** 'Wir sind ein Volk!': a shift from reforming the GDR to uniting with the Federal Republic, which by December 1989 had majority support.
- **German:** Wir sind ein Volk!
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### We are the people!
<a id="we-are-the-people"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Also written as:** We are the people
- **Short:** Slogan of the 1989 East German demonstrations, demanding reform of the GDR.
- **Explanation:** 'Wir sind das Volk!': demonstrators asserted that they, not the party ruling in their name, were the people. By December 1989 it had turned into 'We are one people!'.
- **German:** Wir sind das Volk!
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### weak dictator
<a id="weak-dictator"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Mommsen's phrase for Hitler's chaotic administrative style.
- **Explanation:** It does not mean major policies happened against Hitler's will.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: often misunderstood; refers to administrative style, not lack of power.

### Wehrmacht
<a id="wehrmacht"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1935–1945
- **Short:** Nazi Germany's armed forces, 1935–45.
- **Explanation:** About 17–18 million men served; around 5.3 million died. The army took part extensively in crimes against Jews, POWs and civilians, destroying the 'clean Wehrmacht' myth. Military courts executed about 15,000–20,000 of its own soldiers.
- **German:** Wehrmacht
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Also in:** `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wehrmacht exhibition
<a id="wehrmacht-exhibition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1995–1999, 2001
- **Short:** Touring exhibition (1995–99) documenting army crimes, revised in 2001.
- **Explanation:** Organised by the Hamburg Institute for Social Research, it shattered the 'clean Wehrmacht' myth; photo errors led to a revised version that confirmed the core findings.
- **German:** Wehrmachtsausstellung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Weimar classicism
<a id="weimar-classicism"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Dates:** c. 1786–1805
- **Short:** Goethe's and Schiller's literary movement in Weimar, aiming at humanist education and aesthetic cultivation.
- **Explanation:** It made the small town of Weimar a symbol of the German 'cultural nation' (Kulturnation).
- **German:** Weimarer Klassik
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Weimar coalition
<a id="weimar-coalition"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** The pro-republic alliance of SPD, Centre and liberal DDP.
- **Explanation:** It governed at the start of the republic and was prefigured by the wartime Interparty Committee. It lost its majority early, and later governments rested on shifting coalitions.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Weimar Constitution
<a id="weimar-constitution"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1919
- **Short:** The 1919 constitution of Germany's first democracy.
- **Explanation:** It gave all adults over 20 an equal vote, a government responsible to parliament, extensive rights, and a strong directly elected president with emergency powers (Article 48). The Nazis never formally abolished it; they suspended it by decree.
- **German:** Weimarer Verfassung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md` · `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md` · `twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md` · `twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Weistümer
<a id="weistumer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Village 'wisdoms': written records of local customary law.
- **Explanation:** They limited lords' demands and gave peasants a collective voice.
- **German:** Weistümer
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### welcome money
<a id="welcome-money"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Short:** 100 D-Marks given by West Germany to each East German visitor in 1989.
- **Explanation:** Begrüßungsgeld: a gift paid to East Germans on visiting the West; after the Wall opened millions collected it.
- **German:** Begrüßungsgeld
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### welfare state
<a id="welfare-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** A state that provides social security: pensions, health insurance, unemployment benefits.
- **Explanation:** A state that protects citizens against life's risks through social insurance and benefits. West Germany built a strong welfare state on the older Bismarckian insurance model; the GDR promised social rights as a substitute for freedom.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### well-poisoning
<a id="well-poisoning"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** False accusation that Jews poisoned wells, especially during the Black Death.
- **Explanation:** Used to justify the 1348–50 persecutions.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Welser
<a id="welser"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Augsburg merchant house prominent around 1500.
- **Explanation:** One of the south German merchant houses which, by 1500, tied Augsburg to Italian trade and mining finance.
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Weltpolitik
<a id="weltpolitik"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** from 1897
- **Short:** 'World policy': Germany's drive for global power, colonies and a fleet.
- **Explanation:** Associated with Bülow and Wilhelm II; it fed the naval race with Britain.
- **German:** Weltpolitik
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)

- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wende
<a id="wende"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Dates:** 1989
- **Short:** 'Turn': common German term for the 1989 change, originally Egon Krenz's word.
- **Explanation:** Krenz used it for his own attempt at controlled reform. Many historians and activists prefer 'Peaceful Revolution' to stress that citizens, not the party, made the change.
- **German:** Wende
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/12_reunification.md`](../twentieth_century/12_reunification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Werewolf
<a id="werewolf"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Short:** Nazi plan for guerrilla resistance against the occupiers in 1945.
- **Explanation:** The regime planned 'Werewolf' guerrilla units to fight on behind Allied lines in 1945. In practice such resistance to the occupation was negligible.
- **German:** Werwolf
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md`](../twentieth_century/08_occupation_and_denazification.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### West parcels
<a id="west-parcels"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Short:** Gift parcels sent by West Germans to relatives in the GDR.
- **Explanation:** Packages of coffee, chocolate, clothing and other Western goods that maintained family ties and gave recipients privileged access to scarce goods.
- **German:** Westpakete
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)

### Westbindung
<a id="westbindung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Adenauer's policy of binding West Germany firmly to the West.
- **Explanation:** 'Western integration': joining NATO, European communities and reconciling with France, preferring security and trust in the West over hopes of quick reunification.
- **German:** Westbindung
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md`](../contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Westendhall
<a id="westendhall"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** Moderate left club of the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** A proto-party named after its meeting place, positioned on the moderate left.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Western integration
<a id="western-integration"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 1949–1950s
- **Short:** Adenauer's policy of binding West Germany into Western alliances and European institutions.
- **Explanation:** Westbindung: membership of NATO (1955) and the European Economic Community (1957), given priority over reunification — which is why Adenauer rejected the 1952 Stalin Note.
- **German:** Westbindung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

### Westernisation
<a id="westernisation"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Short:** Adoption of the political values and culture of the Western democracies.
- **Explanation:** Concept used by historian Anselm Doering-Manteuffel for how West Germany took on liberal-democratic political culture after 1945; debated against the narrower idea of 'Americanisation'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)

### What is Enlightenment?
<a id="what-is-enlightenment"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Dates:** 1784
- **Short:** Kant's 1784 essay defining Enlightenment as 'man's emergence from his self-incurred immaturity'.
- **Explanation:** Published in the Berlinische Monatsschrift, it celebrated free public reasoning while accepting obedience in official duties.
- **German:** Was ist Aufklärung?
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/05_enlightenment.md`](../early_modern/05_enlightenment.md)

### White revolutionary
<a id="white-revolutionary"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** white revolutionary
- **Short:** Gall's label for Bismarck: using revolutionary means for conservative ends.
- **Explanation:** 'White' refers to conservative monarchism (as opposed to 'red' revolution).
- **German:** weißer Revolutionär
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/05_unification.md`](../nineteenth_century/05_unification.md)

### White Rose
<a id="white-rose"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1942–1943
- **Also written as:** Weiße Rose
- **Short:** Munich student resistance group whose leading members were executed in 1943.
- **Explanation:** In 1942–43 the White Rose, around Hans and Sophie Scholl, wrote leaflets urging Germans to resist. Its members were executed; the RAF later dropped their last leaflet over Germany.
- **German:** Weiße Rose

### white-collar employees
<a id="white-collar-employees"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** White-collar employees
- **Short:** Salaried office and clerical workers.
- **Explanation:** Contrary to older theories, they voted Nazi less than average.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### whole house
<a id="whole-house"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** Whole house
- **Short:** Early modern ideal of the household as one unit of family, servants and work under the husband.
- **Explanation:** Linked to the Protestant marriage ideal: the husband was head of the house, though widows could run businesses. It gave way to the 19th-century bourgeois ideal of separate spheres.
- **German:** das ganze Haus
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/01_reformation.md`](../early_modern/01_reformation.md)
