### Wilhelmine era
<a id="wilhelmine-era"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1888/1890–1918
- **Also written as:** Wilhelmine
- **Short:** The reign of Kaiser Wilhelm II, especially after Bismarck's dismissal in 1890.
- **Explanation:** Named after Wilhelm II (emperor 1888–1918). After Bismarck's fall the 'New Course' brought an ambitious global policy (Weltpolitik), a naval race with Britain and mass politics inside a semi-authoritarian system, ending with defeat and revolution in 1918.
- **German:** Wilhelminische Ära
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Winter Relief
<a id="winter-relief"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Annual Nazi charity drive collecting money and goods for the needy.
- **Explanation:** Street and doorstep collections for Winter Relief were a constant ritual of participation; refusing to give could attract attention.
- **German:** Winterhilfswerk
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md`](../twentieth_century/05_everyday_life_under_nazism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wirtschaftsbürgertum
<a id="wirtschaftsburgertum"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Short:** The propertied middle class: merchants, bankers and early industrialists.
- **Explanation:** German term for the business middle class, distinguished from the educated middle class (Bildungsbürgertum). Both were the social base of liberalism.
- **German:** Wirtschaftsbürgertum
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/class.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Wirtschaftswunder
<a id="wirtschaftswunder"></a>

- **Kind:** term · era/period
- **Dates:** 1950s–1960s
- **Also written as:** economic miracle
- **Short:** The 'economic miracle': West Germany's rapid post-war boom of the 1950s–60s.
- **Explanation:** Period of very fast growth, full employment and rising consumption. Its causes are debated: most economic historians stress catch-up growth and institutions, with the Marshall Plan playing a limited direct role.
- **German:** Wirtschaftswunder
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: causes of the economic miracle are debated (catch-up view dominant).

### witch persecutions
<a id="witch-persecutions"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Also written as:** Witch persecutions
- **Short:** Trials and executions of alleged witches, peaking in some territories during the Thirty Years' War.
- **Explanation:** They were especially severe in the prince-bishoprics of Würzburg and Bamberg in the 1620s–30s — a sign of social stress and of local authority running amok.
- **German:** Hexenverfolgung
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### women's movement
<a id="womens-movement"></a>

- **Kind:** term · movement/ideology
- **Short:** Organised campaign since the 19th century for women's education, work and political rights.
- **Explanation:** In the 19th century women were excluded from politics and, in Prussia, from political associations until 1908. Suffrage came with the revolution of 1918.
- **German:** Frauenbewegung
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### women's suffrage
<a id="womens-suffrage"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Dates:** 12 November 1918
- **Short:** Women's right to vote, introduced in Germany in November 1918.
- **Explanation:** Women voted from 1919 with high turnout and entered the National Assembly — one of the lasting gains of the revolution.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/democracy.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wonder weapons
<a id="wonder-weapons"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Also written as:** wonder weapons
- **Short:** Late-war Nazi propaganda promise of secret weapons that would turn the war.
- **Explanation:** Late-war Nazi propaganda promised secret weapons, such as the V-1 flying bomb and V-2 rocket, that would turn the war. They could not prevent defeat.
- **German:** Wunderwaffen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md`](../twentieth_century/07_world_war_two.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### work-creation programmes
<a id="work-creation-programmes"></a>

- **Kind:** term · economic term
- **Short:** Public works schemes to create jobs.
- **Explanation:** Begun before 1933, they helped reduce unemployment under the Nazis.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### workers' and peasants' faculties
<a id="workers-and-peasants-faculties"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1949–1963
- **Short:** Special GDR colleges preparing workers' and farmers' children for university.
- **Explanation:** Arbeiter-und-Bauern-Fakultäten (ABF), which enabled young people without traditional school qualifications to study, creating a new loyal elite.
- **German:** Arbeiter-und-Bauern-Fakultäten
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### workers' and peasants' state
<a id="workers-and-peasants-state"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** workers' state
- **Short:** The GDR's official self-description as a state ruled by workers and farmers.
- **Explanation:** Official formula of the GDR constitutions: 'a socialist state of workers and peasants … under the leadership of the working class and its Marxist-Leninist party'. In practice the SED leadership ruled, and the 1953 uprising showed workers turning against it.
- **German:** Arbeiter-und-Bauern-Staat
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### workers' and soldiers' councils
<a id="workers-and-soldiers-councils"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1918–1919
- **Short:** Revolutionary committees formed across Germany in November 1918.
- **Explanation:** They spread from the Kiel mutiny and briefly held local power; most were led by moderate socialists rather than radicals.
- **German:** Arbeiter- und Soldatenräte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md`](../twentieth_century/01_world_war_one.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Working class
<a id="working-class"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** working class
- **Short:** Industrial wage earners; by 1914 the largest social group in Germany.
- **Explanation:** Never homogeneous: skilled and unskilled, Protestant and Catholic, native and Polish. Much of it organised in the SPD and unions.
- **German:** Arbeiterschaft
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md`](../nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### Working towards the Führer
<a id="working-towards-the-fuhrer"></a>

- **Kind:** term · concept
- **Also written as:** working towards the Führer · worked towards the Führer
- **Short:** Kershaw's idea that officials anticipated Hitler's wishes to win his favour.
- **Explanation:** It explains cumulative radicalisation: subordinates pushed policy further than explicit orders.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md`](../twentieth_century/04_nazi_state.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### workplace brigades
<a id="workplace-brigades"></a>

- **Kind:** term · social group
- **Also written as:** brigades
- **Short:** Small work teams in GDR enterprises that were also social and political units.
- **Explanation:** Work collectives (Brigaden) that competed to meet plan targets and organised social life, outings and political education, making the workplace a social centre.
- **German:** Brigaden
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `05_REGIME_MATRIX.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### works councils
<a id="works-councils"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1920
- **Short:** Elected employee bodies in workplaces, introduced by law in 1920.
- **Explanation:** They were among the Weimar Republic's social gains for workers.
- **German:** Betriebsräte
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/continuity_and_change.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/socialism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Württemberger Hof
<a id="wurttemberger-hof"></a>

- **Kind:** term · party/organisation
- **Dates:** 1848–1849
- **Short:** Left-centre club of the Frankfurt National Assembly.
- **Explanation:** One of the Assembly's proto-parties, named after an inn; it stood between the liberal centre and the democratic left.
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md`](../nineteenth_century/04_1848_revolutions.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Wüstungen
<a id="wustungen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Deserted villages abandoned in the late Middle Ages.
- **Explanation:** Many villages were abandoned after the famines and plagues of the 14th century.
- **German:** Wüstungen
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/03_medieval_society.md`](../medieval/03_medieval_society.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### WVHA
<a id="wvha"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** SS Economic and Administrative Main Office, which ran the concentration camps.
- **Explanation:** It managed the camp system and exploited prisoners' labour.
- **German:** Wirtschafts-Verwaltungshauptamt
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

## Y

### Yad Vashem
<a id="yad-vashem"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Israel's national Holocaust memorial and research centre in Jerusalem.
- **Explanation:** It recognises rescuers of Jews as 'Righteous Among the Nations'.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md`](../twentieth_century/06_holocaust_and_persecution.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Young Plan
<a id="young-plan"></a>

- **Kind:** term · treaty/agreement
- **Dates:** 1929
- **Short:** 1929 settlement fixing German reparation payments, which replaced the Dawes Plan.
- **Explanation:** The nationalist campaign against it in 1929 gave Hitler respectability through an alliance with Hugenberg's DNVP. Economist Ritschl argues the foreign debt crisis under the Young Plan was what really constrained Brüning.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Also in:** `twentieth_century/03_rise_of_nazism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Yugoslav wars
<a id="yugoslav-wars"></a>

- **Kind:** term · war/battle
- **Dates:** 1990s
- **Short:** The 1990s wars in which Yugoslavia broke apart, sending refugees to Germany.
- **Explanation:** A series of conflicts in the Balkans during the 1990s. Germany took in many refugees and, in the Kosovo war of 1999, joined its first combat mission.
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

## Z

### Zabern affair
<a id="zabern-affair"></a>

- **Kind:** term · event
- **Dates:** 1913
- **Short:** 1913 scandal when officers illegally detained civilians in Alsace.
- **Explanation:** The Reichstag passed a vote of no confidence in the Chancellor, which had no constitutional consequence.
- **German:** Zabern-Affäre
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md`](../nineteenth_century/06_german_empire.md)
- **Also in:** `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### ZDF
<a id="zdf"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1963–
- **Short:** One of Germany's two national public broadcasters.
- **Explanation:** Second German Television, a public broadcaster funded by fees. Public broadcasting is autonomous from government but contested by populists; the KB cites ZDF reporting on East–West gaps.
- **German:** Zweites Deutsches Fernsehen
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/03_modern_society.md`](../contemporary/03_modern_society.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/01_post_reunification.md` · `contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/class.md` · `themes/women_and_family.md` · `twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zeitenwende
<a id="zeitenwende"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Turning point': Germany's security shift after Russia invaded Ukraine in February 2022.
- **Explanation:** Chancellor Scholz's word for the break of 24 February 2022. It stands for a €100 billion Bundeswehr fund, weapons for Ukraine, ending Russian gas imports and abandoning post-Cold War security assumptions.
- **German:** Zeitenwende
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/01_post_reunification.md`](../contemporary/01_post_reunification.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `contemporary/04_germany_in_europe.md` · `themes/industrialization.md` · `themes/militarism.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zersetzung
<a id="zersetzung"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** 'Decomposition': Stasi tactic of secretly destroying opponents' lives and careers.
- **Explanation:** Stasi method of psychological harassment — spreading rumours, sabotaging careers and relationships — to paralyse critics without open arrests.
- **German:** Zersetzung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/10_east_germany.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zeytregister
<a id="zeytregister"></a>

- **Kind:** term · document
- **Short:** Hans Heberle's diary of the Thirty Years' War, as seen by a Swabian cobbler.
- **Explanation:** It records his flight into Ulm some thirty times.
- **German:** Zeytregister
- **Main chapter:** [`early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md`](../early_modern/02_thirty_years_war.md)
- **Basis:** KB

### Zollverein
<a id="zollverein"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Dates:** 1834–1871
- **Also written as:** Customs Union
- **Short:** Prussian-led customs union of German states (1834) that excluded Austria.
- **Explanation:** By removing internal tariffs it created a common market under Prussian leadership. Historians debate how far it paved the way for Prussian-led unification — many member states fought with Austria against Prussia in 1866.
- **German:** Zollverein
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md`](../nineteenth_century/02_german_confederation.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `02_MASTER_TIMELINE.md` · `nineteenth_century/03_nationalism.md` · `nineteenth_century/05_unification.md` · `nineteenth_century/07_industrial_society.md` · `themes/industrialization.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K
- **Note:** CONTESTED: whether the Zollverein made Prussian-led unification inevitable is mostly rejected.

### Zündapp
<a id="zundapp"></a>

- **Kind:** term · other
- **Dates:** 1917–1984
- **Short:** German motorcycle and moped manufacturer.
- **Explanation:** Nuremberg- and later Munich-based maker of motorcycles and mopeds. A Zündapp moped was given in 1964 to Armando Rodrigues de Sá, welcomed as the millionth guest worker.
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/09_west_germany.md)
- **Also in:** `contemporary/03_modern_society.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md` · `themes/minorities_and_migration.md`
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zunftkämpfe
<a id="zunftkampfe"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** guild struggles
- **Short:** 14th-century 'guild struggles' for a share in town government.
- **Explanation:** In some towns guild masters won council seats.
- **German:** Zunftkämpfe
- **Main chapter:** [`medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md`](../medieval/04_cities_church_feudalism.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zustimmungsgesetze
<a id="zustimmungsgesetze"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Also written as:** consent laws
- **Short:** 'Consent laws' that need the approval of the Bundesrat.
- **Explanation:** Laws affecting the Länder's interests require a majority in the Bundesrat, letting opposition-led states block them. The 2006 federalism reform reduced their share.
- **German:** Zustimmungsgesetze
- **Main chapter:** [`contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md`](../contemporary/02_modern_political_system.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zwangslagen
<a id="zwangslagen"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** German for 'compelling constraints' — situations leaving no real choice.
- **Explanation:** Knut Borchardt's word for the position he believed Brüning faced in the Depression.
- **German:** Zwangslagen
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md`](../twentieth_century/02_weimar_republic.md)
- **Basis:** KB+K

### Zweispitz
<a id="zweispitz"></a>

- **Kind:** term · German word
- **Short:** Two-cornered hat (bicorne), Napoleon's trademark.
- **Explanation:** Napoleon's bicorne was captured by Prussian soldiers after Waterloo and is now in the German Historical Museum.
- **German:** Zweispitz
- **Main chapter:** [`nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md`](../nineteenth_century/01_napoleon.md)
- **Also in:** `00_FINAL_EXPLANATION.md` · `images/IMAGE_INDEX.md`
- **Basis:** KB

### ZZF
<a id="zzf"></a>

- **Kind:** term · institution
- **Short:** Leibniz Centre for Contemporary History in Potsdam.
- **Explanation:** Zentrum für Zeithistorische Forschung, a research institute that, with the Berlin Wall Foundation, documented deaths at the Berlin Wall (2005–2017).
- **German:** Zentrum für Zeithistorische Forschung
- **Main chapter:** [`twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md`](../twentieth_century/11_cold_war_germany.md)
